The subtlety, handled explicitly: advanceDay settles the day that just ENDED, so it captures the serial before incrementing and settles under that number (dailyIncomeTotal(settledSerial)); the Bank displays businessDailyNet() at the CURRENT serial — the day in progress, whose night tonight is exactly what tomorrow's tick will settle under the same serial. Both readings are therefore the same night. Crew percentages stay on the base gross (the bonus is on the house take), and the Bank's club row carries a "Weekend door" note that reads "Live tonight" on Fri/Sat. TAXI PASSENGER PERSONALITIES (game/jobs.ts + Phase7): every fare rolls a type when it hails — Calm 40% (the pre-Phase-23 fare, maths untouched), Hurried 20%, Chatty 20%, Nervous 20%. The type is shown before you commit: the jobTarget marker label reads "Fare waiting — <place> (Hurried)" and the hail toast describes them ("keeps checking the time…"). The boarding bark states the deal in the passenger's own words. ALL tip maths lives in settleFare(type, fare, elapsed, par, dmg) in jobs.ts — the one place a tip is computed; Phase7's completion block calls it and does nothing else with tips. Its rules: the standard speed window (elapsed ≤ 1.25 × par) pays +25% of fare and a gentle ride (≤2 damage) adds $8, both as before; HURRIED replaces the +25% with +40% when you land with >25% of par still in hand (elapsed ≤ 0.75 × par) — one speed bonus per fare, never stacked; damage > 15 still zeroes any tip; NERVOUS zeroes the tip on ANY damage (dmg > 0), overriding everything, and the passenger complains twice — the moment the first knock lands mid-ride (so you learn the tip died when it died) and again at the kerb, with their star rating capped at 2★; CHATTY pays the standard maths and fires 2–3 subtitle barks across the ride from CHATTY_LINES (original harbour small-talk, scheduled from boarding). The completion toast now breaks the fare out ("Fare $X + tip $Y = $Z — n★ ride") and the Sable Cabs phone message carries the breakdown plus settleFare's one-line note. jobStats gains taxiTips (lifetime tips total, shown in the Jobs → Earnings taxi row) — the only new persisted field; personality state itself is session state on J and resets everywhere the fare resets (completion, both abandonment paths, and the NG+ stand-down, which now clears taxiType/barks explicitly — verified in ngplus.ts). Tally unchanged: 86 ✅ / 7 🟡 / 0 ⬜ — no line changed state; the enterable-buildings 🟡, the taxi ✅ and the businesses ✅ lines grew. Phase 12/14 structure preserved: the club systems ride Interiors' existing useFrame (scalar near-checks, 24 material lerps, a transition-gated audio start/stop), the drink vignette rides the 8 Hz HUD poll as a boolean (no new per-frame React state), the taxi beats are scalar checks in Phase7's existing loop, no new eager chunks — initial-load gzip stays in the ~817 kB class (819.4 kB measured).*

*Phase 24 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the fishing ✅ and random-events ✅ lines grow): the round opens with a REGRESSION AUDIT of Phase 23 in the established style. Two defects found and fixed: (1) CLUB CLOSING TIME WAS UNDEFINED — Phase 23 gated ENTRY by the club hours (20:00–04:00) but never defined the other end: a player inside at 04:00 could drink at the bar forever and no system acknowledged the night had ended. The behaviour is now chosen, documented in game/club.ts and enforced in Interiors.tsx, following the pattern Thread & Tide set for its own hours from inside: at 04:00 the doors lock (entry was already gated), the bartender calls last orders ONCE as the hour turns (a bark, watched on the hour transition), the bar stops serving, and nobody is ejected — the floor, the bass and the exit door keep working until the player leaves, and re-entry is gated as usual. No ejection teleport: a pocket interior has no safe forced-exit state worth inventing. (2) settleFare's MESSAGE lied for a slow Hurried fare — the maths was right (no speed bonus), but the note fell through to "A clean harbour fare", so the Sable Cabs breakdown described a calm ride for a passenger who had spent it watching the clock; a Hurried fare outside both speed windows now gets its own note ("too slow for the speed bonus"). Verified clean in the same audit: the BOUNCER (the exterior door branch is the ONLY way in — interior state is session-only and never persisted, so save/load lands you outside; exit drops you at the same door, which re-checks hours and stars; the pendingGo fade can't smuggle a press past the check because hours/wanted are read when the prompt is acted on); the BASS (started/stopped purely on the G.interior transition inside Interiors' frame, with an unmount cleanup; toggleMute drives clubMaster with the other gains; bust/wasted route through respawnPoint, which clears G.interior — so the loop stops — and NG+ started from inside the club leaves the interior untouched, so the bass rightly keeps playing: you never left the room); the WEEKEND DOOR, re-derived for both nights — advanceDay captures the serial BEFORE incrementing and settles the ended day under its own number, while the Bank renders the current serial, the day in progress whose night settles at tomorrow's tick under that same serial: a Friday in progress (serial ≡ 4 mod 7, serial 0 = Monday) shows the +25% and is paid it when Friday settles; Saturday (≡ 5) likewise; a Sunday display (≡ 6) shows no uplift and Sunday's settlement pays none — shown = paid on both weekend nights; settleFare AS THE ONLY TIP PATH (its single call site is Phase7's completion block; taxiEarned/taxiTips are written nowhere else), Hurried REPLACING not stacking (the in-hand branch is if/else against the standard window), the Nervous first-knock complaint firing exactly once (taxiDmgBarked), personality state resetting on completion, both abandonment paths and the NG+ stand-down (the sub-fields — barks/barkAt/dmgBarked — are re-rolled at the next hail before any read), and the Phase 23 courier-restart fix (the Phase7 gate restarts a running chain in place; the Phone button renames itself accordingly). PART B: FISH FROM BOATS (game/phase8.ts + Phase8.tsx + one guard in Vehicle.tsx). The spot model is stated on the code's face: a boat is a MOBILE spot — casting from a still boat builds a one-off FishSpot whose stand point is the hull's position at cast time and whose cast point is the water 7u ahead of the bow (position + facing offset), flagged offshore. The session anchors to the HULL (F.boatCar), not the point: it lives while you're seated in that same boat and ends cleanly when the engine moves you (>1.5 u/s — the line was cast at a fixed point and a moving boat has left it), when you step out, or when a status screen/dialogue takes you — the mini-game discipline every other state follows. The input problem is solved on the tram precedent: E while driving exits the vehicle, so Vehicle's handler yields the interact key (never F) while boatFishClaimsE() holds — mid-cast, or a still boat with the prompt up; F still steps ashore, and stepping ashore ends the session. Any boat counts — owned speedboat, borrowed skiff, traffic-stolen or a marina rental (the rental watch and the fishing session are independent; an expiring pass collects the boat on step-out as always, and the session has already ended by then). The OFFSHORE table is the shore table plus one fish: rollCatch now filters offshore defs OUT of shore pools and weights offshore water rich like island water (exponent 0.6); the SABLE TUNA (95–185 cm, $130 base) is offshore-only and runs day or night — the deep water doesn't keep harbour hours. The creel is the shore creel: one bucket, sold at Harbour Wraps, best-catch records shared. THE FISHING DERBY (game/derby.ts + Phase17.tsx) joins the event rotation as the third kind (challenge 40% / cargo 32% / derby 28%) on the scheduler's existing stand-off contract — it holds G.eventNow for its full 3 minutes, so B2 events, the challenge and the cargo all stand off from it and it from them. Entry is at the pier ring (free, on foot); the contest is a WINDOW, NOT A LEASH (documented in derby.ts): every catch landed inside the window scores its sale value wherever it was caught — pier, island dock or offshore — and leaving the area, or a bust, costs only fishing time. At the whistle settleDerby reads the score once: best score persists in jobStats either way (derbyBest/derbyWins, shown on the Stats fishing row); beating the house pays $300 + a Harbour Wire headline. THE HOUSE SCORE, honestly: Port Sable has no fishing skill stat (skills are driving/shooting/stamina/strength), so the target scales mildly with the game's fishing-experience record — lifetime catches: $180 + $4 per catch, capped at +$140 — the more you've fished, the better the pier regulars expect you to do. NG+ stands the derby down WITHOUT settling (standDownDerby: no prize, no best-score record) and Phase17 frees the slot when it sees the window closed; the same stand-down also reels in any cast in progress (Phase 23's list had missed fishing — F is a live mini-game state like any other). Phase 12/14 structure preserved: boat fishing rides Phase8's existing frame loop and the derby rides Phase17's scheduler — scalar checks only, the derby ring is one static mesh, no new per-frame React state, no new eager chunks — initial-load gzip stays in the ~819 kB class (821.1 kB measured).*

*Phase 25 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the map ✅ line grows): the round opens with the screenshot that started it — a teal sedan parked HALF INSIDE Café Coral's wall at the Docks (the café was added in Phase 13 over a Phase 1 spawn point; neither phase re-checked the other). PART A — COLLISION SANITY. The audit is a permanent tool, not a one-off: tools/audit-placements.mjs bundles tools/audit-entry.ts (which imports the REAL data modules and the REAL isBlocked) with the client's own esbuild and tests, headlessly: every CAR_SPAWNS entry as a heading-rotated OBB against every collider; every traffic lane swept over its real travel span against the building AABBs; every gameplay point (registers, doors, buy points, bus stops, hail/destination sets, staff beats replicated through their own fallback logic, townsfolk stops, fishing/derby/rental points, event spots) through isBlocked; pairwise AABB interpenetration across all 135 colliders; a face invariant (no collider face inside the asphalt band of its own grid); and the scatter arrays (props/trees/palms/lamps/destructibles) against colliders and the traffic envelope. THE FOUND LIST (214 findings in the shipped Phase 24 world): 6 parked cars inside walls (the screenshot car at (3,106) inside the café; a Coral car inside the Goods shopfront; a Northgate car inside the showroom; a taxi inside a Downtown tower; two more inside generated buildings) + 2 beached boats + a Bronto parked across both lanes of the x=−100 road; 4 valet points inside buildings or lanes; traffic lanes clipping the Docks strip (4 shopfronts), the Coral shop row, the gym (its west face crossed the x=60 centreline), the car-wash hall, the gun store and 4 Northgate mansion-row houses; BUILDINGS INTERPENETRATING BUILDINGS — all 9 Downtown towers inside the (0,10) filler building of their block, 3 Coral glass towers inside filler buildings, the Coral waterfront houses overlapping the shop row by ~2.9u, and both suburb house rows inside generated blocks; points unreachable inside colliders (4 registers — the Market Row grocer's counter was inside a building with NO stand point inside interaction radius — a bus stop, the barber + tattoo spots, 6 townsfolk stops, a Northgate ped spawn, the Coral fuel point on a tower corner, the Pier fishing cast landing on sand); and dozens of scatter items inside AABBs (lamps, trees, palms, benches, hydrants), Coral/Northgate corner props standing IN the road (the ±15 inset is 1u from a centreline on the 32-pitch grids), street trees in cross streets, and destructibles parked on centrelines. THE FIXES move placements, never buildings — with one generator-level exception that changes no position, height or colour: every generated/hand-placed building's DIMENSIONS are now clamped so its faces keep ≥4.05u from its grid's centrelines (the traffic envelope is 2.8 lane + 1.15 half-width ≈ 3.95; before the clamp 104 of 107 buildings had a face inside the asphalt band and 40 inside the envelope itself). Where a hand-placed tower and a filler building shared a footprint, the filler spot is suppressed with its seeded rnd rolls still consumed in order, so every other building keeps its exact rolled size/colour. The Coral waterfront was re-laid (houses z 214→206, shops z 218→216.5, doors/registers on the promenade gap at z 211.7), the Docks strip moved 2.6u north with its doors and garage zone, the gym moved east off the x=60 centreline, the car wash became a slim front in the only gap its corner allows, the farm moved off the z=−100 road, and a scatter cleanup pass nudges props/trees/palms/lamps out of every collider along the smallest axis and drops the few that would land in the traffic sweep. The audit now prints ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR; its only notes are the 5 allowlisted parking-garage deck rails (meshes at y≈3.75 that ground traffic passes under — the 2D blockers seal the under-deck bays, shipped Phase 6 behaviour). THE RUNTIME GUARD (game/depenetrate.ts): the core test lives in the C++ core as building_overlap / resolve_overlap (penetration depth + smallest-axis push, resolve_aabb's rule) with the EXACT TS mirror in GamePhysics.ts (own WASM-loader try/catch block, same pattern as Phases 4/6/8/10/14; the guard calls the mirror directly because it runs at module-load/spawn time, before the async engine exists — parity is proven, see Part D of the phase report). It runs on: last-car + fleet restore (applyCarSave), the garage-kept car load, garage take-out, valet spawn, rental spawn, traffic init, and a 1 Hz sweep over settled cars (the driven car exempt; boats exempt — a hull must never be pushed onto land; the mansion is tested as its two visual footprints because the coarse blocker seals the driveway pad by design). PART B — SMOOTHNESS, honestly bounded: the adaptive-DPR governor's old rule stepped down on a SINGLE 1 s window under 47 FPS, so one busy second (a chunk streaming in) dropped the resolution and it crept back over ~5 s — visible pumping. It now needs the rolling rate under 45 FPS sustained 3 s to step down toward 1.0, and over 58 FPS sustained 10 s to step back up to the tier cap; ladder and caps unchanged, nothing new in the UI. At Low, the sun shadow map re-renders every 3rd frame instead of every 2nd (the Phase 12 snapped-transform rule still forces an instant update when the sun or player actually moves, so shadows never lag a turn). The shadow-caster audit found the caster set already bounded — casters live inside the streamed chunks, the shadow camera is a tight ±55u follow box — so no visual change was taken there; streaming radii at Low already ride the Phase 16 view-distance lever (near = 0.65×). NO physics/sim changes. These bound the worst cases; FPS on any given machine is not promised from a build log — the counter on the HUD is the verdict. PART C — EASTMERE, and the honest architecture for "other cities on the server side": a browser game cannot execute world geometry on a server — there is no server-side scene to stream from, and pretending otherwise would be a fake feature. The real equivalent, and what shipped: the district is a SEPARATE BUNDLE CHUNK (components/Eastmere.tsx, 9.4 kB / 2.7 kB gzip) that the web server only sends when the player approaches the causeway — the main download carries none of its geometry. The eager bundle holds only the loader stub (game/eastmere.ts) plus, in world.ts's streamed-district registry, the district's bounds + causeway gate — needed by the boot guard and called out here as the one unavoidable eager constant. Crossing the gate approach (within 70u, polled twice a second in game) dynamic-imports the chunk, which registers its colliders + road lines into the registry (isBlocked/blockersNear/isOnRoad/isWaterAt/districtAt all consult it), mounts the district, and shows "Entering Eastmere — Gull Rock Lookout" on the mission banner. Once loaded it STAYS for the session — colliders are never unregistered (a car or ped standing in Eastmere must not lose its ground truth mid-session). The district: land x 44..122, z −300..−192 east of Northgate across a short causeway on the z=−248 line; 9 buildings + the Eastmere Diner + the Eastmere Light lighthouse (10 colliders), streets z=−248 / x=84 / z=−216 drawn on the minimap + big map (from the registry, only once loaded, with an EASTMERE label on the big map) and named on the HUD district line via districtAt; 4 parked cars; 5 ambient walkers on sidewalk beats incl. the path to GULL ROCK LOOKOUT, the named viewpoint at the south-east corner. TRAFFIC-FREE, by choice and stated plainly: wiring a streamed area into the traffic grid (lane state, density ranks, dormancy/despawn) was the risky option; parked cars + peds is the robust one. SAVES: the player always boots at the pier (positions were never persisted), but CARS are — a last-car/fleet/garage-kept snapshot saved inside Eastmere whose chunk has not loaded yet restores at the causeway gate (34,−248) on the main map instead of in open water (streamedGateFor, applied in applyCarSave and the garage-kept load). PART D — C + PYTHON: the g++ smoke suite passes 48/48 checks including 7 new ones for building_overlap/resolve_overlap (one is the screenshot's café-wall case itself); TS↔C++ parity for the new pair is byte-identical across a 67-case input grid (two boxes, two radii). The Python server has NO test files (pytest collects nothing — verified in a clean venv, not a failure) and both server modules pass py_compile; the server was not otherwise touched this round. Perf sanity: initial-load JS 823.4 kB gzip (index 448.1 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 10.7 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 24's 821.1 — +0.3%, entirely this phase's eager code (the depenetration guard, the registry + loader stub, the governor retune, the map's registry drawing). Eastmere's 2.7 kB gzip chunk is NOT in the initial load; it is fetched only on approach. cc0Data stays lazy.*

*Phase 26 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the map ✅ line grows: streamed districts now count 2): PROOF & WESTBROOK. PART A — PHASE 25 REGRESSION AUDIT. (1) Depenetration: three defects. The 1 Hz settled sweep exempted only the driven car — it could shove a settled car while the player stood at its door inside the 3.6u enter radius (the exact moment a player is most likely to be next to a car), and it knew nothing of job roles: the valet car and the tow target are plain settled G.cars entries, and dispatched traffic units were protected only by accident (they keep parked=false while dispatched). The sweep now skips the valet car and tow target by id, any car within 4.5u of the on-foot player, and dispatched traffic. resolve_overlap itself terminates (one call is a fixed 3-pass loop), but ACROSS sweeps a car wedged between two colliders could be pushed out of A into B and back forever; depenetratePoint now keeps the best-of-passes position and accepts a move only when it strictly reduces total overlap — the sequence is monotone, so oscillation is impossible, and a truly wedged car stays put instead of jittering. Neither the sweep nor traffic-init depenetration checked the destination against other cars (traffic-init could push a car INTO the next lane's car); both now refuse any destination that would overlap a neighbouring car (new destinationBlocked + guardRadius in game/depenetrate.ts; the C++ core is untouched and its smoke suite still passes 48/48). (2) Eastmere: one defect, two clean. DEFECT — if the chunk's dynamic import FAILED (404/offline), App cleared the poll and showed nothing: no retry, no message, the district silently unreachable all session (the world stayed graceful — an unloaded district is water, the causeway ends at the shore, no soft-lock, and a half-registered collider set is impossible because registration is the chunk module's own act). The loader is now a shared factory (game/streamed.ts) with single-flight loads and explicit failure semantics, and App retries on a 5 s backoff with a one-time-per-approach toast ("Couldn't load … — check your connection. The causeway stays closed until it loads."); the banner now also names the district from the registry. CLEAN — registerStreamedDistrict is idempotent, so leaving and returning registers colliders exactly once; streamedGateFor coverage is complete: last-car + fleet via applyCarSave, the garage-kept car via the boot load, and stored cars carry no position at all (kind/colour/mods only — they are taken out AT the garage), so there is nothing further to guard by construction. (3) Adaptive DPR: one defect, one clean. DEFECT — the governor never reset its windows on a manual quality-tier change, so counts earned under the old tier acted on the new one (it could step the fresh tier down within seconds of the user's choice); it now watches the tier and restarts its ladder + windows on any change. CLEAN — the tier cap is airtight: the ladder index is clamped to the cap every window and only incremented below it (caps High 1.5 / Med 1.35 / Low 1.25), so no length of smooth play raises DPR past the tier cap. (4) The placement audit re-run prints ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR for the base world (135 colliders; only the 5 allowlisted deck-rail notes). PART B.1 — THE SERVER TEST SUITE (server/tests/, pytest + FastAPI TestClient, hermetic: persistence redirected to a temp dir before import, no token configured except in the one test that sets it): 32 tests, ALL PASSING — root/health, leaderboard bests + the service-token gate, economy snapshot round-trips; the WS join flow (snapshot contents, player_joined, state relayed to peers and never to the sender, leave broadcast, disconnect cleanup down to an empty room list, duplicate-id rejection, room-full); malformed input (bad JSON, non-object JSON, unknown types, invalid room ids, eleven invalid state shapes incl. a raw NaN literal, events before join / unknown events, event payload clamping, a >64 KB message — the connection and the room survive every one); and the anti-teleport gate. The gate tests caught THREE REAL BUGS, all fixed in server/main.py and each pinned by a regression test: (a) the plausibility anchor advanced BEFORE the speed check, so a rejected teleport moved the anchor and a 1-unit creep from the rejected spot laundered the jump — the anchor now advances only on accepted states (probe: old code accepts the creep, new code rejects it); (b) the anchor survived disconnect, so a player rejoining at a respawn point was measured against their pre-disconnect spot and rejected — join and disconnect now clear it; (c) WORLD_LIMIT=150 on both axes rejected legitimate state from most of the map (Northgate z=−248, Coral Shores z=224..304, Sable Island, both streamed districts) — replaced by per-axis limits (X 210 / Z 340) sized to the real map with margin, with a test spawning a fresh player in each of five real far-flung spots. PART B.2 — WESTBROOK, the second streamed district, sited on the map's west water edge: the mainland ends at x=−126 and the z=20 road already runs to the Ferry Quarter shore, so the causeway leaves on the z=20 line — the mirror of Eastmere's gate road. The registry gains one entry; the loader, poll, banner, big-map drawing (whose label was hardcoded "EASTMERE" — now the district's own name at its own centre) and streamedGateFor were already data-driven or are now generalised (game/streamed.ts createDistrictLoader; game/eastmere.ts is a 3-line binding with identical exports and behaviour; the Eastmere chunk is byte-size-identical at 9.36 kB). The district: land x −194..−132, z −26..66; 8 harbour-industrial buildings (dock offices, a cold store, net lofts, a chandlery, a bunkhouse) + the landmark WESTBROOK GRAIN SILO (three tubes with cone roofs on the west shore) + 2 dock cranes on the east quay; streets x=−164 / z=48 plus the z=20 causeway road; 3 parked cars (a pickup by the dock office, a sedan, a hatch); 5 walkers on sidewalk beats; lamps; the BREAKWATER POINT viewpoint at the south-west shore; a welcome sign at the causeway end; HUD district name via districtAt; big-map label post-load. TRAFFIC-FREE like Eastmere, stated plainly, for the same reason. Its static data lives in game/westbrookData.ts (pure data — imported by the lazy chunk and headlessly by the audit); the audit's new section 6 checks the registry entry, that the bounds are open water pre-load and stay west of the mainland shore, the gate against the base world, and every district collider/car/walker-beat/viewpoint internally — 11 colliders checked, no findings. SAVES: a car saved inside Westbrook restores at its gate (−118, 20) while the chunk is unloaded, via the same streamedGateFor as Eastmere. Perf sanity: initial-load JS 824.4 kB gzip (index 449.1 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 10.7 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 25's 823.4 — +0.1%, the generalised loader + Part A fixes; the Westbrook chunk (10.0 kB, 2.8 kB gzip) is separate and lazy, fetched only on approach. cc0Data stays lazy.*

*Phase 27 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the side-storyline ✅ line grows): THE LEDGER LINE. PART A — PHASE 26 REGRESSION AUDIT. (1) Streamed loader factory: ONE DEFECT, the rest clean. Single-flight verified — load() returns false immediately while an attempt is in flight and the factory's registration is the chunk module's own module-scope act, so a failed import registers nothing and the district stays water (no half-registered state is constructible). The 5 s per-district backoff cannot spin: the App poll only calls load() when the retry timestamp has passed, and each failure pushes it a full 5 s out. DEFECT — the once-per-approach toast re-armed wrongly: the poll reset `warned` whenever needed() went false, but needed() is ALSO false while a load is in flight, so a player standing at a gate through repeated failures got the "couldn't load" toast on every ~5 s retry cycle instead of once per approach. The factory now exposes inRange (pure geometry: within 70 of the gate, or within the bounds + 40 margin — the same shape needed() always used), needed() is defined through it, and the poll re-arms `warned` only when the player has actually left the approach zone; while a load is in flight the poll skips without touching the flag. (2) Depenetration: the role census is COMPLETE and clean — the 1 Hz settled sweep skips stored-away cars, anything moving, the driven car, the valet car and tow target (by job id), dispatched traffic units, and any car within 4.5u of the on-foot player; race rivals and the Bay Circuit ghost are pure render meshes parked underground between races (never G.cars/traffic entries, so never sweep candidates), the Street Challenge show car is a fixed event mesh, the arrest-escort patrol car is a G.cops entry, the Wire courier is a dedicated mesh, boats/planes are exempt inside the guard itself, and the traffic-init pass depenetrates every unit against mutual obstacle lists. The monotone resolve verified: depenetratePoint keeps the best-of-3-passes position and applies only a strict improvement, so the two-collider wedge cannot oscillate — a wedged car stays exactly put. ONE DEFECT — destinationBlocked had no fallback: when the only wall-clearing destination overlapped a neighbouring car, the move was refused outright and retried identically forever; the car was safe from jitter but embedded for good. game/depenetrate.ts now resolves through a ladder (resolveCarPoint, shared by both car shapes): (i) the plain resolve if its destination is car-clear; (ii) a JOINT resolve that relaxes against the building boxes and the obstacle circles together (3 passes), accepted when it clears both; (iii) a documented LAST RESORT — accept the building-only resolution when it fully clears the wall even though it touches a car: two parked cars touching is cosmetic and self-heals the moment either is driven (the vehicle collision step separates them), while a car inside a wall never heals. If none applies, the car stays put (the monotone rule still governs). (3) Server: the anchor discipline verified under the REAL WebSocket flow, not just the unit function — handle_message validates shape, then state_plausible runs BEFORE the state is stored or broadcast, and it writes LAST_STATE only on its accept paths (first-state anchor, and after the speed check passes); the out-of-world rejection happens before any anchor write; join_room and remove_client both clear the anchor. ONE DEFECT — the Phase 26 per-axis limits (X 210 / Z 340) were sized to LAND and clip the water at two shores: charted land spans x −196..+126 and z −300..+324 (world.ts), but boats are water-only with no client-side position clamp, and a boat rounding Westbrook's west point legitimately rides x ≈ −205..−215 while one hugging Coral's south shore rides z ≈ 339+. The bounds are now PER-SIDE — WORLD_X_MIN/MAX −244/174, WORLD_Z_MIN/MAX −348/372: every land extent plus a uniform 48u of open sea — and the two offshore spots join the existing far-district acceptance test (the suite stays at 32 tests, all passing; the 5000-unit rejection probe still rejects). (4) Westbrook audit section re-verified end to end: the audit checks every district collider against the base world and pairwise, parked cars (OBB vs blockers, bounds, pairwise), and walker beats — endpoints AND walked segments — against blockers; the gate restore point (−118, 20) sits on mainland land (the z=20 shore road), so a Westbrook-saved car restores onto a real road while the chunk is unloaded. The full audit still prints ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR. PART B — THE LEDGER LINE (side key "ledger", per slot, post-Ch.3, tracked in the Phone Story app beside the Wire, NG+ resets it with the other arcs). The giver is PETRA VANE, a new named NPC: she stands at her diner hatch in Eastmere (inside the lazy chunk — a grey-ponytail rig with a name label on the staff-presence convention) and joins the Phone contacts; Ines Marlo is the arc's other pole. ledger1 "Cold Storage" ($500): a "tail" mission with a new def flag, holdStart — the van spawns at the Westbrook silo quay DISARMED (no driving, no band, no fail timers; the marker + a "get in position" hold label only) and pulls out the first time the player closes within 55 m, because the mission is accepted by phone in Port Sable while its beat lives a district away; the tail barks now speak in def.giver's name (the Wire's text is unchanged — Ines IS its giver). Its route was walked leg by leg against the tail engine's axis-aligned pathing: (−178,48)→(−164,20) rides Westbrook's z=48 street then the x=−164 street; (−164,20)→(−100,20) is a straight run along z=20 through the gate onto a mainland road line; (−100,20)→(−100,60) climbs a mainland road column to the Ferry Quarter warehouse (story Ch.2's own delivery door). 4 stops, the standard 12–45 m band, 600 s clock (the travel-in is the margin). ledger2 "The Diner Safe" ($650): the photo kind grows two def fields — photoSteps (how many post-meet steps are photo steps; absent = all, the Wire's shape, byte-equivalent behaviour) and runTimer. The diner book is photographed at the diner front under the Phase 18 inside-ring rule (arm in the ring, capture in the ring, leaving disarms); banking it replaces the mission clock with the rival crew's 150 s countdown and the remaining step is a plain reach step to the Northgate Loft door — expiry fails through the standard timer path, and checkpoint retry refreshes the full clock per the standing retry contract (documented, not special-cased). ledger3 "Burn or Publish" ($700 / $900): the finale is a choice mission in the Meridian Pair pattern but with its OWN persisted pair — M.ledgerChoice ("ledger-burn"/"ledger-publish") and M.ledgerDone — because After the Fall already spends the shared M.packChoice on a burn/publish decision with the same flag words; reusing it would have made either arc read the other's answer (and after2's choice UI would have been skipped entirely for a player who had decided there). altActive routes choiceKey:"ledger" defs to the new flag; the branch target lists ride the new targetsAlt/activeTargets pair (same length by construction: warehouse pickup, then Petra's diner front to burn, or the Market Row newsroom to publish). Consequences fire exactly once inside the completion dialogue's !isReplay guard: burn → Eastmere + Westbrook zoneRep +45 (clamped, persisted) + a quiet Wire headline + a cold message from Ines; publish → Market Row zoneRep +45 (the newsroom's district — the pair's informant branch likewise moved its own ground, The Docks) + a Wire special-edition headline + a message from Det. Mara Voss. Parts 1 and 2 also land fallout messages (Petra after the tail; Ines, pointedly, after the photograph). THE DISTRICT GATE: starting ledger1/ledger2 from Petra's contact first runs ensureDistrictLoaded (game/streamed.ts) — already-loaded returns at once; an in-flight attempt (usually the App poll's) is waited out (100 ms polls, ~10 s cap) rather than mistaken for a failure; then one attempt of its own. On false, the mission refuses with Petra's subtitled bark ("the causeway's closed…") and nothing starts — no mission can begin into an unloaded district. Mission starts (including Missions-app replays) and choosing "burn" also warm the relevant chunk opportunistically; an R-retry needs no gate because a loaded district stays loaded all session and M.lastFailed is memory-only. All three payouts flow through effectivePayout, so NG+ scaling applies; every beat is subtitle-dialogued, both finale branches in full. Perf sanity: initial-load JS 828.5 kB gzip (index 453.2 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 10.7 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 26's 824.4 — +0.5%, the arc's defs/engine/App wiring in the main bundle; the Eastmere chunk grows to 10.3 kB (3.0 kB gzip) because Petra lives in it, and stays lazy; the Westbrook chunk is byte-identical (9.97 kB / 2.8 kB gzip). cc0Data stays lazy. Placement audit: ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR. Server suite: 32/32. No C++ touched this round.*

*Phase 29 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the §15.6 police & wanted ✅ lines grow): POLICE & CRIME DEPTH. PART A — PHASE 28 REGRESSION AUDIT. No defects found; the census, in full. (1) KIND-KEYED TABLES, both new kinds present wherever a kind is keyed: CAR_MODELS (world.ts: tempest 232/84, mule 118/46), DEALER_CARS (both, priced $26,000/$3,200, inside the typed kind union), CAR_SPAWNS (id 33 display Tempest, id 34 farm Mule), CarBody (CABIN_POS + SHELL entries and dedicated body cases for both, so dirt/dents/CarFx apply), Vehicle BODY_DIMS ([1.06, 2.18] / [1.1, 2.1]), DRIFT_FACTOR (tempest 1.3 joint-top, mule 0.85), depenetrate HALF_W (1.06 / 1.1 — completed for the Phase 8/18 kinds in Phase 28 itself), the surface-grip branches (mule dirt ×1.38 beside bronto's ×1.25; tempest's post-step push 26.5 in the wedge/hyper idiom; both road caps present), and the kind unions in store.ts/world.ts/CarBody. Absent exactly where Phase 28 said they would be: TRAFFIC_KINDS carries neither (grid-bound traffic has no district gating, so no Downs-only Mule is claimed; the Tempest is never traffic), and VALET_CAR_KINDS is a fixed guest-car roster, not a kind table. MODS: clampMods is kind-agnostic (levels only) and ModMenu resolves the stored entry by kind+colour — both cars take the full ladder. (2) PERSISTENCE ROUND-TRIP BY KIND STRING: last-car (noteLastCar stores kind as a string; spawn-id matching requires CAR_SPAWNS[id].kind === car.kind, true for ids 33/34), fleet (same snapshot path, upsertFleet keyed by spawn id), garage-stored (StoredCar.kind is a plain string; take-out matches kind+colour; names resolve through CAR_MODELS). No kind is ever parsed, switched on or defaulted in a save path. (3) BOAT CLAMP vs FISHING/DERBY: the clamp rewrites next.x/next.z only, inside the boat branch, before the water check; boatFishCar() anchors on |state.speed| < 0.5 and the session follows the hull — a clamped hull is still a still hull, so a fishing session at the chart edge holds; the derby reads no boat state at all. The once-toast flag (chartToasted) is a component ref that is never reset — exactly the documented once-per-session semantics; it cannot re-fire within a session and cannot suppress a first fire. (4) STALL CHIP HYSTERESIS: set only while airborne (alt > 0.5) below 13 u/s; cleared above 15.5, at alt ≤ 0.5, and by the not-driving branch that also zeroes the speedo — landing, recovering and stepping out all clear it; no path sets it on the ground, so it cannot stick. (5) The placement audit re-run on this tree prints ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (spawns 33/34 audited like every other). PART B.1 — EVIDENCE & HEAT (new game/heat.ts; state persisted per slot as harborfall-heat under its own load guard — evidence, fixerDay, headlineStage only; the time-based fields ride the session clock G.timeS and are deliberately not persisted). Gain is hooked into store.applyCrime — the single choke point where a crime becomes known: instantly-known kinds (ped/shoot/robbery) land at once, quiet crimes land when processPendingCrimes applies the witness report, and crimes swallowed by applyCrime's own 4 s cooldown leave nothing (the cooldown already said the patrol couldn't process them). Severity: ped 20, shoot 15, robbery 15, assault 10, steal 8, theft 6, hit 5, reckless 4; cap 100. FLOOR: applyEvidenceFloor runs immediately after the crime's own star maths in applyCrime — wanted = max(wanted, min(3, ⌊evidence/25⌋)), inside the standing 6★ cap and upstream of the star sfx, so a floor-raised response sounds like what it is; a throttled Dispatch bark ("that report matches the harbour file") names the mechanic the first time it bites in a minute. SEARCH: GameSystems' star-drop threshold is now searchSeconds() = 11 + evidence × 0.08 (19 s at a full file), composed with the existing disguise-lite 1.6× cooldown rate exactly as the flat 11 s was. DECAY: a 1 Hz tick in GameSystems calls tickHeat(dt, playerSeen) — decay runs only while unseen (at 0 stars trivially true) and clean (30 s since the last evidence-adding crime), 1 pt per 4 s, with a fractional carry so the rate is exact; FRESH LOOK multiplies the rate by 4 for 120 s from noteFreshLook(), called by the Loft wardrobe, Thread & Tide's buy-and-wear, and Sable Cuts (cut AND trim), each appending its suffix to the toast only while a file is open. BUSTED/WASTED: halveEvidence() inside resetAfterArrest/resetAfterWasted (⌊evidence/2⌋). The two $100 surrender paths (hold-B, HQ desk) do NOT halve — they never reach those functions, and the rule is deliberate: a fine is not time served; §15.6 and the Stats note both say so. HEADLINES: crossing 50 fires "HARBOUR GHOST STILL AT LARGE", crossing 90 "THE FILE WITH NO FACE" — once per climb (headlineStage persists); the stage re-arms when the file thins below 25, so a second climb is news again. HUD: the snapshot gains heat at the standing 8 Hz poll; the chip renders beside the stars only when evidence > 0, teal on glass in the chip family, with the full rules in its title; the phone Stats app gains the evidence row (count, live floor, live Fixer quote) and a plain-text rules note. PART B.2 — THE FIXER. Marek Doss joins CONTACTS (his pre-Ch.2 line/hint fall through the generic contact dialogue and say he doesn't take Kade's calls yet — the gate is honest in-fiction too). The App contact handler intercepts him after Ch.2 (storyIndex ≥ 2, the Lina-arc convention) and runs the refusal chain in blocker order: active mission ("Not mid-job, Kade.") → arrest escort in progress → cooldown (ECO.daySerial ≤ fixerDay) → nothing to fix (evidence 0 AND wanted 0) → LINE OF SIGHT (wanted > 0 AND G.copSeen — a new runtime mirror GameSystems writes beside playerSeen every step, heli-at-4★ included, since the phone handler cannot reach the component ref) → insufficient funds (the quote is named in the refusal). Every refusal is dialogue-only: no charge, no cooldown consumed, no state touched. On payment, applyBribe (heat.ts) deducts max($250, $150 + $6×evidence + $200×wanted), zeroes evidence, drops wanted/starHeat/cooldown/bustTimer, clears pending witness reports + the reporting marker, deactivates every cop, stamps fixerDay = ECO.daySerial, and persists; App adds the phone receipt message, the cash sfx and a three-line subtitle exchange. NG+: resetHeat() runs with the wanted stand-down (file wiped, cooldown forgotten — the Phone NG+ note now says the file is wiped). PART B.3 — DISGUISE-LITE HONESTY CHECK. Verified by reading every consumer: police LOS (GameSystems losClear + sight radius) keys on cop/player positions, building occlusion, weather and crouch/cover — never appearance; witness memory (Ped.memWitnessUntil, set in addCrime) keys on the witnessed event and drives reporting + the Phase 8 recognition barks; zoneRep recognition keys on district reputation; the Phase 6 disguise-lite keys on car id + district while unseen. No field anywhere stores what the player looks like for a recognition decision (CLOTH/LOOK overrides are render-only inputs to the rig). Conclusion, implemented as specified and NOT extended: changing outfit/hair grants the fresh-look decay window and nothing else; §15.6's disguise line and the Stats note both state that recognition is not appearance-based, so no player is promised a reset that doesn't exist. GUARD NON-REGRESSION: the wanted cap stays 6 (floor ≤ 3 by construction); patrol scaling (NG+ multipliers), LOS/last-known search, witness delay (0.8/1.5/2.5 s), the escort scene, depenetration, the streamed loader, the Ledger arc and the NG+ scaling are untouched — the only police-loop edits are the copSeen mirror write, the searchSeconds threshold and the 1 Hz tick call. Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈832 kB gzip (index 456.9 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 10.8 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 28's 829.9 — +0.3%, the heat module + handler + chip; Eastmere (3.0 kB gzip) and Westbrook (2.8 kB gzip) chunks unchanged and lazy. Placement audit: ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR. Server untouched this round; the suite re-runs 32/32. No C++ touched.*

*Phase 28 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the vehicle ✅ lines grow): VEHICLES. PART A — PHASE 27 REGRESSION AUDIT. (1) Ledger arc state machine. CLEAN: holdStart arms exactly once — `armed` is set only inside the `!cv.armed` branch, which is unreachable once true; a checkpoint retry (checkpointStep > 0) spawns armed by design, a fresh start disarmed. CLEAN: the photoSteps/runTimer swap fires exactly once — the swap sits in the photo-bank branch behind `M.step >= meetSteps + photoTotal`, and banking advances the step past the last photo step, so the branch cannot re-enter (checkpoint retry refreshing the full clock instead of the run timer is the standing documented retry contract, not a second swap). CLEAN: ledgerChoice/ledgerDone exactly-once — resolveChoice persists the choice the moment it is made (persistMission), so a save/reload mid-finale re-offers ledger3 with altActive routing to the locked branch and ledgerDone still false; the consequences fire inside completeMission's dialogue callback under the !isReplay guard and set ledgerDone there, persisted by the same branch. CLEAN: NG+ resets the arc AND its keys (ngplus.ts clears ledgerChoice/ledgerDone with M.side.ledger and persists). CLEAN: the tail barks were generalised to def.giver in Phase 27 — every bark/popup in the tail and photo branches reads the speaker from the def, and the Wire's giver IS "Ines Marlo", so its rendered text is unchanged; the one hardcoded popup ("Now the evidence. Three spots…") fires only on meet steps, which only wire1 has. DEFECT — the tail courier could outlive its mission: it was retired by failMission, by the tail fail branches and by completion, but NG+ ends the active mission via endMission() from game/ngplus.ts, which cannot reach the GameSystems ref — the van would render at the quay for the rest of the session, and worse, a later tail mission whose key (`def.id|checkpointStep`) matched the stale entry would REUSE its armed, mid-route state instead of spawning fresh. GameSystems now self-heals every frame: no active tail mission in play phase ⇒ courier retired (intro/outro phases are safe — the courier exists only inside play, and completion already nulls it). DEFECT (same family) — photoArm survived mission end: failMission never reset it, so a photo mission that died with the player standing in the ring (timer expiry) left an arm for that step; an R-retry to the same step then banked on the first capture taken ANYWHERE, its timestamp merely postdating the stale arm — the Phase 18 farming hole reopened across attempts. Completion had the same hole (the final bank calls completeMission directly, leaving the arm standing for the next photo mission's same-index step). photoArm now resets in both failMission and completeMission. (2) Streamed loader: DEFECT — createDistrictLoader.load() cleared `state.loading` only on FAILURE; on success the flag stayed true forever. Consequences: ensureDistrictLoaded's wait loop polls `loading`, so a Petra-gated start whose chunk was already in flight under the App poll waited the FULL ~10 s cap even when the chunk landed in 300 ms — the 10 s wait leaked into gameplay input exactly as the audit brief feared; and the App poll's single-flight skip read a stale flag. load() now clears the flag on both paths. CLEAN otherwise: single-flight holds (load() returns false in flight; startMission's fire-and-forget warm and the Petra gate share the one loader, so no double-load is constructible). (3) Depenetration ladder: CLEAN — resolveJoint is a fixed 3-pass loop (terminates by construction); the last resort requires depenetratePoint to have found a STRICT improvement from an actually-overlapping start (a merely-touching car has start overlap 0 ⇒ moved 0 ⇒ resolveCarPoint returns null before any fallback) AND the building-only destination to fully clear the wall (totalOverlap ≤ 0). One gap found and fixed in passing: HALF_W had no entries for the Phase 8/18 kinds (hyper/gt/tow/kestrel/bronto fell back to a 1.0 guard circle, up to 0.12u under their real half-widths) — the table now carries them, plus the two Phase 28 kinds. (4) Server bounds vs the client's real envelopes, re-measured class by class: cars clamp in Vehicle.tsx to |x| ≤ 130, z −302..330 — inside. The plane clamps to |x| ≤ 135, z −305..335 — inside (the audit brief's worry does not materialise: no legal flight is rejected). On foot, the swim limiter (Player.tsx canStand) allows |x| < 175, z −320..365 — DEFECT: x up to 174.99 pokes past WORLD_X_MAX=174, so a swimmer at the client's own legal east limit was rejected; WORLD_X_MAX is now 178 and the far-positions test gains the swim-limit spot (174.5, 120) inside the existing test (suite stays 32 tests, all passing). DEFECT — boats had NO client clamp at all: isWaterAt is true everywhere off-land, so a hull could sail unboundedly into open water and produce positions the server must drop as off-chart (multiplayer state silently unrelayed; no crash, but an envelope the two sides disagreed on). The boat branch now clamps to the chart (x −240..170, z −344..368 — inside the server bounds with margin) with a once-per-session toast; the plane/car clamps were already inside, so every position the client can now produce is one the server accepts. PART B.1 — TWO NEW CARS. Integration is by kind string through the existing machinery, verified point by point: CAR_MODELS (display stats), DEALER_CARS (Phone dealer list + showroom cycle + forecourt browse all read the one table), CAR_SPAWNS (fleet build in store.ts spreads the spawns; both new spawns pass the placement audit — Tempest id 33 on the Sable Motors display row beside the wedge/GT display cars, Mule id 34 on the grass south of the farmhouse), CarBody (new shells in the construction kit + CABIN_POS + SHELL, so dirt/dents/CarFx all work), Vehicle BODY_DIMS, drift DRIFT_FACTOR, mods via clampMods (kind-agnostic — tune/brakes/drift-tyres all fit both), garage/fleet/last-car/stored persistence (kind stored as a string everywhere; applyCarSave + the stored-car loader resolve names through CAR_MODELS), and race eligibility (street races and the Sable Cup take whatever car the player is driving — there is no kind whitelist to extend; the Bay Circuit and Sparrow Run are mode-locked to boat/plane as before). VANTARA TEMPEST ($26,000, teal-navy #274b5e): the flagship GT brief — display topSpeed 232 / handling 84 against the Kestrel RS's 216/86 (a step up in speed, a hair less twitchy), priced above the Kestrel's $18,000 and the Marlowe GT's $12,500, level with the wedge's $25,000 and below the hyper's $40,000; in the step it joins the wedge/hyper post-step push idiom at cap 26.5 u/s with a 7.2 push (the Kestrel lives at the shared 24 clamp on handling; the Tempest buys its extra top end the exotics' way), and its drift factor is 1.3, joint-top with the Kestrel — rear-drive and drift-friendly by the scoring model's own table. NOT traffic-spawned: dealer + the one display car only. SABLE MULE ($3,200, dust-tan #7a6a4f): the farm brief — display 118/46; on the surface path its dirt grip scales ×1.38 (base 0.72 → ~0.99 before weather, capped at 1) against the Bronto's ×1.25 (~0.9), making it honestly the best dirt tool of the pickups; on tarmac it is capped at 11.5 u/s like the work truck it is (the Bronto caps at 14). TRAFFIC, answered honestly: the traffic system is grid-bound — units run fixed lanes on the three city grids with no district gating (the Phase 18 Bronto is a re-kinned grid unit whose route merely passes the farm corner, and its comment says so) — so a Sable-Downs-only traffic Mule cannot be shipped truthfully; the Mule is dealer + farm-parked, and this note is the record of that call. PART B.2 — BOAT HANDLING PASS. All in the TS boat step + the mesh sync (the C++ boat_buoyancy pose is consumed, not modified): steering authority now scales with (1 − 0.45 × speedFraction) on top of the existing low-speed ramp, so docking keeps full helm and a fast run stops twitching; the bow rise is an eased presentation pitch (target = speedFraction × 0.055 rad, rising at ~2/s and settling slower) layered over the buoyancy pitch in the mesh sync; wake puffs carry the laying speed's fraction — spawn cadence 0.13 s → 0.045 s and puff spread/opacity scale with it; storm drift applies G.weather (Storm ×1, rainAmt ≥ 0.55 ×0.45) × (0.5 + wind × 0.9) × 1.15 u/s toward +x/+z (the onshore south-westerly the harbour's palms already lean in) BEFORE the water check, so drift can push a boat around but never beach a still one (a zero-speed drift into land reverts position like any other non-water step). FISHING/DERBY NON-REGRESSION, verified in code: boatFishCar() anchors on |state.speed| < 0.5 and the session follows the hull (F.boatCar) — drift changes position, never state.speed, so a storm cannot fake an engine start or end a session; the derby scores catches in a window and reads no boat state. PART B.3 — PLANE HANDLING PASS. Verified already shipped in Phase 9 and left alone: banked turns (targetRoll = −steering × 0.55 while airborne, damped at 4/s) and the stall SINK (vy −= 4.5 below 13 u/s). New, all presentation/warning — no flight number moved (lift 16.5, sink threshold 13, ceiling 60, top 36, drag 0.1/0.32, turn 0.5): the stall now WARNS — G.stallWarn drives a pulsing HUD chip (App snapshot at 8 Hz) with hysteresis (sets below 13 u/s above 0.5u altitude, clears above 15.5 or on the ground, and clears when the player leaves the plane) — and the nose DROPS in the stall (a +0.2 rad droop folded into the pitch target, clamped to 0.42). Touchdown compresses the gear: the flight branch records a squash (arrival |vy| / 9, clamped 0.25..1) on any ground contact, and the mesh sync sinks the airframe by squash × 0.13 while it decays at ~2.2/s; the hard-landing damage rule (vy < −7.5 from above 0.5u) is byte-identical. The prop gains a blur disc (PlaneBody: a translucent disc at the prop plane, opacity = clamp((speed − 2)/30) × 0.34 — parked display copies have no carIndex and keep opacity 0). SPARROW RUN / FLIGHT CONTENT RE-CHECKED: the only timed flight content is the Sparrow Run (jobs.ts, par 120 s, checkpoints with cpAlt bands) — its completability depends on speed/accel/lift, all unchanged, so the par stands exactly as reachable as in Phase 27; no story mission flies. Perf sanity: initial-load JS 829.9 kB gzip (index 454.5 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 10.8 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 27's 828.5 — +0.2%, the two car shells + handling branches + the stall chip; Eastmere (10.3 kB / 3.0 gzip) and Westbrook (10.0 kB / 2.8 gzip) chunks unchanged and lazy. Placement audit: ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (both new spawns audited). Server suite: 32/32. No C++ touched this round.*

*Phase 30 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the §15.9 economy ✅ lines grow): PROPERTY INCOME. PART A — PHASE 29 REGRESSION AUDIT. Two defects found and fixed; the rest of the census clean. (1) DEFECT — BUSTED/WASTED WAS NOT EXACTLY-ONCE. Every bust path (instant bust, escort completion, wasted) funnels into App's respawn(), which calls resetAfterArrest/resetAfterWasted — each of which halves the evidence file and charges its fine. But respawn() itself was unguarded: it branches on G.status and clears it at the end, and its button renders from hud.status — the 8 Hz HUD snapshot — so after the first click the button stays live for up to ~125 ms with G.status already "". A second click in that window (or a click landing beside an R press, whose handler IS status-guarded) re-entered respawn, took the else branch, and ran resetAfterWasted on a player who had just been arrested: a second $100 fine, a second halveEvidence (the file quartered, not halved), a hospital respawn instead of the patrol one. Fix: respawn() returns immediately when G.status is empty — with no live status there is nothing to respawn from, and every caller (button, R key) is now exactly-once regardless of click speed. The escort path inherits the fix: finishEscort only SETS status "busted"; the single respawn path still owns the halving, once. (2) DEFECT (narrow) — EVIDENCE COULD DECAY DURING THE ARREST ESCORT. tickHeat's `seen` input is GameSystems' LOS result, computed from the patrol CARS' positions. During the escort the officer walks the player to the car from up to 34u away; the sight radius is 58 × (1 − fog×0.45) × (1 − rain×0.15) — as low as ~27u in the worst weather — so in heavy fog the car could fail to "see" a player being marched by its own officer, and with the 30 s clean window already served during a long chase, the file could thin mid-arrest. The docs define decay as clean + unseen; a player in custody is neither in spirit nor in fact. Fix: the tick call now passes `playerSeen || ESCORT.active` — custody counts as seen, unconditionally. CLEAN, verified in code: (3) CHOKE-POINT CENSUS — every addCrime call site (Vehicle steal/hit, GameSystems ped/hit, Combat assault/hit/shoot, Interactions theft/robbery, theft.ts, Phase7 reckless, Phase9 theft/hit/assault) reaches applyCrime and therefore addEvidence; applyCrime's 4 s cooldown swallowing a crime swallows its evidence too, as documented. The ONLY wanted-raising code outside applyCrime is missionState's forceWanted (startPlay/startPlayAt): a scripted director beat — the mission orders the response, no crime is witnessed or reported — so it leaves no evidence BY DESIGN, and it also doesn't consult the evidence floor (the floor is defined on the next REPORTED crime, which is what applyCrime is). heat.ts's own floor write is the third and last G.wanted assignment outside reads. (4) FLOOR INSIDE THE CAP — applyEvidenceFloor sets wanted = min(6, floor) with floor ≤ 3 by construction (min(3, ⌊evidence/25⌋)); it can never breach or even reach the 6★ cap, and it runs after the crime's own min(6, …) maths inside applyCrime. (5) HALVE EXACTLY ONCE — post-fix: resetAfterArrest and resetAfterWasted each call halveEvidence once and are reachable only from the now-guarded respawn; the wasted-mid-escort path cancels the escort first (cancelEscort never touches evidence) and lands in the same single respawn; the two $100 surrender paths (Player hold-B, HQ desk in Interiors) zero wanted directly and never call either reset — the fine-is-not-time-served rule holds in code, not just in the Stats text. (6) FIXER COOLDOWN — HEAT.fixerDay persists inside harborfall-heat, which the Phase 14 slot layer prefixes per slot automatically; the App handler compares ECO.daySerial ≤ fixerDay, both restored from their own keys on boot, so the cooldown survives reload on the right slot; NG+ calls resetHeat() with the wanted stand-down (verified in ngplus.ts) and the Phone NG+ note discloses the wipe. (7) copSeen FRESHNESS — GameSystems writes G.copSeen EVERY frame: the computed LOS result when wanted > 0 (heli-at-4★ folded in), and false when wanted == 0. There is no frame in which an old true survives a wanted drop; the only theoretical staleness is a stopped render loop (hidden tab), where the phone can't be clicked either. The Fixer can never be blocked forever by a ghost. (8) DECAY DURING MISSIONS — no carve-out exists and none is needed: the documented rule is clean (30 s) + unseen, full stop; a mission that keeps the player hidden and crime-free for 30 s earns the decay exactly as free-roam hiding does. PART B — RENTAL PROPERTIES (all in game/economy.ts beside the businesses they share a tick with; state as RENTALS under its own key `harborfall-rentals` with its own load guard — owned ids validated against the defs, due amounts validated positive). THE UNITS: coral-flat (Coral Shores Rental Flat, $18,000 → $140/day, door (65, 242.6) on the mid-rise row's south face), north-townhouse (Northgate Townhouse, $32,000 → $260/day, door (−59, −236.7)), docks-warehouse (Docks Warehouse Unit, $45,000 → $390/day, door (60, 103.2)) — payback 129/123/115 days at headline rent, deliberately the slowest money in the game (businesses repay in 27–39): property is the endgame sink, and the prices sit on the existing property ladder (apartment $5,000, loft $9,000) extended honestly. THE WAREHOUSE IS REAL: a new STATIC_BLOCKER at (60, 109.1) in the Docks strip's own geometry (hw 5, hd 4.1, matching the clinic/café slabs) and a corrugated Unit 7 visual in World.tsx's Phase7Frontages (roller door, personnel door, Sable Lets board); the two generated-building doors get Sable Lets A-boards in the world so they can be found. All three door points are point-tested by tools/audit-placements.mjs (which also pairwise/lane-checks the new blocker) — ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR, 136 colliders. THE GATE: the warehouse requires owning ANY business — checked in buyRentalProperty, stated in the door prompt when unmet, stated in the Bank blurb, stated by Ana on the phone. THE TICK: settleRentDaily() runs inside advanceDay's day-serial guard, after business income and before the loan settlement (income before obligations, the standing order) — rent can never double-pay, and sleeping pays it like everything else. SINGLE SOURCE: rentFor(p) (0 while a due bill stands) and rentDailyNet() feed BOTH the tick's expectation and the Bank's per-unit and total lines; the tick's actual payout differs from the figure only by the day's tenant-event rolls, which is the documented game of it (the Bank line is labelled with the same "paid each morning" contract as the business line, whose club-weekend term has the same property). TENANT EVENTS (one roll per let, unpaused unit per settled day): repair 10% (bill $60–$180: cash ≥ bill → auto-deducted, rent lands in full, Ana messages the plumber's invoice; cash < bill → the bill becomes DUE, the unit pays nothing and rolls nothing until settled — the pause is total, so a due bill can't compound), late 12% (half rent, Ana's apology), good tenant 10% (full rent + 2 zoneRep in the unit's districtAt district, persisted), quiet 68% (full rent, no message — Ana writes only when something happens, plus the aggregate SableBank rent line whenever rent lands). Cash is never taken below $0 anywhere in the system: the repair auto-pay requires cash ≥ bill, payDueBill requires cash ≥ due, and the DUE path takes nothing. SELLING: sellRentalProperty pays round(80% × price) minus any due bill on that unit (floored at 0), clears the unit and its bill, and rent stops the same day because the tick reads RENTALS.owned live; sold from the door (hold-E — the Phase 30 verb generalisation of Phase7's hold-to-confirm: "Paying…"/"Selling…" join "Buying…", and the stale-hold clears in Interactions/Phase8 learned the new verbs too) or from the Bank behind the NG+-style two-step arm/confirm. ANA RUIZ joins CONTACTS as the Sable Lets property manager; her messages carry every event, and calling her (App handler) reads the portfolio live — holdings, per-unit state, due bills, the daily total — or makes the three-unit pitch when nothing is owned. NG+: startNewGamePlus never touches RENTALS (verified — ECO and the rentals key are absent from its reset list, beside businesses/homes/loans in its KEEPS header, now documented there): the portfolio and any due bill carry into the new run with everything else the player owns. NO MANAGEMENT MINI-GAME, stated in the Bank tip and here: tenants are Ana's problem; the player's verbs are buy, pay a bill, sell. VERIFICATION: placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (136 colliders, 3 rental doors point-tested); a 34-assertion headless functional test of the rent module (buy gates incl. the business gate and double-buy, rentDailyNet arithmetic, quiet/late/good-tenant days to the dollar, repair auto-pay vs DUE at the cash boundary, the no-roll-while-due pause, door payment refusal/success, sell-back proceeds with a due bill netted out, advanceDay paying rent once per serial) — ALL PASS; server suite 32/32 (server untouched, re-run to prove it). Perf sanity: initial-load JS 836.0 kB gzip (index 460.5 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 10.8 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 29's ≈832 — +0.5%, the rent module + Bank section + door block + Ana handler; Eastmere (3.0 kB gzip) and Westbrook (2.8 kB gzip) chunks unchanged and lazy. No C++ touched.*

*Phase 31 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the §15.1 map ✅ line grows: streamed districts now count 3): NORTHREACH. PART A — PHASE 30 REGRESSION AUDIT. One defect found and fixed; the rest of the census clean. (1) DEFECT — SLEEPING DOUBLE-PAID THE MORNING TICK. sleepToMorning calls advanceDay() itself ("sleeping IS the morning tick") and then sets G.dayTime = 0.055. Phase7's day-cycle detector fires advanceDay whenever `G.dayTime < prevDay − 0.5` — built to catch the natural 1.0→0 wrap past 06:00. A sleep landing is also a backward jump: from any dayTime > 0.555 (game time ≈ 19:20–06:00 — precisely when players sleep) the drop exceeds 0.5, so on the next frame the detector fired advanceDay a SECOND time. The day-serial guard inside advanceDay cannot catch this: it stops the same serial paying twice, but the second call increments daySerial again and pays the fresh serial in full — businesses, rent, loan interest and the 10% auto-repay all ran twice for one sleep, and the day count advanced by two. (Sleeping before ~19:20 never triggered it, which is how it survived since the Phase 7 tick — and Phase 30's rent inherited it the day it landed inside advanceDay.) Fix: sleepToMorning stamps the serial it settled (SLEEP_TICK in game/economy.ts); Phase7's wrap branch skips its advanceDay when the stamp matches the current serial, and consumes the stamp at every wrap check, matched or not — a stamp is valid for exactly one check, because the first frame after a sleep is the only frame whose prevDay still predates the landing, so a later natural wrap can never be mistaken for a sleep. Destructibles still reset on a sleep landing (a slept night repairs them like a wrapped one). Post-fix, every sleep mints exactly one serial and pays exactly once; sleep-spam pays once per sleep, each sleep being a morning by design. (2) EXACTLY-ONCE, THE REST — CLEAN: ECO.daySerial and ECO.lastPaidSerial persist atomically in the single harborfall-eco key at the end of the same synchronous advanceDay that spends them, so a save/reload at any midnight boundary cannot split them; settleRentDaily sits inside the same guard as the business income. (3) DUE-BILL DEADLOCK — NONE: payDueBill accepts cash == due exactly (the door offers the payment whenever cash covers it and explains the pause when it doesn't); cash is earnable through every job system, so a paused unit is a debt, not a trap; and the Bank/door sell-back settles the bill out of the proceeds with the result floored at $0 — with bills capped at $180 against a minimum sell price of $14,400, proceeds cannot go negative in practice either. While due, the unit rolls no further events (the pause is total), so a bill cannot compound. (4) THE BUSINESS GATE — RULE DECIDED AND DOCUMENTED: buyRentalProperty (and the door prompt) read CURRENT business ownership at purchase time only. It is a lease-signing condition — Sable Lets wants a landlord with a trading name when the lease is signed — not a continuing covenant: rentFor and settleRentDaily never re-check it, so the warehouse keeps paying for as long as it is owned. Businesses cannot be sold in Port Sable, so ownership cannot actually shrink after the fact; the rule is now stated at the check in game/economy.ts so the code and the docs cannot drift. (5) NG+ — VERIFIED IN CODE: startNewGamePlus never imports RENTALS, never writes the harborfall-rentals key; its KEEPS header documents the portfolio and the due bill riding with its unit; the Phase 14 slot layer prefixes the key per slot automatically. (6) BANK = TICK — VERIFIED FOR EVERY COMBINATION: the Bank's per-unit line is rentFor(p) (0 while a bill is due, the due amount shown instead) and its total is rentDailyNet(); the tick's expectation is the same rentDailyNet, differing only by that morning's tenant-event rolls — the documented contract, identical in shape to the business line's club-weekend term. 0–3 units × due/not-due all read the one source. PART B — NORTHREACH, the third streamed district. SITING: Northgate's x=−20 road column runs that district's full height and already carries the causeway south to Port Sable; Northreach continues the same axis north — a short bridge deck (z −305..−295) from Northgate's north shore (z=−296) to a clifftop headland, land x −72..32, z −368..−304. The registry gains one entry; the gate sits on Northgate land at the bridge mouth (−20, −294, heading π) — the Eastmere/Westbrook pattern, since a restore point must stand on loaded land (the audit's first run flagged a gate placed mid-bridge: isBlocked rightly calls open water blocked). The district: main street x=−20 + cross street z=−336; 8 colliders — a stone CHAPEL (pale stone, steep teal pyramid roof, slim spire, amber window) and an OBSERVATORY (stone drum, weathered-teal dome, shutter slit) as landmarks, plus a tea house, a cliff store and four cottages; 3 parked cars; 4 walkers on kerb + cliff-path beats; a cliff fence along the north edge; 4 lamps; wind-bent pines; the NORTHREACH POINT viewpoint at the cliff edge at the main street's end; a welcome sign at the bridge's north end; HUD name via districtAt; big-map label post-load. Its static data lives in game/northreachData.ts (pure data — the lazy chunk and the audit read the same table); the audit's new section 7 checks the registry entry, open-water bounds pre-load, clearance from Northgate's north shore, the gate, and every collider/car/beat/viewpoint internally — 8 colliders, no findings. TRAFFIC-FREE like Eastmere and Westbrook, stated plainly. SAVES: a car saved inside Northreach restores at its gate while the chunk is unloaded, via the same streamedGateFor. CROSS-CHECK (1) — SERVER BOUNDS: charted land now reaches z=−368, so WORLD_Z_MIN moves −348 → −416 (every land extent + the uniform 48u sea margin, the Phase 27 rule), with the far-positions test extended by a Northreach standing spot and a boat rounding its north shore; the client's envelopes were re-measured against the new bound (below). Suite stays 32 tests, 32/32. CROSS-CHECK (2) — MAP PROJECTION: drawMap's world→map frame was span 700 around centre (5, 15), covering z −335..365 — Northreach (to −368) fell off the top of the map. The frame is now span 720 around centre (5, −20), covering z −380..340 and x −355..365: one uniform scale, no distortion — the existing city sits in a slightly wider frame so the new headland is on the map at all. The streamed-district drawing was already registry-driven; its causeway stub now follows the gate's approach axis (a gate south of its district draws a vertical stub — Eastmere/Westbrook draw byte-identically to before). CROSS-CHECK (3) — THE LEDGER ARC + A DEFECT THE CENSUS FOUND: the App loader poll is a data-driven array and now carries three districts (Northreach adds no mission gates; the ledger gates name their own loaders and are untouched — the arc cannot break). But measuring the movement envelopes against the registry exposed a REAL PRE-EXISTING DEFECT: the driven-car clamp in Vehicle.tsx was a fixed box fitted to the Phase 24 map (|x| ≤ 130, z −302..330) with no streamed-district exception — it has walled the player's car out of Westbrook (x −196..−130) since the day that district shipped. Eastmere (x ≤ 122, z ≥ −300) fit inside the box by luck of siting; Westbrook never did (the player could only walk in, and Ledger 1's tail could only be armed on foot); Northreach (z ≤ −304) would have been walled off entirely. The plane clamp had the same shape (|x| ≤ 135, z ≥ −305 — the Sparrow could not overfly Westbrook or Northreach). Both clamps now grow to cover a LOADED streamed district the vehicle is in or entering — the same rule isWaterAt already applies to land — while the water/blocker checks remain the real walls and an unloaded district stays unreachable exactly as before. The boat chart clamp and the swim limit moved north with the chart (boat z ≥ −412 = server bound inset by 4, the Phase 28 rule; swim z > −388 = 20 past the new farthest shore, the standing pattern), so a boat can round Northreach and a swimmer can reach its strait, both inside the server's bounds. VERIFICATION: placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (base 136 colliders + Westbrook 11 + Northreach 8); pytest 32/32; build PASS. Perf sanity: initial-load JS 836.4 kB gzip (index 460.9 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 10.8 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 30's 836.0 — +0.05%, the stub + registry entry + poll/render wiring; the Northreach chunk is 10.65 kB (2.93 kB gzip), separate and lazy, fetched only on approach; Eastmere (3.02 kB gzip) and Westbrook (2.77 kB gzip) chunks unchanged. No C++ touched.*

*Phase 32 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the §15.9 jobs ✅ lines grow by two): SERVICE JOBS. PART A — PHASE 31 REGRESSION AUDIT. Two defects found and fixed; the rest of the census clean. (1) DEFECT — THE PARKING-GARAGE DECK FROZE THE PLAYER ON FOOT. Player.tsx canStand: any point with groundYAt > 0.05 (or a current position so elevated) is forced into the SOUTH-BRIDGE corridor test (|x − BRIDGE.x| ≤ halfW + 0.4, z 86..238). The Northgate parking garage (PARK_GARAGE x −80..−56, z −276..−252, deck 3.1 at z ≤ −264, ramp x −80..−75 / z −268..−256) is elevated ground far outside that corridor, so a player who parked on the deck and stepped out failed canStand for EVERY axis step — frozen on foot until they got back into a car (the only escape, since car entry doesn't consult canStand). Fix: inside the elevated branch, a point inside the PARK_GARAGE footprint defers to !isBlocked — the deck's own perimeter walls are STATIC_BLOCKERS, so the rails still hold, the ramp still lands at its ground-floor mouth, and walking off the deck edge is still stopped by the wall band the step has to cross. The bridge corridor rule is untouched for bridge points. (2) DOC DEFECT — one stale comment: drawMap kept its pre-Phase-31 "span 700 around centre (5, 15)" line directly above the Phase 31 correction; removed, the Phase 31 text now stands alone. (3) DISTRICT-AWARE CLAMPS — CLEAN, conditions read exactly: both the car clamp (Vehicle.tsx, base |x| ≤ 130, z −302..330) and the plane clamp (base |x| ≤ 135, z −305..335) widen only when a district's `loaded` flag is set AND the vehicle's NEXT position lies within that district's bounds grown by 6; the widened bound is the district's own edge − 2. A loaded district the vehicle is not entering changes nothing. Districts never unload — registerStreamedDistrict guards re-registration and nothing ever writes loaded back to false — so no clamp can shrink under a moving vehicle; a car saved inside a district restores at its gate (streamedGateFor) precisely because the chunk starts unloaded. (4) ON-FOOT vs NORTHREACH — CLEAN: Northreach land is flat y=0 (groundYAt returns elevation only for PARK_GARAGE and the south BRIDGE), so canStand falls through to the blocker test on the headland; the swim envelope (|x| < 175, z −388..365) covers its strait. (5) SLEEP-TICK STAMP — CLEAN: SLEEP_TICK is stamped by sleepToMorning with the serial it settled; Phase7's wrap branch skips advanceDay only when the stamp matches the current serial, and the stamp is consumed (set −1) at EVERY wrap check, matched or not — so it can suppress exactly one check, the first frame after the sleep, and a later natural wrap always pays. A codebase-wide search finds exactly two writers of G.dayTime: GameSystems' frame advance and sleepToMorning's landing — there is no manual time-set, debug setter or mission time-skip anywhere, so no second double-pay path exists. (6) SERVER BOUNDS vs CLIENT ENVELOPES — CLEAN, all four recomputed against server/main.py WORLD_X −244..178, WORLD_Z −416..372: car envelope (base box; district growth Eastmere x1+2=124, Westbrook x0−2=−198, Northreach z0−2=−370) inside; plane envelope (same growth over its base box) inside; boat chart clamp (x −240..170, z −412..368 — the server bounds inset by exactly 4) inside; swim envelope inside. (7) MAP PROJECTION — CLEAN: one drawMap in App.tsx serves the minimap, the big map and every marker/label/route through the single S = size/720, mx = (x−5)·S + size/2, mz = (z+20)·S + size/2; a search for other world→map math (720/700 spans, centre constants) finds nothing outside that one function. PART B — SERVICE JOBS. THE FLEET: two new kinds join every table the kind census names — world.ts (CarSpawn union + CAR_MODELS: Sable Medic Van / Sable Fire Tender), CarBody.tsx (CarKind union, CABIN_POS, SHELL, and two new bodies: the medic van is the van shell in service white with a red waist stripe, flank + rear crosses and a lightbar base; the tender is a working truck — short cab, tall shuttered equipment body, flank hose reels, roof ladder, lightbar), Vehicle.tsx (BODY_DIMS [1.05, 2.1] / [1.1, 2.2]; road caps in the bronto idiom: ambulance −7..17 brisk, tender −6..13 heavy), game/drift.ts (0.8 / 0.6), game/depenetrate.ts HALF_W (1.05 / 1.1), the audit's BODY_DIMS copy, and store.ts's NpcCar kind union — the one entry the first pass missed, caught by tsc within the round, which is the census working as designed; traffic never spawns either kind (TRAFFIC_KINDS untouched). STATE (game/jobs.ts, flat fields in the house style): medPhase/medCarId/medPatient (the MED_SPOTS index)/medSpot/medTimer/medTotal/medDmg0/medCollect and firePhase/fireCarId/fireN (the FIRE_SPOTS index)/fireSpot/fireSize/fireWater/fireRefillAt/fireCollect; jobBusy() and jobTarget() extended (the amber pillar + GPS come free); jobStats gains patientsDelivered, medShifts, firesOut, fireShifts, serviceEarned, persisted in the same harborfall-jobstats key (per-slot via the Phase 14 layer, as every job stat). Spots reuse the dispatch sim's INCIDENT_SPOTS — the same roadside points its NPC units have worked since Phase 7: medical spots sorted nearest-first from the hospital (Ferry Quarter ~83u → Docks strip ~105u → Market Row ~136u), fire spots nearest-first from the station (Ferry Quarter ~84u → south-west grid ~167u) — the escalation is distance, stated in the data. VEHICLE DISCIPLINE is the rental/valet discipline verbatim: spawnServiceCar pushes a dynamic unowned G.cars entry at the shift spawn (depenetrated, Phase 25 guard), collect sets storedAway + state (0, −500) after clearing a last-car pointer at it, endMedJob/endFireJob mirror endValetJob (wreck → the hulk stays, pointer cleared; player still seated → collect-on-step-out via the medCollect/fireCollect flags ServiceJobs watches). PARAMEDIC (components/ServiceJobs.tsx): start prompt at HOSPITAL_DOOR (−97, 103), gated !jobBusy(), no mission, wanted 0 (a wanted player gets the warn line instead — dispatch won't sign you on with the patrol watching). The ambulance waits at (−98.5, 106.5) on the hospital lot, ~5.7u clear of the dispatch rush-event parking. Legs: pickup (clock 60 + dist/5 s — generous by design; the pay pressures, not survival) → arrive within 4.5u → treat: on foot within 2.6u, hold-E 3 s through G.hold (the channel PAUSES when you let go or step off; it does not reset) → return: the same generous clock reset for the home leg; delivery = the ambulance stopped (< 1.5) within 6.5u of the hospital door. Pay per patient = $110 + up to $60 × the return leg's remaining fraction + $40 if the ambulance took no damage across that patient's legs (medDmg0 snapshot at dispatch), + $100 shift bonus for a full board of 3, every figure through ngPayoutMult() — jobs use effectivePayout's multiplier directly since effectivePayout itself takes a MissionDef (the same reason Phase 22/24 jobs cite; the older jobs not applying the multiplier is a pre-existing inconsistency this round documents rather than silently spreading or fixing out of scope). A clock that runs out loses that patient honestly — no pay, Ren says so, the board moves on. The patient is a buildHumanoid rig lying on its back at the spot (rig cycles by patient), with a subtle breathing bob; the dispatch sim never had a patient visual — this one is new, stated. FIREFIGHTER: a NEW BUILDING — the Sable Fire Station joins the Docks strip at (−80, 109.1) (STATIC_BLOCKER hw 5.5/hd 4.1, pairwise- and lane-checked by the audit like every other; World.tsx frontage in the Phase7Frontages set: brick-red slab, two tender doors, hose tower, lit watch-room windows), FIRE_EXT_DOOR (−80, 103.2) on the strip's door line. The tender waits at (−88, 108) on the civic lot. NO flame presentation existed anywhere in the codebase before this round (the dispatch sim's fires are abstract — a unit parks 8 s) — so one is built, modest and new: three flickering emissive cones (scale = the fire's strength × flicker), six recycled rising smoke puffs, and one fire point light, all pooled meshes driven per frame. Hose: park the tender within 9u (respond → hose), then on foot within 7u hold E: strength drains over ~6 s of water (1/6 per second), the G.hold ring shows 1 − strength, a recycled droplet arc flies the parabola from your hands to the base, and steam puffs rise where the water lands — the arc + steam both shipped, neither is faked.
