The tank is 100 and a fire drinks exactly 55 (FIRE_COST × the full drain), so after fire one the tank reads 45 and CANNOT finish fire two — the refill is genuinely required, not theatre: refill phase points the marker at the nearer of the nearest hydrant (the street scatter's real hydrant props, filtered from world props) or the station door; either point works if the tender is parked within 9u of it and you stand within 3.4u, hold-E 2.5 s. A fire left burning 240 s (respond + hose time) burns out unpaid. Pay per fire = $160 + up to $70 × (1 − age/240), + $120 for both out, through the same multiplier. SIREN & COURTESY — the honest scope: the beacons are ServiceJobs meshes riding each unit's lightbar (alternating red/blue at the GameSystems svc cadence), flashing while a call is live; the SOUND is audio.ts's existing siren voice via a new jobSiren flag (audioTick's condition is now wanted > 0 || jobSiren), set every frame while you drive your unit on a call above walking pace, cleared by the same per-frame write so no stand-down path can leave a phantom siren. Traffic courtesy reuses the Phase 22 horn path by writing the exact value a honk writes — hornPull = 2 — on traffic cars ahead of the unit (cone 1.5–12u, |lateral| < 2.6, the horn scan's own exclusions: no dormant/dispatched/service/bus/parked/wrecked), throttled to a 0.4 s scan: those cars ease kerb-ward VISUALLY for ~2 s through hornSwerve. Their lanes, routes and speeds are never touched. There is NO police-yield hook in this build to reuse — the patrol yields to nobody — and none is claimed; the design document for this phase offered "reuse the yield hook if one exists" and the audit answer is that it doesn't, so the horn path is what shipped. WANTED-NEUTRAL: shifts start only at wanted 0; Vehicle.tsx's enterCar skips the steal roll for a live service unit (isServiceCar) and breakInEligible excludes both units — the service hands you the keys, so there is no break-in hold on your own tender; nothing in either job calls addCrime, and the siren is not a crime input anywhere (heat reads crimes + LOS, not sound). E-OWNERSHIP: serviceClaimsE() (jobs.ts) joins boatFishClaimsE in Vehicle's keydown — while a treat/hose/refill context is live on foot, E belongs to the channel, not the unit's door beside you; F still enters. STAND-DOWNS: bust/wasted mid-shift ends the shift (ServiceJobs watches the G.status edge and ends both jobs through endMedJob/endFireJob — resetAfterArrest/Wasted have already stepped the player out and teleported them, so collection is immediate); NG+ stands both down in startNewGamePlus beside the valet block (step-out-first, endServiceJobs, collect flags honoured); a wreck (damage ≥ 100) ends the shift and the hulk stays in the world; walking > 60u from your unit for 25 s abandons the shift. DISPATCHER: every bark and phone summary comes from "Dispatch — Ren", who also joins phone CONTACTS with the full how-to hint. Phone shows Paramedic/Firefighter rows beside Valet/Courier in all three stats listings. AUDIT additions: BODY_DIMS copy, carTests for both shift spawns, FIRE_EXT_DOOR in the door set, pointTests for every MED_SPOTS/FIRE_SPOTS point (the new station blocker is pairwise/lane-checked automatically). One overlap honestly noted: a job spot can coincide with a live dispatch incident at the same point (the sim may pick a spot the shift is using, and vice versa) — the two systems don't exclude each other; the coexistence is rare and cosmetic (an NPC unit and the player working the same kerb), and excluding it would mean coupling the sim's scheduler to job state for a visual nicety. VERIFICATION: placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (base 137 colliders — the fire station is the +1 — + Westbrook 11 + Northreach 8); pytest 32/32 (server untouched); build PASS. Perf sanity: initial-load JS 842.5 kB gzip (index 467.0 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 10.8 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 31's 836.4 — +0.7%, the ServiceJobs component and the jobs/world/phone additions in the main chunk; no new lazy chunks (ServiceJobs rides the main bundle like Phase7/Phase17 — the streamed-district chunks are untouched: Eastmere 3.02, Westbrook 2.77, Northreach 2.93 kB gzip). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 33 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the §15.7 combat ✅ lines grow by three): COMBAT DEPTH. PART A — PHASE 32 REGRESSION AUDIT. Three defects found and fixed; the rest of the census clean, verified in code. (1) DEFECT — RELOAD MID-SHIFT RESURRECTED THE UNIT. Vehicle's enterCar notes EVERY car as the last-used car (Phase 13), including the job ambulance/tender: noteLastCar wrote a dynamic snapshot (spawnId −1, kind ambulance/firetruck), and a reload mid-shift restored it through applyCarSave as a fresh dynamic car — while J, which is never persisted, reset to idle. The result was an orphan service unit in the world with no shift attached, and entering it rolled a STEAL (isServiceCar reads the live J ids, both −1 after reload) — the opposite of the Phase 32 wanted-neutral contract. Fix, in game/store.ts: noteLastCar returns early for the two service kinds (a unit is never the player's last car; their own car's snapshot, noted at its own wheel, stands), and the harborfall-lastcar load refuses a service-kind snapshot outright, so saves written by the Phase 32 build itself can't resurrect one either. The fleet path needed no guard (only owned cars join it; the units are unowned) and the garage path none (unowned cars can't be stored). (2) DEFECT — THE TOW JOB COULD BOOK YOUR OWN AMBULANCE. Phase8's tow-target eligibility (!owned, !storedAway, kind ∉ boat/bicycle/bus/tow) was written in Phase 8, two dozen phases before unowned dynamic service cars existed; a live job unit satisfied it and could be picked as the "breakdown" — the same census class as the NpcCar union the Phase 32 build itself caught. Fix: the filter now also excludes isServiceCar indices and the ambulance/firetruck kinds. A codebase-wide sweep for other unowned-car eligibility filters finds exactly one more — Vehicle's breakInEligible, which Phase 32 already taught isServiceCar. (3) DEFECT — A BURNED-OUT FIRE COULD MAKE THE NEXT FIRE UNFINISHABLE. ServiceJobs' burnout branch (a fire left 240 s) dispatched the next call directly. The fire-OUT path routes through the refill gate for exactly the right reason — a fire drinks 55 of the 100 tank — but a fire hosed to ~15% and then abandoned burns out with the tank at ~48, and the next fire then cannot be finished: the hose runs dry mid-fire, the hold label says "refill at a hydrant or the station", and no refill phase is reachable from hose — the only exits are the second fire's own 240 s burnout or abandoning the shift. Fix: the refill-point selection is now one routeRefill() helper shared by both paths, and the burnout branch routes through it whenever the tank holds less than FIRE_COST. CLEAN, verified: the dispatch overlap — MED_SPOTS/FIRE_SPOTS are fresh {name,x,z} copies derived from INCIDENT_SPOTS at module init; the sim's incident is its own ephemeral state (G.incident + car.dispatch on G.traffic entries), the jobs never read or write either, so the same OBJECT cannot be double-booked and the Phase 16 watchdog (which reads only traffic entries) is untouched by a shift working the same kerb; the coexistence stays cosmetic, as documented. The unit lifecycle: shift end / step-out collect (medCollect/fireCollect flags watched per frame), wreck (hulk stays, pointer cleared), bust/wasted (the G.status edge ends both shifts in ServiceJobs; the arrest reset has already stepped the player out, so collection is immediate), NG+ (startNewGamePlus steps the player out of a unit first, ends both jobs, then honours the collect flags inline), abandon (>60u for 25 s), and reload (phases never persist — no shift resurrects; the unit itself was defect (1)). The siren: setJobSiren is called UNCONDITIONALLY every ServiceJobs frame with a value recomputed from live state (phase + driving + speed), never latched by an event — so every stand-down path clears it within one frame, and audioTick's App-interval cadence can't outrun a stale flag. The garage-deck fix vs the bridge rule: the canStand garage branch is reachable only for points inside PARK_GARAGE (x −80..−56, z −276..−252) and only when the point or the player is elevated; the bridge corridor (z 86..238) is disjoint, bridge points can never enter the garage branch, and garage points defer to !isBlocked, whose deck perimeter walls are the rails. The start-gate census: a service shift can only start from its door prompt, which requires !jobBusy() (all nine job phases), !M.active, both service phases idle, wanted 0; taxi/delivery/farm/valet/courier starts all consult jobBusy(), which includes the service phases, so none can start over a shift; tow, race and mission starts use older per-system gates that never consulted jobBusy for ANY job (tow checks taxi/del/idle + no race; races check J.race + M.active; App's startMission checks nothing) — pre-existing for every job, not a Phase 32 change, and the overlap is state-safe (the shift's clocks simply keep running; no system writes another's fields) except for the tow filter, defect (2). PART B — COMBAT DEPTH. (1) COVER-LITE. The geometry already existed: since Phase 6 the vitals block computes G.inCover (crouched, on foot, outdoors, a blocker within 1.15u on any side or a car within 2.6u) feeding the police sight radius (0.62×) and the bust rate (0.6×). Phase 33 derives COMBAT cover from it (new G.inCombatCover, computed in the same block): inCover AND a firearm in hand (pistol/SMG/shotgun/carbine — a bat or bottle is not a gunfight) AND not swimming (the crouch flag can outlive entering water; the cover claim cannot). No new key — cover is still just crouch + position. Its effects land on the two resolutions this build actually runs against the player, plus presentation: the 🛡 IN COVER chip on the HUD (same snapshot poll as the STALL chip), a deeper crouch tuck in Player's pose layer (−0.48 → −0.56 dip, torso 0.34 → 0.44), and the PEEK rule — while G.aiming is held from combat cover you are exposed: the sight factor falls back from 0.62 to the plain-crouch 0.85 and the bust relief is suspended (grab builds at the full rate); stop aiming and both return. THE HONEST SCOPE, stated wherever the feature is described: a full-codebase search finds NO place where an NPC resolves a shot against the player — Port Sable's police close to arrest (bust meter, escort, K9), and nobody shoots back; there is no accuracy maths to hang a modifier on, so none was invented and no "incoming accuracy" is claimed. Interiors: cover never engages indoors — inCover's guard already requires !G.interior, Combat can't fire indoors at all, and the pocket rooms' furniture is not in the city blocker map the probe reads, so there is nothing honest to detect against. (2) THE KESTREL CARBINE. A new WeaponKind end-to-end: Combat spec (cd 0.14 — automatic, held-trigger fire like the SMG; range 38; perp 1.1; spread 0.03; carDmg 6; pedDown 5; recoil 0.045), its own WebAudio voice (sfx.carbine — deeper body than the SMG's snap, a shorter tail than the shotgun's boom), teal muzzle-flash/light/tracer tints (the palette's second colour; every other weapon keeps amber), and its own procedural pickup model (long receiver, curved magazine, skeleton stock, teal sight dot — no weapon in this build has an in-hand mesh; the pickup model is where weapon models live). Sold at Sable Arms for $950 (+40 rounds; rebuys top up like the SMG), carbine rounds ×40 for $80, and one world pickup on the mansion drive's east end (−75.5, 96.5 — the same proven-open strip as the Phase 3 pistol pickup, now point-tested in the placement audit). Q-cycle order gains carbine between SMG and shotgun in both App handlers; syncWeaponMirrors' armed flag includes it; the Phone stats weapon count goes /6. MODS — the full bench applies: WMOD gains suppCarbine + gripCarbine in the same persisted harborfall-wpmods record; the suppressor routes carbine shots through the soft crime path like the pistol/SMG (suppressedFor + the FX skip), and the match grip + long barrel ($390) tightens spread 0.03 → 0.018, stretches range 38 → 44 and softens recoil — applied in specFor, the same builder every other mod uses (there is no numeric clamp ladder for weapon mods in this build; buy-once booleans ARE the ladder, and the carbine is on it). AMMO, stated plainly: G.weaponAmmo is keyed per weapon kind — there are no ammo classes and no rifle class in the table (the automatics are the SMG and now the carbine) — so the carbine carries its own pool under the same 240 cap, it does not share the SMG's. (3) SUPPRESSION-LITE. In Combat's fire(), once per trigger pull after the pellet loop: peds within 4.5u of where the shot ended (the hit point, or the range end on a clean miss) roll 50% to flee; peds within 11u of the muzzle (they heard it) roll 18%; each other shot inside the last 3 s adds +10% (cap +30%) — sustained fire genuinely scatters a crowd — and the timestamp list ageing out is the decay; a suppressed weapon halves both chances (quieter, the same fiction as its crime path). Flight is the ordinary fleeUntil channel (5–8 s), downed peds don't roll, and the pass writes nothing else: no crime, no wanted, no evidence. The police half of the brief is a verification, and it holds: every crime Combat (and Phase9's bottle impacts) commits flows through addCrime → applyCrime → the Phase 29 heat choke point — a grep of Combat finds no direct G.wanted/G.starHeat writes — so sustained fire near police already raises evidence by witnesses alone, and suppression adds no second path. VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (the carbine pickup point joins §3); pytest 32/32 (server untouched, re-run to prove it). Perf sanity: initial-load JS 842.1 kB gzip (index 466.9 + three 177.3 + react-three 186.3 + CSS 10.9 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 32's 842.5 — flat (−0.05%, the carbine/cover/suppression code rides the existing main chunk); streamed chunks untouched (Eastmere 3.02, Westbrook 2.78, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 34 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the §15.10 activities ✅ line grows): DRIFT CHAMPIONSHIP + LOCAL BESTS. PART A — PHASE 33 REGRESSION AUDIT. One defect found and fixed; the rest of the census clean, verified in code. (1) DEFECT — THE SERVICE-KIND GUARDS DID NOT HOLD ON THE GARAGE PATHS. Phase 33's audit taught noteLastCar and the last-car load to refuse the Phase 32 service kinds, and its note concluded "the garage path none [needed] (unowned cars can't be stored)". That conclusion was wrong. Three write paths store a car with NO ownership or kind check anywhere: GaragePanel.storeCurrent stores whatever car the player is sitting in; GaragePanel.takeOut's swap branch stores the current car the same way; and the villa/apartment-drive auto-save in GameSystems (park any driven car on the mansion drive or the apartment spot for 2 s) calls persistSavedCar AND pushes into G.storedCars. Both restore paths then recreate the car as OWNED — takeOut builds the fresh entry with owned: true, and the boot restore of harborfall-garage-car does the same ("Garage-kept"). So: drive the job ambulance onto the mansion drive mid-shift, wait two seconds, and the unit is filed away; the shift's J.medCarId still points at the now-storedAway entry (ServiceJobs' collection then operates on a car the garage also owns), and next session an owned Sable Medic Van waits at the villa, no shift attached — the rental discipline ("never ownable/storable", Phase 32) broken through the garage door, and the Phase 32 build could already have written such records. Fix, at every point the census found: GaragePanel.storeCurrent refuses the two service kinds (and its button is not offered for a unit — the card reads "Service unit — not storable"); the takeOut swap treats a service-unit current car as no car (the take-out itself proceeds); the GameSystems auto-save's onDrive test excludes both kinds; persistSavedCar itself refuses a service-kind snapshot at the single write point; and both loads are defended for saves the earlier builds already wrote — the stored-cars load filters the two kinds out, and the garage-car boot restore refuses a service-kind snapshot and clears the key, exactly as the last-car load does. The fleet path needed no guard, verified again: only noteLastCar feeds upsertFleet, it requires car.owned, and noteLastCar refuses the kinds before either write. (2) COVER-LITE — CLEAN. G.inCover is reset to false at the top of the GameSystems vitals block and re-derived every frame (crouched, on foot, !interior, blocker within 1.15u or car within 2.6u); G.inCombatCover is assigned — never latched — in the same block from inCover + a firearm in hand + !swimming. Standing breaks the crouch, driving breaks the on-foot guard, entering an interior breaks the interior guard, and the swimming exclusion covers the one flag that can outlive its context (crouch in water). Nothing else writes either flag (a codebase search finds the vitals block as the only writer), so cover cannot stick. The peek fallbacks restore EXACTLY: the sight factor's ternary returns the plain-crouch 0.85 while (inCombatCover && aiming) and the cover 0.62 otherwise — the pre-Phase-33 numbers in both branches — and the bust rate's relief is (inCover && !(inCombatCover && aiming)) ? 0.6 : 1, so an unarmed crouch (inCover, never inCombatCover) keeps its Phase 6 relief even with the aim flag up, as designed. The HUD chip is a mirror, not a state: the App snapshot reads the same two fields. The Player pose reads the same flag. (3) THE CARBINE CENSUS — COMPLETE. Every place a weapon kind is enumerated carries the carbine: the WEAPONS record (a Record<WeaponKind, …>, so the compiler enforces it), specFor (gripCarbine branch), suppressedFor (suppCarbine — a suppressed carbine shot takes the same soft crime path as the pistol/SMG: addCrime("shoot", suppressed) with the FX skip, verified in fire()), the WMOD record and its Object.assign load (older saves simply lack the keys and default false), both Sable Arms lists (the gun itself at $950 with the 240 ammo cap applied in its apply(), rounds ×40 at $80, both mod entries), the world pickup (cap 240 in the shared pickup handler), the Q-cycle order in BOTH App handlers (keyboard + touch, carbine between SMG and shotgun), syncWeaponMirrors' armed flag, held-trigger auto fire in Combat's useFrame, sfx.carbine, and the Phone Stats count (Object.values over the six kinds — /6 is correct). No enumeration was found without it. (4) SUPPRESSION — CLEAN. The timestamp list cannot grow unbounded: fire() filters it to the last 3 s BEFORE pushing, so its length is bounded by the fastest weapon's cadence inside 3 s (≈38 at the SMG's modded 0.08 cd), and the heat bonus caps at +30% regardless. Downed peds are skipped (the loop continues on G.timeS < ped.downUntil). MISSION-CRITICAL NPCS CANNOT BE SCATTERED, structurally: G.peds is populated only by GameSystems.initWorld's ambient spawns (street walkers with walk/sit/phone/shelter modes) — every named or roled figure lives elsewhere (mission givers and Petra are component meshes, service patients are ServiceJobs rigs, the arrest escort officer is escort state, the tail target is a vehicle, staff are StaffWorld rigs), so the flee rolls can only ever reach ambient pedestrians, which is the feature. A seated/phone vignette ped can be startled out of its pose for the 5–8 s flee — cosmetic, and the same thing a horn or a crash already does to them. Fleeing touches nothing else: witness memory (memWitnessUntil) is a separate field, so a scattered witness still reports on schedule and the evidence path is unchanged; the pass writes only fleeUntil, exactly as documented. (5) SERVICE GUARDS ELSEWHERE — CLEAN (see (1) for the garage exception): noteLastCar's early return and the last-car load refusal (store.ts), the tow filter's isServiceCar + kind exclusions (Phase8), breakInEligible and the steal roll (Vehicle) all read as Phase 33 left them. PART B — THE SABLE SLIDE SERIES. Structure mirrors game/cup.ts deliberately: SLIDE_ROUNDS + a persisted per-slot state (harborfall-slide, own load guard, id-shape check with the Phase 19 migration rule — a mismatched save rebuilds the rounds but never revokes the earned gold), J.slideArm armed from the Phone → Jobs card, conversion at the course ring in components/SlideSeries.tsx (mounted beside ServiceJobs, polite prompt claims), J.slide as the live run (jobs.ts, beside J.race; jobBusy and jobTarget both know it, so a run owns the player's attention and gets the objective pillar + GPS like a race). SEQUENTIAL UNLOCK: round i arms only once round i−1 is passed; a stale arm for a re-locked round (a series reset) is cleared at the ring with a toast. THE COURSES (all on race-proven geography, all placement-audited in the new §8): Docks Yard — start (20,100) on the Docks Sprint's road, gates (60,100) (100,100) (100,20) (100,−60), target 500 in 120 s; Bay Infield — start (44,100), the Bay Run's shore route (60,160) (72,224) (104,224) (104,304) (40,304), target 800 in 150 s; Northgate Interchange — start (−36,−216), the Loop rectangle (−84,−216) (−84,−280) (−20,−280) (−20,−216), target 1,000 in 120 s. Start rings sit a short offset from the race rings on the same roads so the two series never share a prompt radius. SCORING HONESTY: the run holds no score of its own — drift.ts bank() now stamps DRIFT.bankSeq + DRIFT.lastBank, and the run loop adds lastBank whenever the sequence moves (at most one bank can land between frames: a bank zeroes the chain and a new one needs a full segment plus a clean second). Banks before GO never count (the sequence is re-snapshotted at GO). A collision loses the open chain inside drift.ts before it can bank, so the standing rule polices the championship with no new code. Points banked in a run still pay their usual cash — the Series board and the drift income are the same points, stated in the card text. ENDS: last gate records the run (pass = route finished AND target banked); the clock dying records it as an attempt (best counts, no pass); out of the car 5 s abandons with nothing recorded (the races' rule verbatim); busted/wasted stands the run down off the G.status edge with nothing recorded (the ServiceJobs pattern); NG+ stands a live run down beside the race stand-down. PRIZE: on the third pass — $3,000 flat into cash + lifetimeEarned (recordCupResult pays its $2,500 the same flat way), a Harbour Wire headline, the title in Stats → Records, and SLIDE GOLD (#d8a920) as an extra swatch in ModMenu's EXISTING respray palette, gated on a career goldUnlocked flag that survives resetSlide exactly as an unspent Cup voucher survives resetCup. The brief offered "a gold drift-tyre tint if a tint slot exists" — the respray palette is the build's car-tint system (per-car colour, persisted with the car through fleet/stored/last-car by kind+colour), so the gold lives there rather than in an invented tyre-tint slot; no new cosmetic system was built. CARS ONLY, stated at the ring: the drift feed is written by Vehicle's road-car step, so boats, the plane, bicycles and the motorcycle can never score — the prompt refuses them by name of the reason ("a drift needs four wheels and a handbrake"). LOCAL BESTS BOARD: Phone → Stats gains a Records section (🏅) gathering what was previously scattered or absent: best drift chain + lifetime banked points (jobStats), the Slide title + per-round bests/pass marks, ALL FIVE race bests — the brief asked to verify whether per-race bests exist: they do, jobStats.bestTimes is written by all three finish blocks (street in Phase7, boat + air in the special-race block) and was already displayed per-race in the Jobs app; Records now gathers them in one place with the Sparrow par alongside — the Fishing Derby best/wins, and the best catch with the lifetime count. The Jobs app keeps its own per-race rows (they were already there; the board is the gathering, not a move). SERVER LEADERBOARD — CHECKED, DRIFT DELIBERATELY NOT SUBMITTED: server/main.py's POST /v1/leaderboards accepts {player, race_best, job_earned} where race_best is a TIME (seconds, lower wins, _clean_num clamps 5..3600 and keeps the MINIMUM), and GET returns race + jobs boards on those semantics. Race bests fit and already flow — leaderboard.ts postRaceResult posts the bestTimes minimum + lifetime job earnings on every race finish (Phase 8). A drift score fits nothing: it is a higher-wins point total, and posting it as race_best would write a fake "race time" onto the board (a 900-pt chain would sit as a 15:00 time; a 40-pt chain as a fraudulent 0:40). The server contract is unchanged, per the brief; the Records screen says why in one line. VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (new §8: the three slide starts as car tests, all 13 gate centres body-checked — 137 base colliders); pytest 32/32 (server untouched, re-run to prove it). Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈846.1 kB gzip (index 470.5 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 10.9 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 33's 842.1 — +0.5%, the slide module + component + Phone sections riding the main chunk like Phase7/Phase17/ServiceJobs; streamed chunks untouched (Eastmere 3.02, Westbrook 2.77, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 35 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the §15.11 smartphone ✅ lines grow): PHONE MEDIA. PART A — PHASE 34 REGRESSION AUDIT. One defect found and fixed; the rest clean, verified in code. (1) DEFECT — A DRIFT CHAIN OPENED BEFORE GO BANKED INTO THE RUN. The bank accounting itself was right (banks before GO never count: the run loop re-snapshots DRIFT.bankSeq at GO), and the collision interplay was right (tickDrift's loseChain fires on the collision sample before any bank can stamp the sequence). But the chain is a state, not an event: a player could build a chain during the 3 s countdown — or carry one in from free roam, start the run, and release inside the first second — and the bank landed after GO with the sequence legitimately moved, counting a slide the run never saw. Fix: components/SlideSeries.tsx calls drift's resetDrift() at the GO transition (beside the sequence snapshot; resetDrift does not touch bankSeq/lastBank, so the snapshot still excludes pre-GO banks). The slate is wiped at the count — every banked point in a run now comes from a chain started inside the run. DECIDED + DOCUMENTED (game/slide.ts header): a chain started after GO while simply driving the course counts wherever it banks. That is intended, not a leak — the run is free driving on a gated course, the gates and the clock are the course, and drift.ts is the only scorer; forbidding banks "between gates" would require a second, parallel scoring judgement, which is exactly what the Series refuses to build. (2) ROUND STATE vs STAND-DOWNS — CLEAN. Busted/wasted: SlideSeries watches the G.status edge and voids a live run (countdown or run phase — the check is on J.slide, not the phase) with nothing recorded, the ServiceJobs pattern. NG+: ngplus.ts clears J.slide + J.slideArm beside the race stand-down; the Series state (rounds, bests, champion, goldUnlocked) is untouched and persists, the documented career-record rule mirroring the Cup. No other reset path touches slide state: resetSlide is player-invoked from the Phone card and preserves goldUnlocked by design. (3) THE GOLD + SLOT HANDLING — CLEAN, WITH A PREMISE CORRECTED. The brief asked to check that a slot WIPE clears the gold career flag. There is no slot wipe in this game: a codebase search finds no delete/erase path for slot data at all — saveslots.ts offers switchSlot (a meta stamp + reload) and slotSummary (a read-only peek), and the only removeItem calls in the build clear the garage-car and last-car keys. So there is no wipe for the flag to survive or fail. What does hold, verified: harborfall-slide passes through the Phase 14 prefix layer like every save key (stored as harborfall-sN-slide), loadSlide runs at module boot against the active slot, and a slot that never played the Series has no key and loads freshSlide() — gold included. Per-slot isolation is the layer's whole design and the Records screen reads the same loaded state, so no slot can show another's gold or records. (4) RECORDS SCREEN — CLEAN. Every value reads its real persisted source: bestDrift/driftBanked/derbyBest/derbyWins/bestFish from jobStats (harborfall-jobstats), Slide rows from the loaded slide state (whose loader enforces round-id order against SLIDE_ROUNDS, so the index-aligned rows can't drift), race rows from jobStats.bestTimes with the Sparrow row carrying its par label, the Cup row from cup state, the evidence row from HEAT. Nothing is recomputed, cached or cross-slot. (5) RACE BESTS — COMPLETE. Both finish blocks in Phase7.tsx write jobStats.bestTimes[def.id] (rounded to 0.1 s, min-kept) and persistJobStats BEFORE recordCupResult: the street block (which carries the Cup's street rounds — recordCupResult is called in the same block, after the best-time write) and the special-race block (Bay Circuit + Sparrow Run; the Bay Circuit's Cup call likewise follows the write). There is no third finish path — abandoned races record nothing, by the standing rule. A Cup finish therefore cannot skip bestTimes. (6) postRaceResult — CLEAN. leaderboard.ts post() is async with the fetch inside its own try/catch and is invoked as `void post(...)`; postRaceResult itself only reads jobStats, length-guards bests before Math.min(...bests), and playerName() is try/caught. No synchronous throw path reaches the race frame, and with no server URL configured it returns before any fetch. PART B.1 — SABLE TUNES (new game/tunes.ts; Phone's "Music" app relabelled Sable Tunes). THE PLAYER: TUNES lists four tracks with name/sub/BPM/loop length; the Phone app shows a now-playing card (disc state, elapsed mm:ss, position inside the loop as a bar + mm:ss of mm:ss), Play/Pause, Prev/Next, and a per-track Play list with the current track marked. The readouts are honest by construction: the loops are fixed grids (theme 128 16ths at 102 BPM = 18.82 s; club figure 16 8ths at 116 = 4.14 s; the new loops 64 16ths = 10.0 s / 11.43 s), so loop position is a real measurement — and there is deliberately no progress-to-end bar, because a loop has no end; the stopped state says exactly that in the card. THE TWO NEW COMPOSITIONS, both original and written for this phase in the codebase's documented style: "Daylight Causeway" (96 BPM, 64 steps) — Cmaj7 | Am9 | Fmaj7 | Gadd9, one chord a bar; triangle pads, a root–fifth–octave sine bass figure, soft four-on-the-floor kick + 8th hats + 16th shaker, rhodes stabs on steps 7/10, and a clean-pluck triangle lead that sits out bar 1 and plays an original line across bars 2–4 (E5 D5 C5 B4 | F5 E5 D5 C5 | D5 E5 G5 E5). "Neon Meridian" (84 BPM, 64 steps) — Am | F | C | G; detuned saw pads (±0.15%), an octave-pulse saw bass on 8ths through a 620 Hz filter, soft kick + bandpass noise snare, a quiet square arp cycling chord tones [0,1,2,1] a octave up, and a sparse vibrato saw lead across bars 3–4 (A5 G5 E5 F5 G5 A5). Both run on ONE scheduler in tunes.ts (the theme's lookahead idiom: 80 ms interval, 0.16 s lookahead, step grid per track) writing into a music bus from audio.ts (musicBus/syncMusicBus/audioCtx are the only audio.ts additions, plus isClubBassPlaying). PLAYBACK RULES: one music source at a time — playTune stops the current source across all three engines (theme, club, tunes loops) before starting the next; the theme track delegates to startMenuTheme/stopMenuTheme and the club track to startClubBass/stopClubBass, so there is one implementation of each loop. CLUB PRIORITY: Interiors.tsx no longer calls the club functions directly — its room-transition edge calls tunesClubEnter/tunesClubExit (the unmount cleanup calls tunesClubExit too, guarded to a no-op when not inside). Enter: a playing non-club phone track is stopped and marked suspended, then startClubBass takes the room. Exit: the room loop stops UNLESS the phone's own track is the club loop (same loop — it keeps playing, now carried by the phone), and a suspended track restarts from its top. Selecting a track from the phone while inside the club arms it suspended (the UI shows the held-by-the-club note) — the room is never fought. TITLE SCREEN UNTOUCHED: App's first-gesture kick still starts the theme and beginPlay still stops it; beginPlay additionally calls resumeTunes() after the stop, so a persisted playing=true track (selection + intent persist per slot in harborfall-tunes, loaded under the slot layer at module boot) picks up from its top in the world. A fresh slot defaults to not playing — the title theme remains a new player's first music, exactly as before. MUTE: the brief's "settings volume" does not exist — G.settings has no volume field and no music source has ever had one; toggleMute is the one audio switch, hard-setting the theme (0.5) and club (0.4) masters. Tunes gets the identical treatment: its bus is created at the mute state and re-synced to isMuted() on every 80 ms scheduler tick (worst case ~90 ms of lag after pressing N, inaudible in practice), and the Phone tip states the arrangement instead of presenting a fake slider. PART B.2 — PHOTO GALLERY. WHAT EXISTED: Photo Mode's capture (App.tsx PhotoCapture) renders one frame with gl.render and reads toDataURL in the same task — the standard render-on-capture solution to the drawing-buffer problem (the canvas is NOT preserveDrawingBuffer, which would tax every frame for an occasional photo); the component comment now documents that reasoning at the site. The Phase 9 gallery stored a 320 px-wide JPEG (q0.72) per shot, newest 10, per slot via the prefix layer, listed with date + delete. WHAT CHANGED: GALLERY_LIMIT 10 → 12 (oldest replaced — the app tip says so); galleryAdd takes the district label, captured in PhotoCapture from districtAt(G.playerPos) at the shutter moment (districtAt resolves streamed districts first, so an Eastmere/Westbrook/Northreach shot is labelled with its district, not "Port Sable"); the Gallery app gains a full-screen viewer — tap any shot and the phone page becomes the photo: full-width image, district + date, Delete, back-to-list (the phone's ‹ back button also exits the viewer first), plus a caption stating the honest storage deal (the phone holds the 320 px copy; the full-res PNG downloaded at capture). The cap rationale is written into game/phase9.ts: a 320 px JPEG runs ~15–35 KB, twelve of them well under 0.5 MB against the ~5 MB localStorage quota every save shares, while full-res PNG data URLs (1–4 MB each) would evict real saves within a few shots. BACK-COMPAT: GalleryPhoto.where is optional; pre-Phase-35 shots have none and render date-only in the list and a "shot before Gallery learned districts" note in the viewer — no district is guessed for them after the fact. VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (no world changes — 137 base colliders, §8 slide checks as Phase 34); pytest 32/32 (server untouched, re-run to prove it). Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈847.9 kB gzip (index 472.3 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 10.9 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 34's ≈846.1 — +0.2%, the tunes module + Phone player/gallery riding the main chunk like every phone app; streamed chunks untouched and still lazy (Eastmere 3.02, Westbrook 2.77, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 36 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the §15.1 dynamic-weather ✅ line grows): WEATHER DEPTH. PART A — PHASE 35 REGRESSION AUDIT. One defect found and fixed; the rest clean, verified in code. (1) DEFECT — THE TUNES ELAPSED CLOCK COUNTED SUSPENDED SILENCE. TUNES_STATE.playing stays true while Club Solace holds the player's track (that is the design — the intent persists), but tunesElapsed() measured plain wall-clock from startedAtMs, so five minutes inside the club read as five minutes of listening to a track that was not playing. Worse, playTune's in-club arm path (selecting a non-club track while inside) never re-stamped startedAtMs, so a track that had never sounded a note inherited the previous track's start time and opened with minutes already on its clock. Fix (game/tunes.ts): a suspendedAtMs stamp is taken at the suspend moment (tunesClubEnter) and at the arm moment (playTune's suspended branch also stamps startedAtMs, so an armed track's frozen clock reads 0); tunesElapsed freezes at suspendedAtMs while suspended. On exit the resume path calls startSource, which re-stamps startedAtMs — so the freeze is visible only inside the club, which is exactly where the suspended track's card is on screen. (2) CLUB SUSPEND/RESUME — CLEAN IN EVERY COMBINATION. Entering with the club track selected and playing: tunesClubEnter skips the suspend branch (same loop) and calls startClubBass, which is timer-guarded (clubTimer !== null → return) — no double-start; on exit the (playing && club) guard skips stopClubBass and the loop keeps playing, now carried by the phone. Entering with another track playing: stopSource + suspended, room loop takes over; exit stops the room loop (the guard fails for a non-club track) and restarts the suspended track from its top. Entering with nothing playing: the room loop starts; exit stops it (playing is false, the guard fails, suspended is false — nothing resumes). Leaving mid-suspend is the exit path above. Selecting a track inside the club: a non-club pick arms suspended without touching the room loop; a club pick calls startSource→startClubBass (guarded, already running) and thereafter behaves as the phone's own club track; switching FROM the club pick to another track inside runs stopSource("club"), which deliberately does not stop the room's loop while inClub. Pausing inside: on a suspended track, stopSource/stopLoop are safe no-ops against a stopped engine and suspended clears; on the club track, stopSource refuses to stop the room loop and the exit path stops it instead. (3) TITLE→PLAY RESUME — CLEAN. App's beginPlay stops the title theme then calls resumeTunes, which no-ops when nothing is marked playing or when inClub, and every engine it can start is timer-guarded against a second start (startMenuTheme: themeTimer; startClubBass: clubTimer; the tunes loops: startLoop's loopTimer check). There is no path to two loops at once. (4) MUTE — NO FIGHT. toggleMute hard-sets the theme (0.5) and club (0.4) masters and zeroes engine/siren gains; the tunes bus gain is written by exactly two hands — musicBus() at creation (reads the live muted flag) and syncMusicBus() on the tunes scheduler's own 80 ms tick (same flag). No periodic writer touches the theme/club masters, and toggleMute never touches the tunes bus, so no two writers can disagree about a gain. (5) PER-SLOT PERSISTENCE — CLEAN. saveslots.ts is imported first in main.tsx and installs the Storage.prototype prefix layer (plus the legacy migration) before App — and therefore before game/tunes.ts or game/phase9.ts — is evaluated; both modules read their harborfall-tunes / harborfall-gallery keys through the patched methods at module load, against the boot slot. switchSlot stamps the meta and reloads the page, so a slot switch re-runs every load against the new slot. Isolation is the layer's design and both Phase 35 features inherit it unchanged. (6) GALLERY — CLEAN, WITH THE LABEL'S ONE CONDITION STATED. Replacement order: galleryAdd unshifts the new shot and slices to GALLERY_LIMIT (12) — newest first, the tail (oldest) drops; galleryDelete rewrites the filtered list. Quota: every write (galleryAdd, galleryDelete, and PhotoCapture's own downscale block) sits in try/catch, and galleryList's parse is guarded — a full localStorage loses the phone copy silently and the capture's full-res PNG download has already happened; nothing in the path can throw into the frame loop. The label: PhotoCapture calls districtAt(G.playerPos) at the shutter moment. districtAt checks inStreamed FIRST, and inStreamed reads STREAMED_DISTRICTS — the registry that lives in world.ts (main chunk, data-side: bounds + names) — not the district chunks, which only register blockers/roads into it. So a shot taken in Eastmere/Westbrook/Northreach labels with its district even though every mesh of the place arrived by lazy chunk. The honest condition: inStreamed also requires d.loaded, so the label names the district once its chunk has loaded. That is also the first moment there is any district geometry to photograph — before it, the rendered world at those coordinates is open water, and a shot of that water labels by the mainland rules (e.g. Northgate), which is the truth about what was photographed. No fix: the condition is the semantics, stated here and in the audit summary rather than papered over. (7) SLIDE resetDrift AT GO — CLEAN, AS DECIDED. resetDrift() zeroes score/mult/open/active/angle/chainT/cleanT and names neither bankSeq nor lastBank — the GO snapshot in SlideSeries (run.bankSeq = DRIFT.bankSeq, taken immediately before the resetDrift call) therefore still excludes pre-GO banks, and a chain OPEN at GO is wiped with the slate: intended, per the Phase 35 decision documented in game/slide.ts — a run may only bank chains started inside it. PART B.1 — THE FORECAST (game/weather.ts). The pre-Phase-36 machine was purely reactive: Weather.tsx counted G.weatherIn down and rollWeather() rolled the next state at expiry — no scheduled future existed to expose. The change is structural and small: the weighted pick is extracted verbatim into pickKind() (seasonal weights from game/season.ts, the 13:00–18:00 storm multiplier on the live hour, the same ORDER), and the machine now rolls AHEAD — rollForecast() runs at boot and inside setWeather (both branches), so FORECAST.next is always the committed successor of the current state; rollWeather() at expiry advances into FORECAST.next and setWeather (or the hold-extension path) rolls the next one. A Y-key cycle goes through setWeather, so the manual override re-rolls the forecast in the same call — the forecast can never describe a future the player already overwrote. Because the pick is committed, the forecast is not a guess: barring a manual Y press, what it names is what arrives. It may name the CURRENT weather — the machine has always been able to roll no-change (it extends the hold, no toast) — and the surfaces say so: the phone's harbourweather.ps page gains a "Turning to" row under its pre-existing "Next change ~X min" timer, rendering "Holding as Clear" when the pick equals the current state rather than pretending a change is coming. The HUD glyph: App's snapshot computes forecastInGameMinutes() = G.weatherIn × 4.8 (a Port Sable day is 300 real seconds — GameSystems' dt/300 — so one real second carries 4.8 game-minutes) and the weather chip shows an amber "→ {icon}" when the forecast differs from the current weather and fewer than 10 game-minutes remain. Stated plainly: at this clock rate 10 game-minutes is the final ~2 real seconds — the glyph is a last-moments "it's turning" flash, deliberately brief; the phone page carries the forecast at all times for anyone planning around it. The only weather writers in the build are the state machine and cycleWeather (codebase search), both forecast-aware, so no third path can desync it. PART B.2 — STORM CONSEQUENCES. (a) TARGETED LIGHTNING (components/Weather.tsx makeBolt): strikes already existed (jagged bolt + sky flash + delayed thunder on a 4–12 s storm cadence), but the bolt always ran from y=38 to y≈1.6 at a random point — through rooftops and under the sea. Now ~45% of strikes pick the tallest building within 150u of the camera focus and land on its roof (endpoint = the building's height); the rest land on open ground at groundYAt's real height, clamped at 0 over water so a sea strike ends ON the surface. The spine eases toward the strike point and its final point IS the strike point. SPECTACLE ONLY, stated wherever it is described: lightning has never had a damage path in this game and none was added — no health, vehicle or ped effect exists. (b) WEATHER CHOP (components/Vehicle.tsx boat branch): the Phase 7 swell multiplier — (0.7 + wind×0.7), skiff-damped — now also scales by a chop factor, 1 + rainAmt×0.85 + 0.35 in Storm, so the Harborfall Core buoyancy amplitude (0.07 × swell) and the pitch/roll the hull rides grow to ~×2.2 in a full storm versus calm. Rain alone visibly roughens the ride; the Phase 28 storm DRIFT (positional, on the driven hull) is a separate term and is untouched, as is the fishing anchor (speed-based). (c) DRYING ROADS (game/store.ts G.roadWet; ticked in Weather.tsx; read in World.tsx + Vehicle.tsx): the pre-Phase-36 "wetness" was G.rainAmt itself, which lerps to the weather's target in ~1.5 s — roads snapped dry the moment the weather turned. roadWet is a separate soak value: while rainAmt exceeds it, it rises toward rainAmt at 0.3/s (a street is soaked within ~3 s of the rain arriving — wet grip and gloss arrive as before); when the rain falls below it, it decays at 1/150 per second toward rainAmt, so a soaked street (1.0) takes ~150 s to dry and a Rain-soaked one (0.75) ~110 s. The readers moved: asphalt roughness/metalness, the night wet-floor clamp, puddle opacity + env intensity and the streetlight halo strength in World.tsx, and the grip weather factor + the drift-tyre wet condition in Vehicle.tsx. The grip FORMULA is byte-identical (asphalt 1 − 0.32×wet, dirt 1 − 0.18×wet, drift tyres ×0.88 while wet) — only its input changed, from "is it raining" to "is the road wet", which is the feature: the street stays slippery exactly as long as it looks wet. Readers that are about the SKY stay on rainAmt: rain particles + splash rings, fog shaping, sky/fog darkening, the water shader's rain term, cloud speed and ped rain behaviour. (d) STORM TURBULENCE (components/Vehicle.tsx plane branch): airborne (alt > 2) with weather Storm — or heavy rain (rainAmt ≥ 0.55) at half strength — the Sparrow's mesh gains two slow sine disturbances (roll ±~0.05 rad from ω 1.7 + 2.9 rad/s terms, pitch ±~0.026 rad at ω 1.15 rad/s, phased per airframe). PRESENTATION ONLY, and the reason it can be: the flight branch writes car.pitch/car.roll as STATE (the values the flight model, the camera and Phase7's ring gates all read), while the render branch only copies them onto the mesh — the turbulence is added at the copy, never to the state. The Sparrow Run therefore flies the identical path in a storm as in calm air and its 120 s par is exactly as reachable; the storm unsettles the airframe you SEE. The harbourmaster's phone note has always advised keeping the Sparrow hangared in a storm; now it looks like something. PART B.3 — SEASON TOUCH. WHAT SEASONS ALREADY DO (verified in game/season.ts, Phase 13): the sun's arc and the daylight window are season-warped (seasonSun: summer sunAmp 1.06/bias +0.14 — higher, longer days; winter 0.6/−0.12 — a low, short day; the warped elevation feeds the night factor, ped density and every light), plus sky/fog/water tints, the instanced foliage palette, the weather odds and the afternoon storm bonus; the calendar turns every 7 game-days or pins in Settings. So the brief's first candidate — seasonal sunrise/sunset drift — ALREADY EXISTS and is not re-shipped as new work. The one addition is the second candidate: AUTUMN LEAVES (components/Weather.tsx) — a pooled field of 130 points in a 64×16×64 box around the camera focus, vertex-coloured from the autumn foliage palette (ambers and browns — #a8742f, #8a5a24, #b4622d, #96702e, #7a4a20; no pink/purple anywhere near it), each leaf falling at its own rate (0.55–1.25 u/s by phase), swaying on two sine terms and pushed sideways by (0.6 + wind×2.2) — the Phase 7 wind value the palms and boats already feel. Landed or blown past +34u, a leaf recycles to the top on the upwind side. The field's opacity fades toward 0.55 only while currentSeason() is Autumn and the player is outdoors, and toward 0 otherwise (~2 s cross-fade, so a season turn or a door never pops it); other seasons get no leaves — spring blossom and winter litter were considered and NOT built, stated plainly. VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (no world changes — 137 base colliders, §8 slide checks as Phase 35); pytest 32/32 (server untouched, re-run to prove it). Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈849.0 kB gzip (index 473.4 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 10.9 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 35's ≈847.9 — +0.13%, the forecast state + leaves field + roadWet readers riding the main chunk; streamed chunks untouched and still lazy (Eastmere 3.02, Westbrook 2.77, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 37 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the side-storyline ✅ line grows): THE QUIET HARBOUR. PART A — PHASE 36 REGRESSION AUDIT. One defect found and fixed; the rest clean, verified in code. (1) FORECAST MACHINE — CLEAN. The writers census is closed: G.weather is written only inside game/weather.ts's setWeather, G.weatherIn only in setWeather/rollWeather (and decremented in Weather.tsx's frame loop, which calls rollWeather the same frame it crosses zero). Every weatherIn assignment is ≥110 s (the same-kind hold is 110 + rand×50; a manual Y pick holds 240), so no zero or negative timer can spin transitions — the only sub-110 value is the decrement itself, which fires the roll immediately. Boot: rollForecast() runs at weather.ts module import against G's defaults (Clear, weatherIn 110), so the first forecast exists before the first frame. cycleWeather (the Y key) goes through setWeather, which re-rolls the forecast in the same call — the manual override can never leave a stale forecast standing. SEASON PINNING: currentSeason() checks the Settings pin first, and the pin only steers pickKind()'s weights (via seasonMeta) for FUTURE rolls — nothing writes the weather or the forecast from the season path, so a mid-hold pin change cannot falsify the committed forecast; it shapes the roll AFTER next. SLEEP: sleepToMorning touches no weather state; the weather clock is real-seconds in Weather.tsx and frozen while paused. HUD GLYPH: App computes forecastInGameMinutes() = G.weatherIn × 4.8 (a Port Sable day is 300 real seconds, so 1 real second = 4.8 game-minutes) and shows the amber → glyph when the forecast differs and fewer than 10 game-minutes remain — the final ~2.08 real seconds, exactly the "last-moments warning" the Phase 36 note documents. (2) roadWet — CLEAN, WITH THE PERSISTENCE QUESTION ANSWERED. The reader census: Vehicle.tsx's asphalt grip factor (1 − 0.32×roadWet) and its drift-tyre wet condition (roadWet > 0.05 → ×0.88) read the same value the same way; World.tsx's gloss/puddle/halo readers moved with them; the sky-side readers (rain particles, fog shape, water shader, ped rain behaviour, police sight radius) stay on rainAmt, which store.ts's comment documents as the deliberate split — sky vs street. PERSISTENCE: weather, rainAmt and roadWet are session-only G fields (defaults Clear/0/0) — no save key anywhere writes them. A reload is therefore a CONSISTENT cold start (clear sky, dry road) from which roadWet re-derives from the live weather; there is no saved mid-dry state to snap FROM, and within a session the value is continuous. No fix: the semantics are the honest ones, stated here rather than papered over. (3) TURBULENCE — CLEAN, RENDER-ONLY VERIFIED AT THE WRITE SITES. Vehicle.tsx's plane branch adds turbPitch/turbRoll only inside the mesh group's rotation.set, composed WITH car.pitch/car.roll at the copy; the flight state fields are written by the flight model alone, and Phase7's Sparrow Run gates read the state. A storm cannot move a ring gate or the par. (4) LIGHTNING — ONE DEFECT. The ~45% targeted branch picks the tallest entry of world.ts's `buildings` — the BASE-CITY generated list only; streamed districts register Blockers (no heights) into STREAMED_DISTRICTS, never into `buildings`. The fallback random point (55–115u from the follow position) was NOT footprint-tested: ty = max(0, groundYAt) is 0 almost everywhere, so a bolt could land INSIDE a base-building footprint and spear visually through its roof to ground level, and the same inside a LOADED streamed district's building — contradicting Phase 36's stated "open ground at its real height". (An UNLOADED district is safe by construction: its land is water pre-load, groundYAt ≤ 0 clamps to the sea surface, and groundYAt only ever returns height for the Bay Bridge profile and the parking-garage deck — no floating endpoints are constructible.) FIX (Weather.tsx makeBolt): the fallback candidate is footprint-tested — inside a base building it strikes THAT roof (ty = its height); inside a loaded district's blocker it resamples (up to 4 attempts; district colliders carry no heights, so their roofs are avoided, not aimed at — the tallest-roof targeting likewise covers the base-city list only, and the docs now say so); otherwise open ground or sea at groundYAt. The last-resort fallback is the ground under the player's own feet, which is always a real surface. Still spectacle only: no damage path exists or was added. (5) AUTUMN LEAVES — CLEAN. Weather.tsx's leaf field calls currentSeason() every frame, and currentSeason() returns the Settings pin when one is set — the pin wins over the calendar, per frame, with the ~2 s opacity cross-fade absorbing the change; G.interior suppresses the field outright (and rain, by the same flag). PART B — THE QUIET HARBOUR (side key "quiet", per slot, requires M.storyDone; offered by phone through the next part's own giver — Elias, then Lina, then Elias — checked in App's contact handler after the After the Fall pack; tracked in the Phone Story app with a per-part row and a settled plaque line; NG+ resets the arc and, with it, the plaque). TAMIM'S STATE, USED HONESTLY: the story code frees him in the finale (the ending frees Tamim; after1, "Tamim's First Shift", opens "Parole came through — Elias pulled two strings and a favour" and puts him to work alongside Lina), so the arc treats him as a free man working the harbour — his lines are written from that life, not from the cell. (a) quiet1 "What the Tide Left" ($150, collect): three of Solano's brass mooring markers on the town beach — the pier head (6, 116), Lantern Beach east (88, 118) and the west shore (−96, 118), all in the sand band the surface model calls beach, audit-verified standable and on land. The collect kind's popup was hardcoded to the Ledger Line's text ("Ledger page recovered!"); MissionDef gains an optional collectLabel (default unchanged — the Ledger's own collect use is untouched) and quiet1's markers name themselves. Elias waits at the pier as a named figure for the duration (components/QuietHarbour.tsx). (b) quiet2 "Lina's Day Off" ($200, delivery): garage pickup (−40, −28 — the same kerb as s1/lina1/after1) → the observatory road (8, −336) → Northreach Point (−20, −361, the viewpoint pad's road end). THE PASSENGER, HONESTLY: this game has no in-car passenger mesh for anyone — the player's own figure hides while driving, and the taxi system renders a hailing figure at the kerb plus bark() lines en route (the Chatty pattern). quiet2 is exactly that shape, as a mission: Lina's figure waits at the garage during the pickup step and at Northreach Point for the closing beat, and en route she talks — five subtitle beats (boarding, rolling clear, Northgate, the bridge, over the bridge) fired once each from GameSystems on distance/z thresholds, only while she is aboard and the player is driving; the fired-set clears whenever the ride leaves step 1, so a retry replays the conversation. NO TIMER PRESSURE, SCOPED TO THE ENGINE: the mission loop decrements M.timer every frame and fails at zero — there is no timerless mission — and bust/wasted cannot fail ANY mission (the sim freezes while G.status is set and the mission persists across the respawn). quiet2's clock is therefore 3600 s against a ~4-minute drive: no pressure in practice, and the scoping is written here and in the def rather than the mission being described as "unfailable". DISTRICT GATING: calling Lina for part 2 runs ensureDistrictLoaded(northreachLoader) — the Ledger pattern (waits out an in-flight load, retries once) — and on failure plays Lina's postpone dialogue and starts nothing; startMission also warm-loads Northreach for quiet2 (replays included, as the Ledger's replays warm their districts; the App's district poll keeps retrying at the gate on approach, so a replayed ride is never stranded either). (c) quiet3 "The Bench" ($250, goto): a NEW permanent bench prop joins world.ts's scatter on the Coral Shores promenade at (72, 200.3) — the open gap between the waterfront houses, facing north across the bay toward the city and the Bay Bridge — rendered by the existing instanced prop pass and covered by the audit's scatter sections automatically. Elias and Tamim wait beside it as figures; the outro is the arc's long closing conversation; the $250 is Elias's gift, framed as his in the dialogue, paid through effectivePayout like every completion (NG+ scaling applies, as to all payouts). THE PLAQUE: components/QuietHarbour.tsx renders a brass plaque on a post at the bench's north side — canvas-textured face reading "For the ones who stayed." — whenever M.side.quiet >= 3. No new save key: the side record IS the persistence, so the plaque survives reloads per slot, and ngplus.ts's reset of M.side takes it away with the arc (the NG+ confirm note in the Phone names it). Fallout: part 1 → Elias's thanks by phone; part 2 → Lina's text; part 3 → the Wire headline "A QUIETER HARBOUR", Tamim's message, and a toast naming the plaque. PLACEMENT AUDIT §9 (tools/audit-entry.ts): the arc's real defs are imported from game/missions.ts (no drift possible); quiet1's points point-tested + water-checked; quiet2's Northreach targets and Lina's Point figure tested against NORTHREACH_BLOCKERS directly (district blockers are not in the base collider set — the §7 rig) and inside the district bounds; quiet3's target point-tested, the plaque post circle-tested, and all four land-side figure spots point-tested (literals mirrored from the React component, the standing pattern). The audit caught TWO first-draft defects, both fixed: Elias's pier spot (3.2, 107.5) clipped the harbour-office frontage blocker (west edge x=3.0) — moved to (1.2, 107.5); Lina's garage spot (−36.8, −24.6) clipped her own building's SW corner — moved to (−38.5, −22.5), still inside the pickup radius. The bench itself passed sections 4/5 on its first run (nearest Coral lane envelope ≥23u; nearest house face 5.5u clear). VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (137 base colliders — the bench is a prop, not a collider; §9 quiet checks as above); pytest 32/32 (server untouched, re-run to prove it). Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈852.7 kB gzip (index 477.1 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 10.9 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 36's ≈849.0 — +0.44%, the arc's defs + the QuietHarbour component + wiring in the main chunk; streamed chunks untouched and still lazy (Eastmere 3.02, Westbrook 2.77, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 38 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the §11 multiplayer 🟡 line grows and stays 🟡 — presence is session-only, there are no accounts and no persistence of who was in a room, and a self-hosted server is still required): MULTIPLAYER POLISH. PART A — PHASE 37 REGRESSION AUDIT. One defect found and fixed; the rest clean, verified in code. (1) collectLabel — CLEAN. The popup branch (GameSystems) reads `def.collectLabel ?? "Ledger page recovered!"` — the fallback is the pre-Phase-37 hardcoded text, byte-identical, so every def without the field renders exactly what it rendered before Phase 37: the Ch.2 ledger-pages mission (its text is literally about ledger pages), the Ledger Line's own collect use, and quiet1 alone overrides. CENSUS NOTED, NOT CHANGED: two older side missions reuse the collect kind (Lina's "sugar the vans", the heist's "take the manifest book") and have shown the ledger-pages text since long before Phase 37 — a pre-existing quirk of the kind's reuse, not a Phase 37 regression; rewriting their popups is content work for a later round, recorded here rather than silently "fixed" inside an audit. (2) quiet2's bark fired-set — CLEAN, PROVEN AT THE LIFECYCLE JOINTS. The set (GameSystems' quietBeats ref) clears on any frame where the ride is not (quiet2 ∧ play ∧ step 1). failMission ends through endMission() (M.active = null), so frames with no active mission necessarily run between a failure and the player's R press — the set is empty before startPlayAt re-enters play, even when the checkpoint IS step 1. Step advance 1→2 falsifies the condition the same way; completion likewise; NG+ ends the active mission (endMission from ngplus.ts) before its resets, so a fresh run starts with an empty set. No path re-enters step 1 with a stale set. (3) THE 3600s CLOCK — ONE DEFECT (presentation), FIXED. The objective banner (App.tsx) rendered `M.phase === "play" ? " · mm:ss"` for every mission with no exceptions, so quiet2 — designed and documented as no-pressure — wore a ticking "60:00" for its whole ride. No existing flag could hide it (census: MissionDef had no timer-display field; 3600 is the largest timeLimit in the game, the next is 600). FIX, in the def-flag idiom of collectLabel/holdStart/runTimer: MissionDef gains `hideTimer?: boolean` (missions.ts, with the reasoning in its comment), quiet2 sets it, and the banner's timer segment is skipped when the active def sets it. The engine is untouched: M.timer still decrements and the fail-at-zero backstop still exists — after a full uninterrupted hour, which is the honest scope the Phase 37 note already documented; the debrief's time-left readout is a results screen, not pressure, and is unchanged. (4) THE PLAQUE — CLEAN. QuietHarbour renders it from `M.side.quiet >= 3` alone (a per-frame edge-compare against the persisted record, the StaffWorld pattern). Completion increments side progress only inside completeMission's dialogue callback under the !isReplay guard, capped by Math.min — a replay returns early and can neither advance nor decrement; nothing anywhere writes side.quiet downward except ngplus.ts's full reset, which is the documented NG+ behaviour (the plaque leaves with the arc). (5) THE BENCH — CLEAN. world.ts registers it in the props scatter (72, 200.3), and props benches join the isBlocked AABB set (hw/hd 1.05), so peds and the player collide with it rather than walking through; the placement audit's scatter sections cover props generically and §9 point-tests the bench target + plaque post circle on top — ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR stands (re-run this round). The Coral promenade at z≈200 is not a traffic lane (nearest lane envelope ≥23u per the Phase 37 audit). (6) PHASE 36 SPOT RE-CHECKS — ALL CLEAN, at the write sites. FORECAST: setWeather re-rolls FORECAST in BOTH branches (change and same-kind hold), rollWeather advances into the committed forecast, the boot roll runs at module import, and cycleWeather (Y) funnels through setWeather — no writer can leave a stale forecast; weather itself is session state, so a reload cold-starts Clear + a fresh forecast consistently. LIGHTNING: the Phase 37 footprint fix is in place as documented (base-building hit → that roof; loaded-district blocker → resample ≤4; last resort the ground under the player's feet; unloaded district = open water at y=0). roadWet: soak 0.3/s toward rainAmt, dry at 1/150 per second in Weather.tsx; Vehicle's grip factor + drift-tyre wet condition and World's gloss/puddle/halo readers all read G.roadWet (grep census — no straggler reads rainAmt for a street-side effect); session-only, so no saved mid-dry state exists to snap. LEAVES: currentSeason() is read per frame (the Settings pin wins), G.interior suppresses, ~2 s cross-fade. PART B — MULTIPLAYER POLISH (server/main.py + client; every change additive — a Phase 26 client against this server, and this client against a Phase 26 server, both still work). B.1 DISPLAY NAMES. The client sends `name` on join from a new Phone → Settings field (G.settings.mpName, default "Rayan" — the character's name; settings persist per save slot through the slot layer, old saves pick the default up via the settings merge; it applies on the next join, stated in the UI tip). The server owns the truth: sanitize_name() (strings only; control characters — ord <32 and DEL — stripped; trimmed; capped at 24; anything unusable becomes "Player", which is also exactly what a name-less old-protocol join gets). Names are stored per room (PLAYER_NAMES), echoed in the `joined` reply, carried in snapshot entries and in `player_joined`, and cleaned up on leave/disconnect/room-empty with the same discipline as states. The client renders a remote player's name as a floating canvas-sprite label over their avatar (the StaffWorld/QuietHarbour pattern; state packets carry no name, so the client keeps the join-time name across state updates; against an old server no name ever arrives and the avatar stays unlabelled). Names are presentation only — identity remains the playerId, and nothing gameplay-side keys off a name (stated in both codebases). Incidental, while editing RemoteAvatar: its marker colour was #6e72cc, a purple the game's no-pink/purple palette rule bans — now teal #2f8f83. B.2 TEXT CHAT. A dedicated top-level `chat` message type (the older event-relay "chat" event is untouched and still passes its Phase 26 tests). Server validation, in order: membership ("Join a room before sending chat."), text must be a string, control characters stripped, empty rejected, >200 chars rejected ("too long"), then the rate limit — one ACCEPTED chat per player per 1.5 s (LAST_CHAT; a rejected-fast chat does not extend its own lockout; join/leave clear the anchor). The relay is {"type":"chat", playerId, name, text} to room peers only — never the sender, never another room. Client: sending lives in the game panel's chat box (input + Send, maxLength 200) — deliberately NO global chat key: the keymap is full of driving keys, and the global keydown handler already ignores input targets, so panel typing can never steer the car. Received lines (and the sender's own, appended locally since the server never echoes) show in an overlay toggled from the panel: the last 6 lines, each fading with age (full for 6 s, then easing to 0.35 over 30 s, 1 s tick while visible). Server errors (too long / too fast) surface on the panel's existing status line. B.3 ROOM CODES. The join payload accepts the room as `room` (new) or `roomId` (Phase 26) — the client sends both, same value, so either server vintage reads one. Sanitised like names but NOT truncated: the pre-existing >48 check still rejects an over-long code with the error old clients know. THE DEFAULT ROOM: a `join` naming no room now lands in DEFAULT_ROOM = "sable" — previously it minted a random room, which meant two old clients that both omitted the room could NEVER meet (each sat alone in a unique room); "sable" is the city's own name and the shared lobby. `create_room` naming no room still mints a fresh one — creating a private room is its explicit purpose, and its Phase 26 test pins it. The client's panel field is the room code: it persists per slot (`harborfall-mproom` through the slot-prefix layer), the auto-join uses it, and the client's built-in fallback default changes "downtown" → "sable" to match the server's default (the VITE_MULTIPLAYER_ROOM env override is unchanged), so two fresh installs with a server configured meet in "sable" by doing nothing. Rooms were already fully isolated by the ROOMS dict — state, events, chat and join/leave notes are all broadcast within one room only; the new isolation test pins it for state + chat together. BACKWARDS COMPATIBILITY, stated plainly: of the 32 Phase 26 tests, 31 pass UNMODIFIED. The one exception is test_ws_flow's player_joined exact-equality assertion, whose own subject — the join notification — legitimately grew the name field (bob joins name-less, so the note now reads {"type":"player_joined","playerId":"bob","name":"Player"}); the conftest clean_state fixture also learned the two new state dicts. Anti-teleport, world bounds and state/event validation are byte-for-byte untouched in behaviour (the state relay dict keeps its exact Phase 26 shape — no name is stapled onto 20-per-second packets). PYTEST: 46/46 — the 32 plus 14 new in server/tests/test_mp_polish.py (sanitize unit cases; sanitised name echoed; snapshot + join-note names; old-client "Player" fallback; chat relay-with-name + no-echo; chat before join; malformed/empty/control-char chat; the 200 cap both sides of the line; the rate limit incl. recovery; roomless join → "sable"; two roomless clients meeting; the `room` field; two-room isolation for state + chat; room-code sanitisation + the 49-char rejection). VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (no world changes); pytest 46/46. Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈853.6 kB gzip (index 478.0 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 11.0 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 37's ≈852.7 — +0.1%; streamed chunks untouched and still lazy (Eastmere 3.02, Westbrook 2.77, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 39 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the enterable-buildings 🟡 line grows, 12 → 14 pocket interiors, and stays 🟡 — each room is still hand-built, and one room per building in the city remains the scope limit): TWO MORE INTERIORS. PART A — PHASE 38 REGRESSION AUDIT. One defect found and fixed; the rest clean, verified in code. (1) THE CHAT RATE LIMITER — ONE DEFECT, FIXED (server/main.py). Phase 38 keyed LAST_CHAT by (room, player) and cleared the anchor in BOTH join_room and remove_client. But join is a client-sent message the server accepts at any time on a live socket (handle_message → join_room → remove_client + re-register), so a spammer's loop was: chat, re-join, chat — each cycle resetting the 1.5 s limit; hopping rooms did the same. The limiter therefore only ever bound a client that chose not to evade it. Threat-model assessment, stated plainly: the server is open-but-validated (no accounts; a playerId is client-chosen), so this was never an identity-grade control — but within that model the anchor is the ONLY anti-spam mechanism chat has, and it should at least bind the identity it is given for as long as that identity persists. The fix is the cheap one the brief named: LAST_CHAT is now keyed by player id across rooms (dict[str, float]), is never popped on join/leave, and expired entries are pruned lazily inside the chat handler (the dict's only write site) once it grows past 512, so it cannot leak unboundedly either. The honest residue, documented rather than fixed: a client that reloads mints a fresh playerId (crypto.randomUUID per client instance) and with it a fresh anchor — closing that needs accounts or IP-keying, both outside this server's stated model, and IP-keying would punish shared-NAT friends, the exact people room codes exist for. Two new tests pin the fix (rejoin on the same socket; chat-in-alpha → hop-to-beta → chat), and the suite grows 46 → 48. (2) NAME/STATE CLEANUP ON DISCONNECT — CLEAN. remove_client pops the member from ROOMS/PLAYER_STATES/PLAYER_NAMES and the LAST_STATE anchor, and when the room empties it pops the room's three dicts together; join_room's failure exits (duplicate id, full room) return before registering anything; a socket that dies mid-broadcast is removed by broadcast's own failure path. No leak found. (3) CHAT vs DRIVING KEYS — CLEAN. There is deliberately no global chat key (sending lives in the panel), and every keyboard consumer — App's shortcut handler, Player, Vehicle, Combat — returns early on isInteractiveTarget(e.target), so typing in the panel's chat input (or any input) cannot steer, fire, or trigger a shortcut. (4) mpName DEFAULT MERGE — CLEAN. store.ts loads settings as { ...defaults, ...saved }, so a pre-Phase-38 save with no mpName picks up "Rayan"; Multiplayer.ts also falls back `G.settings.mpName || "Rayan"` at connect. (5) ROOM PERSISTENCE PER SLOT — CLEAN. connectToRoom writes harborfall-mproom and the boot read goes through the same key; saveslots.ts prefixes every harborfall-* key per slot (verified Phase 36, unchanged), so each slot remembers its own code. (6) hideTimer — CLEAN. MissionDef.hideTimer is set by quiet2 alone; the banner's timer segment is the only consumer, gated `!M.active?.hideTimer`; the debrief's time-left line reads missionResult.timeLeft, a results screen, and is unchanged — as are all other missions' banners (the flag defaults undefined). PART B — THE TWO ROOMS. PLACEMENT DECISION, stated and justified: the pockets live in the BASE bundle (world.ts INTERIORS + Interiors.tsx), not in the streamed chunks. The interior system is a single registry + a single component — Player's clamp, Interiors' prompt chain and RoomShell all read the one table — and a room is a few dozen boxes (the pair cost ≈ +4 kB gzip of initial load, measured below). Splitting two rooms into the lazy chunks would fork the registry for no loading win that matters, and the thing the streaming architecture exists to keep lazy — the districts' street geometry — stays lazy: Eastmere/Westbrook/Northreach chunks grow only by their door dressing. The DOORS are gated on the chunk being loaded (eastmereState.loaded / westbrookState.loaded, the eager stubs), and that gate is provably the only reachable state: an unloaded district is open water in groundYAt/isBlocked terms, so the player cannot stand at either door before the chunk arrives — the explicit check is the belt to those braces (it also covers swimming in toward the shore mid-load). B.1 THE EASTMERE DINER (pocket 13, cx 462). The room: brick walls over dark boards, Petra's counter along the back wall with stools and the coffee machine, three booths down the east wall with two seated regulars (the café guests' pose), a chalk menu board priced exactly as the counter prices it, a runner rug, pendant glows, the warm street-window strip, and the standard exit-door glow. PETRA, WITHOUT DUPLICATION: no earlier interior had to solve this — café guests and club dancers exist only indoors, and every other shopkeeper exists only outdoors — so the handling is new and stated: Interiors renders Petra behind the counter with the IDENTICAL buildHumanoid parameters as her street figure (grey ponytail, apron-brown top, same skin/bottom), and Eastmere.tsx hides the street rig + its name label whenever G.interior === "diner" (written before the chunk's far-away rest, which would otherwise never run from inside the pocket). She is in exactly one place at any moment, and the Ledger Line's street giver is untouched whenever you're outside. THE COUNTER works on the showroom's tap/hold pattern (its own dnEDown clock, both halves on the interact binding): tap E browses, hold E (0.9 s, "Ordering…" hold label) buys — Harbour chowder $14 (+45 health), Diner coffee $6 (full stamina + the café's 2-minute half-drain boost), Fisherman's plate $22 (full health + full stamina + the boost) — priced and powered between the café ($8 coffee / $12 pastry) and the Marina Table's $25 set menu, using the same G fields (health/stamina/staminaBoostUntil/eatingUntil) and sfx.eat, with a Petra bark per dish. THE JUKEBOX (by the door, default OFF — it is an appliance, not ambience) plays Daylight Causeway: game/tunes.ts's club hold is generalised into a room hold (roomHold: "club" | "jukebox" | null) with the club's semantics preserved exactly — entering a hold suspends the phone's track (unless it IS the room's track, which simply keeps playing), leaving resumes it, the elapsed clock freezes across the suspension, and playTune during a hold arms as suspended. The jukebox-specific care: its engine is the shared loop scheduler, so stopSource refuses to stop a loop track while the jukebox holds (a suspended phone loop must not kill the room's music), and the jukebox starts the loop directly rather than through startSource, so the phone's elapsed clock is never re-stamped by an appliance. Leaving the diner, switching the jukebox off, or unmounting hands the phone back; the Phone's suspended tip now names both room systems honestly. HOURS 06:00–23:00 are the diner's own (Petra opens an hour before the 07:00 shops, for the fishing crews — the shopsClosed() helper is the wrong hours and is not used): the street door locks outside them with the hours on the sign and in the prompt, and at 23:00 Petra calls closing ONCE (the club's Phase 24 watcher pattern) — the kitchen stops serving, the door locks behind you, nobody is ejected. B.2 THE WESTBROOK SILO OFFICE (pocket 14, cx 498). A lookout room at the top of the grain silo: the street door stands on the silo's south apron (a lift-shaft housing + teal plate + "SILO OFFICE — ALWAYS OPEN" sign added to the Westbrook chunk), and the standard door fade IS the lift — stated on the sign, in the prompt, and here; no lift simulation exists or is claimed. Inside: grain-dust grey over concrete, a desk with the red harbour log + loose pages + a lit desk lamp, an office chair, a filing cabinet, grain sacks, and two painted views in the Loft idiom — a harbour CHART on the north wall (canvas: the bay, both shores, the causeway, the island light, the amber shipping lane) and the BAY WINDOW on the east wall (canvas gradient: amber-hour sky, the far shore, a crane silhouette, the low sun; emissive-lifted after dark like the Loft's panes). THE HARBOUR LOG is the room's working function: reading the desk fills G.harbourLog from the REAL stores at that moment — the five latest Wire headlines (game/feed.ts newsFeed, the persisted harborfall-news feed) and every district's standing from G.zoneRep, sorted — and App renders it as a dismissible glass panel (the debrief idiom) that pauses the world while you read; empty states are honest ("No headlines yet", "a stranger in every district"). No NPC, always open. BOTH ROOMS follow the standing rules unchanged: wanted inside follows the interior rule (no bust in the pocket — no cop is ever within grab range there; heat bleeds while unseen on the shared cooldown — GameSystems reads G.interior generically, no per-room code), the minimap follows the existing interior pattern (the pocket sits off the city frame, exactly as the other twelve), door fades are the standard 130 ms pendingGo, and the Player clamp is generic over INTERIORS. PLACEMENT AUDIT gains section 10: the diner door against Eastmere's diner blocker (mirrored from Eastmere.tsx — the district has no pure data module — with the mirror-pattern note) + the Eastmere bounds; the silo door against the real WESTBROOK_BLOCKERS + bounds; and containment for ALL FOURTEEN rooms — every spawnIn/exitDoor inside the rect the Player clamp enforces (half extents − 0.55). All fourteen pass, the twelve older rooms included (the audit had never tested interior spawn points before). VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR; pytest 48/48 (46 + the 2 limiter tests). Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈857.6 kB gzip (index 482.0 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 11.0 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 38's ≈853.6 — +0.5%, the two rooms + the log panel in the base bundle, as decided above; streamed chunks still lazy (Eastmere 3.20, Westbrook 2.94, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip — the two door dressings account for the growth). No C++ touched.*
*Phase 40 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the motorcycles ✅ line grows): BIKE HANDLING. PART A — PHASE 39 REGRESSION AUDIT.
