ZERO defects found; every check clean, verified in code. (1) DOOR GATING — CLEAN. Interiors.tsx's street branches check eastmereState.loaded / westbrookState.loaded before offering either new entry action; unloaded yields the warn prompt only, no act. The swimming-at-the-door mid-load edge is the same state (the door spot is open water until the chunk lands), so the same check covers it — there is no reachable frame where the player stands at either door with the district unloaded and an entry offered. (2) G.interior ON EXIT PATHS — CLEAN. respawnPoint() (store.ts) sets G.interior = "" and is called by both resetAfterArrest and resetAfterWasted; App's respawn() is status-guarded (the Phase 30 fix) and funnels through them, so a bust or wasted teleport out of the diner or the silo office always clears the pocket. NG+ never teleports the player (state resets in place), so no interior inconsistency can arise there; character-select teleports are pre-start only. The 130 ms pendingGo fires unconditionally, but respawnPoint is the authoritative last writer in every interleaving — no ordering strands the player in a room they were arrested out of. (3) PETRA'S HIDE — CLEAN, CANNOT STRAND. Eastmere.tsx's frame loop sets the street rig's visibility from G.interior === "diner" BEFORE its far-away early return, and her name label is re-derived on the same condition every relevant frame. It is a per-frame derivation with no latch: exit by door, bust, wasted or reload and the next frame restores her. (4) ROOM-HOLD PRIORITY — CLEAN. tunes.ts's roomHold is a single slot ("club" | "jukebox" | null); the jukebox can only start from inside the diner, the club hold only from the club, and neither room is enterable from inside the other; tunesJukeboxStart refuses while any hold stands, stopSource refuses to kill a loop track while the jukebox holds, the Interiors frame loop stops the jukebox on the first non-diner frame, and unmount releases both holds. The state machine agrees with the geography: both can never engage at once. (5) HARBOUR LOG STALENESS — CLEAN. G.harbourLog is in-memory only; App mirrors it into React state solely via the harborfall:harbour-log event, the panel's close clears that state, and the G copy is only read at desk-read time (filled fresh from newsFeed + G.zoneRep). A reload resets both sides — a stale-open log across a reload is impossible. (6) DINER HOURS — CLEAN. dinerOpen() is the diner's own helper (06:00–23:00); the 07:00 shopsClosed() helper is not used for it (it would have been wrong by an hour at both ends), and the 23:00 closing watcher mirrors the club's Phase 24 pattern exactly — fires once, kitchen + street door both read dinerOpen(), nobody is ejected. (7) CHAT LIMITER — CLEAN. LAST_CHAT is keyed by player id across rooms, never popped on join/leave, and pruned lazily (expired entries only, once the dict passes 512) at its only write site in the chat handler — bounded, as the fix claimed. The two Phase 39 tests were read in full: test_chat_rate_limit_survives_rejoin and test_chat_rate_limit_follows_player_across_rooms genuinely exercise both evasions (chat → rejoin / room-hop → second chat rejected "too fast" → the interval monkeypatched to 0 → chat flows again). PART B — THE FEEL PASS. B.0 HOW THE BIKES ARE STEPPED (the brief asked): the bicycle has its OWN kinematic branch in stepDrive (pedal power, stamina-capped at 7.6 u/s, no physics-core step, pitch/roll never written); the motorcycle goes through the CAR branch (C++ stepVehicleV3 + vehicleBody) with post-step character (launch push to a 29 u/s cap, tighter heading bias, 1.7× fuel drain). B.1 LEAN. The motorcycle already leaned in its mesh-sync branch (−steer × clamp(|speed|/22) × 0.48, driven bike only) — kept as-is. The bicycle had NO mesh branch: it fell into the generic else, which reads c.pitch/c.roll — fields its own step never writes — so it cornered bolt-upright; it now has its own branch with a gentler lean (−steer × clamp(|speed|/7.6) × 0.34). THE RIDER: Player.tsx draws the rider as the player rig re-posed on the bike (visible while driving a bike; seated overrides for both kinds); the rig's root took position + heading from the vehicle but NEVER its roll — the visible gap. A new shared module, game/bikepose.ts, carries the driven bike's pose (lean / wheelie / hopY) from Vehicle to Player; the rig now rolls by the bike's exact lean every frame (written unconditionally, 0 off the bikes, so it cannot latch), and Vehicle zeroes the pose + resets the state machines on any frame where the driven vehicle isn't that bike. B.2 WHEELIE (motorcycle). Input, documented against the keymap: there was NO dedicated wheelie input; the wheelie-lite launch pitch was automatic. The chosen input is the handbrake key — the jump binding (Space by default; the DRIFT touch button on mobile; gamepad X) — HELD together with the throttle at 2.5–15 u/s. The mobile path was verified, not assumed: TouchControls' buttons dispatch real keyboard events and the driving layout carries DRIFT (k=" ") and GAS (k="w") as separate multi-touch hold buttons, so the gesture works on a phone exactly as on a keyboard. While the gesture is live, the key is REINTERPRETED: the physics step is fed handbrake=false (otherwise the "wheelie" would brake) and the drift feed is told the press is not a drift (otherwise holding a wheelie through a corner would bank drift points for it). The wheelie is pitch ONLY — up to ~0.62 rad eased at 1.4/s; NO speed is added anywhere (the standing rule: no free speed), and the only state effect in the whole feature is the loop-out, which costs speed. BALANCE WINDOW: past amt 0.72 (≈0.45 rad) a 1.5 s clock runs; release Space or the throttle in time and the front settles (the clock also decays at 2× when released early); hold past it and the bike LOOPS OUT — the documented arcade rule: pitch overshoots to ~0.9 rad and flops back over ~1.15 s, speed is scrubbed to ≤2 then capped at 3 through recovery (the throttle cannot relaunch mid-fall), a thud + a small shake, and the rider is UNHARMED — no health is read or written anywhere in the feature. Above 15 u/s the gesture won't engage (too fast to hold one up — stated, not simulated). The old −0.1 launch pitch survives for plain W launches and is superseded whenever the wheelie is up. B.3 BUNNY HOP (bicycle) — SHIPPED, because a clean input exists: a TAP of the same jump binding while rolling (>1.5 u/s). The bicycle branch had never read " " (its brake light flag aside, which BicycleBody ignores), so nothing is overloaded; on mobile it is a tap of the DRIFT button while pedalling. The hop is ~0.5u for ~0.46 s (sine arc) applied to the bike mesh AND the rider rig via BIKE_POSE.hopY. PRESENTATION ONLY, stated plainly: car.state is never touched, so the branch's blocker/water checks still stop the bicycle mid-hop — the hop clears NOTHING, not a kerb, not a puddle; it is a hop for feel, not a traversal tool. B.4 THE CENSUS (both kinds through the Phase 28 tables). depenetrate HALF_W: bicycle 0.35, motorcycle 0.4 — present ✓. GameSystems' vehicle-collision radii special-case motorcycle 1.0 / bicycle 0.85 ✓; the Phase 8 tow filter already excludes the bicycle ✓; CAR_MODELS carries both kinds ✓; traffic spawns NO bikes (TRAFFIC_KINDS) — bikes are player-only world spawns, as designed ✓. CABIN_POS / SHELL in CarBody lack bike entries BY CONSTRUCTION (both bike bodies return before those lookups; DirtShell null-guards) — not gaps. TWO REAL GAPS, both the Phase 28 silent-fallback class, FIXED: (a) BODY_DIMS had NO motorcycle entry — the motorcycle read the sedan fallback [1.0, 1.85] in the vehicleBody wheel sampling (sample half-length 1.13 vs its real wheelbase); its mesh ignores the step's pitch/roll, but car.bodyHeave IS consumed by the group position, so the wrong footprint had a small real effect. Now motorcycle [0.12, 1.54] — the sampling formula's wz = dims[1] − 0.72 = 0.82, exactly MotorcycleBody's wheel z. The bicycle entry [0.1, 1.27] (wz 0.55 = BicycleBody's wheel z) completes the table honestly: its kinematic branch doesn't consume BODY_DIMS today, and the comment says so. (b) DRIFT_FACTOR had NO motorcycle entry — the motorcycle feeds tickDrift through the car branch and silently scored at the 0.85 fallback; now explicit at 1.1 (a light rear-drive bike slides willingly). The bicycle never feeds the drift scorer (own branch) — no entry needed, and none was added. B.5 COURIER — THE BRIEF'S ASSUMPTION CORRECTED, AND VERIFIED. The brief said "courier is bike-legal" expecting the motorcycle; the CODE says courier is BICYCLE-only (jobs.ts's own comment; Phase7's eligibility is !driving OR drivingKind() === "bicycle", and the leg clock PAUSES in any other vehicle, motorcycle included). Phase 40 does not change that rule — making the motorcycle courier-legal would be a design change smuggled into a feel pass. What was verified: drop detection is purely positional (within 3.4u of the drop point, read off car.state), and every Phase 40 change is presentation (mesh/rig pose, BIKE_POSE) or input reinterpretation inside the motorcycle's own step — the bicycle's state path is byte-identical except the hop block, which writes only BIKE_POSE.hopY. A courier chain on the bicycle delivers exactly as before, hop or no hop. VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (no world changes); pytest 48/48 (server untouched, re-run to prove it). Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈858.3 kB gzip (index 482.6 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 11.0 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 39's ≈857.6 — +0.1%, the bikepose module + pose code riding the main chunk; streamed chunks untouched and still lazy (Eastmere 3.20, Westbrook 2.94, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 41 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the businesses ✅ and daily-income-tick ✅ lines grow, the employees 🟡 line unchanged): BUSINESS EVENTS. PART A — PHASE 40 REGRESSION AUDIT. Three minor defects found and fixed, all one class: Phase 40 reinterpreted the handbrake key as the wheelie input and applied the reinterpretation to the physics step (handbrake fed false) and the drift feed — but a full census of the key's consumers in the car branch found three more it missed. (1) BRAKE LIGHT — brakeRef is set in stepDrive's preamble from the RAW key set (`k.has("s") || k.has(" ")`), so the brake light burned for the entire wheelie while the bike accelerated; fixed by clearing brakeRef while the gesture is live (during a live gesture it can only be true via the space key — throttle > 0 excludes S). (2) SLIDE TRIM — the driving-skill bonus (`k.has(" ") && G.skills.driving >= 50`) trimmed heading change by 10% during a wheelie as if handbrake-drifting; now gated on !wheelieGesture. (3) SKID SMOKE/SFX — the effects loop's `drifting` read the raw key, so a wheelie above 6 u/s (the wheelie band runs to 15) spawned tyre smoke and the skid sound, contradicting the drift feed's own exclusion two hundred lines earlier; now excluded while the wheelie amount is up or the loop-out flop is running. CLEAN, verified in code: BIKE_POSE zeroing (Vehicle's render loop recomputes the driven kind from G every frame and zeroes lean/wheelie/hop off their bikes; Player's rig applies the lean unconditionally — 0 off the bikes — so entering a car mid-wheelie cannot leave the rider leaned); the loop-out wedge (exiting mid-flop flips the driven kind, the state machine fully resets, and the parked mesh reads the pitch amount only while the bike is the active vehicle — downT can only run inside the driven motorcycle step, so no state can strand); hop vs courier (the hop writes only BIKE_POSE.hopY; the bicycle's car.state is never touched; courier drops are positional on car.state; courier remains bicycle-only by design); reverse logic (reads S/throttle only — the space key never feeds it); the parked-handbrake state (the gesture needs throttle > 0 and speed ≥ 2.5, so no parked interaction exists); the bicycle's brake flag (set from space in the shared preamble, but BicycleBody never reads brakeRef); BODY_DIMS consumers (exactly one — the vehicleBody wheel sampling in stepDrive; depenetrate keeps its own HALF_W table by the standing comment convention; the motorcycle [0.12, 1.54] and bicycle [0.1, 1.27] entries verified present); Phase 39 spot re-checks (tunes.ts roomHold is still a single club|jukebox slot with its guards; server LAST_CHAT still keyed by player id across rooms, never popped on join/leave, pruned lazily past 512 at its only write site; both new interior doors still gate on their district's loaded flag). PART B — BUSINESS EVENTS (game/economy.ts, components/Phase7.tsx, components/Phone.tsx, game/jobs.ts, game/ngplus.ts). THE SHAPE: rollBizEvents(serial) runs inside advanceDay's serial guard, after the rent settle, with the POST-increment serial — the day that has just started, which is the day the Bank app displays and whose figure settles at tomorrow's tick under that same serial. That alignment is the whole design: businessDailyNet reads the event only when the stored serial equals the serial being priced, so shown = paid holds by construction, and a headless check (below) proves it through the real tick rather than asserting it. Exactly-once is doubled: the tick's guard, plus a per-business check that a record already stamped with the serial is never re-rolled. State is ECO.bizEvents (business id → {serial, kind, staff?, fine?, paid?, passed?, fixed?}), persisted in the existing eco key with validated load; BusinessDef gains a district string (the RentalPropDef pattern) for the rep reward and the Wire copy. THE KINDS (one roll per owned business per day, ~35% any event; kind uniform, star only when crewed): RUSH — net ×1.5 (the event scales the whole net, crew and club weekend door included); message from the business's lead hand (crew[0], else the business name); ~50% of days with a rush also file one Wire headline. QUIET — net ×0.7. SUPPLY SNAG — net ×0.5 while unfixed; the fix is the SUPPLY RUN, a one-off errand on the delivery chain's exact shape (arrival = within 6.5u and stopped): start it at the business's door on foot (Press E) or from the Bank badge, collect stock at a supply depot (the delivery pickup points minus Lina's Garage — a customer, not a supplier — at least 40u from the business), deliver to the door; $0 pay, stated everywhere it appears (it's your own stock); landing it restores the FULL day (the fixed flag lives in the same record the net reads) and warms the district +2 rep. Unfixed, the snag expires at day end — the next serial's net simply never sees it. INSPECTION — pass = a message from Inspector Hale and nothing else; fail chance 15% FLAT (the brief's staffing-scaled rule was considered and rejected as fake depth — the note in the Phase 41 brief stands: no hidden maths); fine $120–$300 assessed, collected from cash on hand floored at $0, and the record keeps BOTH numbers (fine, paid) so the Bank badge can say exactly what happened. The fine is the one money effect that does NOT flow through businessDailyNet — it is a cash event at roll time, and the morning message says it was collected this morning; the net was never going to show it, and pretending otherwise would break the contract the other way. STAFF STAR — the named hand's staffDailyBonus contribution doubles for the day inside the crew loop (the wage is unchanged; a star day is not a pay rise); the message comes from the star themselves, naming them. PRESENTATION: morning messages ride pushPhoneMsg with the business's own people as senders; the Bank app shows a badge row per owned business read from bizEventToday — the same record businessDailyNet reads — with the snag badge carrying the "Run the supply run" button (J.wantSnag, the wantDelivery pattern). THE ERRAND'S CENSUS: J.snagPhase joins jobBusy and jobTarget (marker + GPS); it stands down on bust/wasted (Phase7 watches the G.status edge, the ServiceJobs pattern — the snag stays open and re-runnable the same day), self-ends if the day turns under it (the live event check runs every frame), and joins the NG+ stand-down list in startNewGamePlus. NG+ AND THE RECORDS: businesses, staff and levels are kept by NG+ and ECO.daySerial is never rewound, so the event records carry across cleanly — today's event is still today's after the reset, and the next morning's tick rolls the new run's first events; no migration, no wipe, documented here because the brief asked. VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (no world changes); pytest 48/48 (server untouched, re-run to prove it); HEADLESS ARITHMETIC CHECK 23/23 (test kept out of the repo, the Phase 30 precedent): per-kind nets against hand-computed values (rush 327 = round(218 × 1.5) on a two-crew wraps; quiet 153; snag 109 unfixed / 218 fixed; star = base + the star's daily bonus again, 346 with Sana; a star named for a non-crew hand = base; club Friday rush scales the weekend-inclusive net, 845), serial scoping (an event at serial S prices S only), dailyIncomeTotal = Σ nets under mixed events, and the full advanceDay integration — the tick pays EXACTLY the figure the Bank showed for the settled serial, plain day (120) and rush day (180) alike, a pre-rolled event survives the tick's own roll untouched, a second roll for one serial is a no-op, and the inspection floor (cash $50, fine $210 → collected $50, cash $0, both numbers on the record). Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈860.5 kB gzip (index 484.9 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 11.0 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 40's ≈858.3 — +0.3%, the events block + errand + badges riding the main chunk; streamed chunks untouched and still lazy (Eastmere 3.20, Westbrook 2.94, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 42 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the §10 birds/immersion ✅ line and the §15.12 fish ✅ line grow): HARBOUR WILDLIFE. PART A — PHASE 41 REGRESSION AUDIT. Two defects found and fixed. (1) THE TOW GATE WAS A PARTIAL CENSUS — Phase8's tow start checked `towPhase idle && taxiPhase idle && delPhase idle && !race`, a hand-rolled list that predates jobBusy() and was never extended as jobs landed: it did not refuse during a farm shift, a valet run, a courier chain, either service shift, a Slide round — or Phase 41's own supply-snag errand, so a tow job could start on top of any of them (two amber markers, two phone briefings, one confused GPS — jobTarget would have shown the snag and hidden the tow). Every other job start in the game consults jobBusy(); the tow gate now does too (its own towPhase check stays, since jobBusy includes it). (2) THE SNAG RUN COULD OUTLIVE ITS DAY — Phase 41 documented "self-ends if the day turns", and the frame check did end the run whenever the new day's record for that business was absent, a different kind, or fixed; but the check re-read bizEventToday, so a day turn that rolled a FRESH snag for the same business read "snag, unfixed" and the run silently retargeted: yesterday's collected stock would have fixed today's shortage. The run now stamps J.snagSerial with ECO.daySerial at start (endSnagRun clears it), and the frame check tests the stamp first — a run serves the day it started on, exactly. CLEAN, verified in code: EXACTLY-ONCE — rollBizEvents is called from exactly one place (advanceDay, inside the lastPaidSerial guard, after the rent settle) and both advanceDay entry points funnel through that guard: the 06:00 wrap in Phase7 (which skips its tick when SLEEP_TICK matches, consuming the stamp either way) and sleepToMorning (advanceDay + stamp); the roll is keyed to the POST-increment serial (the day that has just started = the day the Bank displays = the serial tomorrow's settle prices), and a per-business check (`bizEvents[b.id]?.serial === serial → skip`) makes a second roll for one serial a no-op — the Phase 41 headless check's "second roll" case still holds by construction. NO SKIP — a record from any other serial is inert at read (bizEventFor compares serials), never carried into pricing. CASH AT ROLL — a grep of rollBizEvents shows G.cash touched in exactly one branch (inspection: paid = min(cash, fine), floored at $0); rush/quiet/snag/star move money only through businessDailyNet at settle. SHOWN = PAID — the Bank badge reads bizEventToday(b) and the figure above it reads businessDailyNet(b), both defaulting to ECO.daySerial; the tick settles dailyIncomeTotal(settledSerial) with the same function under the settled serial; same record, same function, same serial discipline. THE ERRAND'S OTHER EDGES — bust/wasted stand-down (G.status edge watch, snag stays re-runnable), NG+ stand-down (ngplus.ts), door prompt and Bank button both funnelling through J.wantSnag → the single gated start. MISSIONS — mission starts do not consult jobBusy for ANY job (the long-standing "missions win" rule in jobBusy's own doc comment), and the Phase 7 job starts do not consult M.active either; the snag errand follows its sibling jobs exactly, and the exceptions that do check M.active (ServiceJobs shifts, races, Slide) are unchanged — no Phase 41 regression, no behaviour changed. WHEELIE SPOT RE-CHECK — the three Phase 41 fixes sit behind wheelieGesture / wheelieUp, both of which are motorcycle-only by construction (kind check at the definition), so a car's handbrake path is byte-for-byte the pre-Phase-40 one: the step receives the raw space key, the skill slide trim applies, the drift feed counts it, brake light and skid smoke/SFX behave as they always did; on the motorcycle itself, space outside the gesture band (speed < 2.5 or > 15, or no throttle) still reaches the step as a normal handbrake. PART B — HARBOUR WILDLIFE (new game/wildlife.ts + components/Wildlife.tsx; game/phase8.ts and components/Phase8.tsx for the fishing wire; tools/audit-entry.ts section 11). THE POD: five dolphins built from primitives in the house style (scaled-sphere body, cone beak and dorsal fin, box flukes and pectorals; slate grey-teal; NO painted eyes or cartoon faces) porpoise a closed Catmull-Rom loop — west offshore, south of Sable Island, up the channel west of the bay bridge, east along the mainland shore, and back along the island's north shore with a bow around the moored cargo ship's bow face. The path maths lives in game/wildlife.ts (pure, no three/DOM): a 512-sample arc-length table over POD_ROUTE, consumed by the component (podPathPoint per dolphin per frame), by rollCatch (the POD live position), and by the audit — one curve, three readers, no drift. Porpoising is a shaped sine (period 2.8 s, per-dolphin phase offsets, followers trailing 2.8u of arc behind the leader with a slow lateral sway); the pitch follows the arc's derivative, nose up on the rise, down on the entry. SPLASHES: breach and re-entry crossings (and dive starts) spawn rings from a 12-ring pool in the Phase 8 fishing-splash idiom (flat ringGeometry, scale up, fade out) — the Phase 6 wake pool lives inside Vehicle.tsx keyed to the driven boat and is not shareable, so the pod carries its own, as the brief allowed. THE DIVE RULE: each frame each dolphin tests G.cars for a boat/skiff with |speed| > 0.5 within 9u; a hit sets a 3 s dive (depth damped to −1.2, pitch flattening). Moored scenery hulls are not in G.cars and don't trigger it — the route itself keeps clear of them (below), and a stationary rental skiff at its spawn doesn't suppress the pod. HONEST SCOPE, stated in wildlife.ts and here: this is a scripted ambient route, NOT an ecosystem simulation — no feeding, no migration, no weather/time response, no population model; the dive and the fishing bonus are the pod's only interactions with the game's systems. ROUTE VERIFICATION (audit section 11, new): the audit imports game/wildlife.ts and walks podPathPoint every 1.5u of the ~399u loop (266 samples): every sample must satisfy world.isWaterAt, stay ≥19u from Sable Island's centre (r 17 + 2u shore margin), stay out of the bridge clearance box (deck x 55..65 expanded 8u, z span expanded 8u), stay inside the boat chart clamp, keep centre-clearances from the yacht (12u), tug (6.5u) and rental spawn (8u), and keep ≥3u off the cargo ship's hull BOX (ship-local transform — a centre distance is a bad proxy for a 44-long hull whose bow reaches z ≈ 146; the first audit draft's capsule approximation failed its own check at the flat bow and was replaced with the box test, and the route gained the (-72,140) waypoint so the real curve passes ~6u off the bow face). Two gull posts also moved on audit evidence: the pier-deck posts first stood at z 129.2, where the game's own isWaterAt says water (the deck mesh overhangs the waterline) — they now stand at z 126.5 on the deck's landward half. PERCHED GULLS: the brief offered follow-the-boats flock behaviour OR perched gulls; the Phase 2 flock (GameSystems) is a single group rotating as one body — its children have fixed local offsets and no per-bird state, so a follow behaviour would mean rewriting that system, not extending it. The perched gulls are additive instead: six new mooring-post props (pier deck ×2, Sable Island dock shore ×2, mainland waterline ×2 — post tops computed from groundYAt) with a gull each (white/grey primitives, amber beak, no painted eyes, wings on pivots). State machine per gull: PERCHED (wings folded, a 1 cm idle bob) → player within 5u horizontal → SCATTERED (a slow holding circle r 4.5 over the post, wings flapping, alternating direction per gull) → player beyond 9u for 2.5 s → RESETTLE (glide back, re-scatter if the player closes again mid-glide) → PERCHED. Audit section 11 also checks every post: on land per isWaterAt, outside every collider per isBlocked, ≥3u from the fishing stand points and the rental dock point. FISHING INTERPLAY: rollCatch already had a rarity roll, so the bonus is real and bounded — while the pod is within 40u of the cast point (dolphinsNear over the live POD position), the rare-koi chance doubles 3% → 6% and the pool weights rich (exponent 0.6, the island/offshore weighting). Both shore spots can be reached by the route (the pier cast during the north-shore run, the island cast during the island legs) and boat fishing anywhere the pod passes. The bark fires at cast release, once per cast, in Elias's voice: "Dolphins — good sign. The big ones follow the pod; your odds just improved." — it tells the player the odds moved because they did. PERFORMANCE: the pod group hides when the leader is beyond 175u × viewStreamScale() from the player (the traffic dormancy radius under the player's own view-distance lever — Near hides it sooner, Far keeps it longer) and whenever the player is inside a pocket interior; POD position updates regardless (the maths is a table lookup), so the fishing check is never stale. The frame loop allocates nothing — all state in refs, boat scan is an index loop over G.cars, splash/gull state preallocated. Nothing about wildlife is persisted: it is session presentation, like the Phase 2 flock. NOT CLAIMED: no dolphin collision (boats pass through a surfacing dolphin in the dive rule's blind spots — a boat arriving inside the porpoise cycle faster than the 3 s dive reads gets a visual overlap, not a crash; stated here rather than hidden), no gull collision, no wildlife in the streamed districts' waters (the route is the home bay's), no storm behaviour for the pod. VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR including the new section 11; pytest 48/48 (server untouched, re-run to prove it). Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈862.9 kB gzip (index 487.2 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 11.0 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 41's ≈860.5 — +0.3%, the wildlife module + component riding the main chunk; streamed chunks untouched and still lazy (Eastmere 3.20, Westbrook 2.94, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 43 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 86/7/0; the §8 Camera ✅ line grows): PHOTO CHALLENGES. PART A — PHASE 42 REGRESSION AUDIT. Two defects found and fixed. (1) THE BARK AND THE ROLL READ DIFFERENT CAST POINTS — Phase 42 wired the dolphin bonus in two places: the bark in components/Phase8.tsx tests dolphinsNear(F.castX, F.castZ) at cast release — the ACTUAL point where the line landed (stand point + reach × the spot's cast offset, reach = 0.3 + power × 0.85) — while rollCatch in game/phase8.ts tested dolphinsNear(spot.castX, spot.castZ), the spot's NOMINAL full-reach cast point. The two points differ by up to ~15u at the Pier (stand (2,112.5), nominal cast (2,134)) on any cast short of full power, and the pod's 40u bonus radius can easily cover one and not the other: Elias could promise improved odds the roll never granted, or the roll could quietly grant a bonus he never called. Fixed by making rollCatch read F.castX/F.castZ — the same live cast point the bark reads and the bobber floats on (F lives in the same module; rollCatch's only caller is the reel completion, where the cast point is always set). The Phase 42 headless precedent applies: one point, both readers, no drift. (2) THE NG+ STAND-DOWN NEVER CLEARED THE SNAG SERIAL STAMP — Phase 42 added J.snagSerial (the day a supply run serves) and cleared it in endSnagRun, and every exit path routes through endSnagRun (bust/wasted stand-down, day-turn self-end, completion) EXCEPT NG+: game/ngplus.ts hand-rolled its reset (snagPhase/snagBiz/snagPoint/wantSnag) from Phase 41, before the stamp existed, and never cleared it. Inert in practice — NG+ sets the phase idle and a fresh start re-stamps before the frame check can read it — but it is exactly the incomplete-census class Phase 42 was hardening against, so ngplus.ts now calls endSnagRun() (imported from jobs.ts, which it already imports) and the hand-rolled line is gone. CLEAN, verified in code: POD vs STREAMED DISTRICTS — the route (x −135..30, z 140..205) never enters any district's bounds (Eastmere x 44..122 / z −300..−192; Westbrook x −196..−130 / z −28..70; Northreach z ≤ −296), and components/Wildlife.tsx is base-bundle mounted unconditionally in App.tsx importing no chunk state, so chunk load/unload has no interaction with the pod or the gulls. POD VISIBILITY IN INTERIORS — the pod group hides whenever G.interior is set (and beyond the view-distance-scaled cull), while POD/POD_MEMBERS positions keep updating every frame regardless, so the fishing check is never stale; the gulls are ordinary world props at fixed posts (a player inside a pocket is at pocket coordinates, far from every perch, so the state machines simply idle). DIVE RULE / G.cars MEMBERSHIP — remote multiplayer players are NOT in G.cars (they render as separate RemoteAvatar scene objects driven by Multiplayer state; nothing in the MP path pushes into G.cars), so a remote player's boat can neither trigger nor be needed for a dive — the rule sees local traffic, job hulls and the player's own boats only. Parked boats and the player's anchored fishing boat sit at |speed| ≤ 0.5 and are skipped by the gate, consistent with boatFishCar's own stillness rule (< 0.5 to fish); a moving police or traffic boat triggering a dive is the documented rule working, not an edge case. GULL POSTS vs BLOCKERS — the posts are NOT in STATIC_BLOCKERS and isBlocked does not test them: they are pass-through, consistent with the game's other thin vertical props (street lamps and the props scatter — hydrants/crates/benches as POI flavour — are likewise absent from isBlocked, which tests buildings, trees, houses and static blockers only). The Phase 37 Quiet Harbour bench IS a blocker, but it is wide furniture placed as a mission target with its own AABB — a different class from a 0.2u mooring bollard; no change. SNAG SERIAL, OTHER PATHS — endSnagRun clears the stamp (jobs.ts), the bust stand-down and the day-turn/completion exits all route through it, and a page reload resets J wholesale (session state, stamp defaults −1). TOW GATE — Phase8's start condition is now `towPhase idle && !jobBusy()`; jobBusy() includes towPhase itself (the explicit check is redundant but harmless) and every other job incl. the snag errand, med/fire shifts and Slide — the swap is complete. WILDLIFE SPOT RE-CHECKS — splash pool indices wrap modulo SPLASH_N; dive state is per-dolphin refs; POD_MEMBERS (Part B) is written in the same loop after the dive lerp, so a diving dolphin's published height is its dived height. PART B — THE SABLE POSTCARDS (new game/postcards.ts; App.tsx PhotoCapture + photo bar; Phone.tsx Gallery; Interiors.tsx PostcardWall; phase9.ts gallery stamp; audit section 12). THE SET (desk order = evaluation order; one completion per shutter press): THE POD, BREACHING ($150) — a dolphin above the surface within 55u inside the camera's central cone (flat dot ≥ 0.7); AIR SUPPORT ($150) — wanted ≥ 4★ at the press; STORM OVER THE HARBOUR ($125) — weather is Storm and the camera is within 110u of the bay's middle; GOLDEN SPAN ($120) — golden hour (06:00–08:30 / 17:00–19:30 on the game clock), within 80u of the bridge centre, facing it; BREAKWATER GOLD ($110) — golden hour within 40u of Westbrook's viewpoint pad; SOLACE NEON ($100) — club hours (20:00–04:00), within 45u of the club frontage, facing it; THE SIGN AT NIGHT ($100) — night (20:00–06:00), within 110u of the hillside sign, facing it; THE DOME ($125) — within 55u of Northreach's observatory, facing it, any hour. Total $990 across the set. DETECTION HONESTY — the brief's condition is met head-on: there is NO image recognition anywhere in this feature, and the game never pretends otherwise. App's PhotoCapture already holds the real three.js camera at the shutter press (it renders a frame from it for the download), so evaluation reads camera.position and camera.getWorldDirection directly — the true framing, not a proxy — plus G.dayTime, G.weather, G.wanted, and for the pod the live per-member positions. That required ONE honest extension: game/wildlife.ts POD only ever published the leader's point, which cannot say whether any dolphin is breaching; POD_MEMBERS now publishes every member's position + height each frame (written by Wildlife.tsx in the same loop, after the dive lerp, culled or not), and the pod postcard tests a member with y > 0.08 (visibly out of the water on the porpoise cycle) inside the cone. THE HELI QUESTION, answered honestly: the police helicopter's position is a GameSystems ref, not exported state, and its orbit is a function of that component's clock — there is no honest live heli position for the check to read, so AIR SUPPORT checks the wanted level itself, and the Gallery copy says exactly that: at 4★+ GameSystems keeps the heli airborne unconditionally (visible = wanted ≥ 4, or ≥ 3 at night), so the wanted level IS the heli's presence, stated as such rather than dressed up as spotting. Every postcard's phone entry prints its actual check in plain language under its hint. Nothing was dropped: all eight briefed subjects are checkable from pipeline state, so all eight shipped. SEPARATION FROM MISSION PHOTOS — evaluation never reads or writes G.lastPhotoAt or GameSystems' photoArm ref; capturePhoto's existing stamp (which ledger2's arming reads) is untouched, and a press can satisfy a mission photo step and a postcard simultaneously without either system knowing about the other — the Phase 28 photoArm leak class cannot recur through this path because there is no shared state to leak. REWARDS + PERSISTENCE — completePostcard marks the def done, persists, pays the fee into G.cash + G.lifetimeEarned (PhotoCapture flushes the cash persist immediately), and messages from "The Wire — Picture Desk"; the final sale also files the FULL SET headline via pushNews. The winning Gallery entry is stamped with the postcard's name (GalleryPhoto.challenge, an optional field — pre-Phase-43 entries simply have none) and shown in the list and the full-screen viewer. Progress lives in "harborfall-postcards", per-slot automatically via the Phase 14 prefix layer, validated on load (only known ids survive); NG+ never touches the key, so the set survives the reset like the Slide career record — stated in the module header and the phone copy. THE WALL — the Northgate Loft exists as the player's apartment (pocket 10), so the wall is real geometry, not a certificate-only fallback: eight framed slots on the east wall (PostcardWall in Interiors.tsx), dark while unsold, filling with each print's colour as it sells, with a "THE SABLE POSTCARDS" plaque appearing at the full set; live updates ride a window event (the clothing-changed pattern). The phone ALSO carries the completion certificate (the brief's fallback, kept as a second record — a player who never buys the Loft still gets the certificate and the headline). PHOTO MODE HINT — the photo bar gains one line: the nearest incomplete postcard + its condition + the sold count (nearestPostcardHint: anchored postcards by player distance, the pod by its live position, air support only when nothing anchored remains; the full-set state shows a completion line instead). It rides inside the photo bar because the bar IS Photo Mode's UI — it appears on entry and leaves on exit (H/U/Esc) with everything else; the current build has no separate photo-UI hide toggle, and none was invented. AUDIT SECTION 12 (new): the audit imports game/postcards.ts and pins the set's shape (exactly 8, unique ids, fees inside $75–$150) and every fixed anchor to the real data it names — bridge centre from BRIDGE, club frontage against STATIC_BLOCKERS, observatory against NORTHREACH_BLOCKERS, Breakwater Point against WESTBROOK_VIEWPOINT, the storm anchor in open water per isWaterAt, the sign anchor on land with a standable viewpoint 30u south — plus both anchorless postcards asserted anchorless. One design note the audit forced into the open: postcards.ts is base-bundle, so the Breakwater anchor is a LITERAL mirroring WESTBROOK_VIEWPOINT rather than an import (importing westbrookData would pull the lazy district's data module into the initial bundle and shrink the Westbrook chunk's reason to exist — measured: the first draft did exactly that, index +0.6 kB / Westbrook chunk 2.94 → 2.46); the section-12 pin is what makes the literal safe. VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR including the new section 12; pytest 48/48 (server untouched, re-run to prove it); HEADLESS LOGIC CHECK 21/21 (kept in /tmp, the Phase 30/41 precedent): breaching pod in frame banks, submerged/behind/too-far pods don't; golden span banks at 18:00 facing the bridge and not at noon or facing away; air support at 4★ and not 3★; storm banks over the bay and not inland; sign at 23:00 and not by day; club neon at 22:00; the dome at any hour; breakwater gold at 07:00; the overlap rule (storm + golden span both true → storm banks first, span banks on the next press); done postcards never re-bank. Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈865.3 kB gzip (index 489.6 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 11.0 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 42's ≈862.9 — +0.3%, the postcards module + Gallery section + wall riding the main chunk; streamed chunks untouched and still lazy (Eastmere 3.20, Westbrook 2.94, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 44 note (this round — tally recounted at 87/7/0 over 94 topic lines; ONE NEW ✅ line, §12 Assists & accessibility — new scope counted as a new line, no existing line changed state): ASSISTS & ACCESSIBILITY. PART A — PHASE 43 REGRESSION AUDIT. ZERO defects found; every briefed check verified in code. (1) DOUBLE-BANK — capturePhoto (App.tsx) only bumps photoShotRef; PhotoCapture's useFrame processes a value once (the done-ref guard) and renders once per frame, so two presses inside one frame collapse into a single processing pass — one evaluatePostcardShot call, which returns the FIRST unsold def whose test passes in desk order and nothing else; completePostcard marks it done before any later press can be processed. One press banks at most one postcard, and the documented overlap behaviour (storm + golden span both true → storm banks, span banks on the NEXT press) is exactly what the code does. (2) GALLERY EVICTION vs THE BOOK — galleryAdd slices the list to the 12-cap, so a stamped photo can age out of the phone; the postcard itself cannot: POSTCARDS.done is its own persisted key, completePostcard writes it at the shutter press, and Interiors' PostcardWall renders from POSTCARDS.done — gallery presence is never consulted for sold state. The stamp is a label on a photo; the sale is a fact in the book. (3) POD_MEMBERS COST — the array is preallocated at module load (POD_SIZE = 5 objects) and Wildlife's frame loop mutates the members in place (pm.x/pm.z/pm.y) after the dive lerp; the Phase 43 publication allocates nothing per frame. (The path sampler podPathPoint returns a small object per call — six calls a frame, pre-existing Phase 42 presentation machinery, not the publication.) (4) WALL LISTENER — PostcardWall's useEffect adds the POSTCARDS_EVENT listener and its cleanup removes it; unmount cannot leak it. (5) NG+ KEEPS POSTCARDS — ngplus.ts contains no reference to the postcards key or the POSTCARDS module state; its reset list (story/side/choice/job stand-downs) never touches "harborfall-postcards", which loads at module init through the slot-prefix layer like every other per-slot key — the set, the stamps' backing facts and the wall all survive the reset, as documented since Phase 43. (6) PHASE 43 PART A FIXES, SPOT RE-CHECKED AT THEIR SITES — game/phase8.ts rollCatch tests dolphinsNear(F.castX, F.castZ), the live reach-scaled cast point, the same point the bark reads; game/ngplus.ts imports and calls endSnagRun() in its stand-down (the hand-rolled reset is gone), so the snag serial stamp clears on the NG+ path like every other exit. PART B — FOUR SETTINGS, ONE SECTION (Phone → Settings → "Assists & accessibility"; new fields in G.settings — driveAssist/aimAssist as string unions, colorblindHud/reducedFlash booleans — picked up by old saves through the settings merge, persisted per slot, every default reproducing Phase 43 behaviour exactly). (1) DRIVE ASSIST — the smoothing lives in Vehicle.tsx stepDrive immediately after the input merge (keys → gamepad stick → touch stick → gyro), so it acts on the MERGED value and every source — keyboard, Asphalt-style touch stick, gyro — is assisted identically; that placement was verified, not assumed. Form: an exponential chase (assistSteerRef) with time constant tau = base + slope × speed-fraction — Light 0.05 s → 0.18 s, Full 0.08 s → 0.30 s (speed fraction over 20 u/s for cars, 7.6 for the bicycle) — so the wheel is direct at parking speeds and calm at pace. Scope guards: boats and planes take the raw value (the helm's Phase 28 speed-sensitive steering and the flight model are separate systems); the ref tracks raw whenever the assist is off or out of scope, so toggling mid-drive never jumps; it resets on drive start. INTEGRITY: the physics step, the motorcycle heading bias, the bicycle heading and the body-roll feed all read the smoothed value (that IS the assist), but the drift feed (tickDrift) reads rawSteering for both its lock threshold and its angle estimate — drift scoring is bit-for-bit the pre-assist model — and the handbrake, the wheelie gesture and the bunny hop all read the key set directly, so no assist level can suppress a drift, a wheelie or a hop. Top speeds, grip and every physics number are untouched (the C++/TS step receives a different input value, never different constants). FULL adds camera auto-centre in App.tsx CameraRig: a lastOrbitAt stamp fed by OrbitControls' 'start' event (mouse drag, touch drag, wheel) and by the gamepad right-stick block; when driveAssist is "full", the player is driving, the car is moving (|speed| > 2.5) and the stamp is >2.5 s stale, the camera azimuth eases toward behind-the-car heading at ≤0.55 rad/s via the same Spherical-offset write the pad orbit uses (controls.update() still settles the frame; OrbitControls remains the owner). It never runs in the aim, first-person, photo, interior or menu-orbit paths (they return or gate earlier), and pitch/distance are never written. (2) AIM ASSIST — WHICH FORM, AND WHY: Combat's fire() is hitscan along a direction — when free-aiming, the direction is the camera ray projected flat; each pellet then resolves against a perpendicular corridor (knockPed's perp test). There is no crosshair-position state to slow down (the camera is mouse/stick-driven directly), so the brief's fallback (crosshair slowdown) has nothing to attach to; the honest form is MAGNETISM OF THE SHOT DIRECTION at the resolution site, implemented in fire() after base is computed and before the muzzle/pellet maths, so FX and pellets agree. Rules: only while G.aiming (hip fire follows the character facing — unassisted, stated in the help text); only gun weapons reach the block (bat/bottle/fist return earlier); candidates are active, standing peds within weapon range and within the cone (Soft 0.10 rad ≈ 5.7° / pull 0.45; Strong 0.19 rad ≈ 11° / pull 0.70); the nearest candidate whose player→ped segment is clear of the isBlocked AABB set (sampled every ~1.2u — the same blocker data as the Phase 33 cover test and the camera boom guard) wins; base rotates toward it by the pull FRACTION of the angular offset, once, at the trigger pull. No snap (45–70% of the way, never all), no tracking (one adjustment per shot), no through-wall help (blocked candidates are skipped entirely). (3) COLOURBLIND-SAFE HUD — new game/hudpalette.ts is the single owner: hudCanvasPalette(theme.minimap) feeds App's drawMap (police, objective, job target + GPS line, your-car rings, player arrow, traffic dots, incident cross) and hudDomPalette() feeds the DOM (wanted stars, heat chip in App; Bank business/daily/rent figures, the loan-owed figure, the debrief reward, and the Bank event badges in Phone — rush +50% and star ×2 and snag-fixed and inspection-passed read eventGood; quiet −30% and inspection-failed read eventBad). With the setting off the canvas palette returns the theme's colours plus the map's standing literals verbatim and the DOM palette returns nulls (callers pass undefined styles), so the default HUD is pixel-identical to Phase 43 — verified by construction, since every changed call site passes through the module. With it on, the palette is Okabe–Ito: police #56b4e9 sky blue (the wanted stars take the same blue, tying HUD to map), objective/job #f0e442 yellow, player + your-car #ffffff white, traffic #e69f00 orange, incident #d55e00 vermillion, money gain/eventGood #009e73 bluish green, money loss/eventBad #d55e00 vermillion — always IN ADDITION TO the +/− signs and labels already carrying the meaning. NOT recoloured, deliberately: land/water/roads and the personal themes (the world's art), the tram/bus map lines (transit identity, not a blip pair the brief names), fuel dots, and every 3D-world colour. One honest correction to the brief's premise: standard-mode money figures carried NO gain/loss colour at all (neutral text, sign-carried) — there was no unsafe red/green money pair to swap; CB mode ADDS the safe colours on top of the signs. (4) REDUCED FLASH — the flash sites that actually exist, found by census: (a) LIGHTNING — Weather.tsx sets G.lightning = 1 at the strike; the setting caps the peak to 0.3 at that ONE write site, and every consumer scales with it (bolt material opacity ×0.95, GameSystems' ambient-light bump ×1.7, scene background and fog brightening ×0.35) while the fixed 2.6/s decay makes the softer peak also a shorter flash (~0.12 s vs ~0.38 s above the bolt's visibility floor). (b) MUZZLE FLASH — Combat's FX block: sprite opacity 0.95 → 0.35 and scale 0.85 → 0.5, pooled point light peak 26 → 7, impact-spark opacity 0.9 → 0.4; durations unchanged, no new effects. (c) EXPLOSIONS — there are none: no explosion flash effect exists anywhere in the client (census: the only "blast" is the shotgun's sound), so there was no site to soften and none was invented. Police light bars and dispatch beacons flash too, but they are slow identity strobes, not photosensitivity-class flashes, and recolouring/softening them would change what a police car reads as — left alone, stated here. (5) SUBTITLE SIZE — ALREADY SHIPPED, verified not rebuilt: G.settings.subSize (Phase 5) is offered in Phone → Settings AND Pause → Settings, and styles.css scales both renderers — .subtitle-bar .subtitle-text at 13/15/20 px (S/M/L) for mission dialogue and .bark-sub at 11/13/15 px for barks — so the brief's item (5) was complete before this round; the Phase 44 help text points at the existing Subtitles section rather than duplicating the control. GUARDS RE-CHECKED: key remapping is untouched (the assists read the merged input downstream of bindings; remapped keys steer/aim identically), gyro + touch are inside the same merge the assist smooths, wanted/heat are untouched, race timing untouched, and with all four settings at default the build is behaviour-identical to Phase 43 by construction (assist block skipped-or-tracking, magnetism gated on G.aiming ∧ setting, palettes returning shipped values, flash peaks at shipped constants). VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR (no world changes); pytest 48/48 re-run (server untouched). Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈866.2 kB gzip (index 490.5 + three 178.2 + react-three 185.8 + CSS 11.0 + HTML 0.7) vs Phase 43's ≈865.3 — +0.1%, the palette module + settings UI + assist blocks riding the main chunk; streamed chunks untouched and still lazy (Eastmere 3.20, Westbrook 2.94, Northreach 2.93 kB gzip). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 47 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 87/7/0 over 94 topic lines; the §15 Random world events line grows 8 → 10, no line changed state): TWO MORE WORLD EVENTS. PART A — PHASE 46 REGRESSION AUDIT. ZERO defects found; every briefed check verified in code. (1) USER-PAUSE vs THE SCORE GATE — pauseTunes() (the phone's pause) clears TUNES_STATE.playing and never touches the room hold; the score's tick gate is `!settings.ambientScore || TUNES_STATE.playing || tunesRoomHold() !== null`, so outside a room a user pause opens the gate and the score fades back in — the priority chain's documented intent (a paused track is not a playing track). Inside a room the pause leaves roomHold set, so the score stays gated by the room's own loop — also correct. The implication "a suspended track implies a room hold" was verified at the state level: suspension is set only by the room enter/exit paths (clubHold/jukeboxHold), and stopSource's own guards mirror it (the club track skips stopClubBass while roomHold is "club"; loop tracks skip stopLoop while roomHold is "jukebox"), so no reachable state has the score and a held track sounding together — the ~2 s layer fades are the documented crossfade, not a state bug. (2) TENSION LATCH — `tense` in score.ts is written only at step 0 of the bar in scheduleStep; the pad's progression reads the latched value, so wanted dropping mid-bar keeps the minor side until the bar ends, as documented. The pulse layer's per-step wanted ≥5 read is its documented rate behaviour (8ths at 5★+), not the chord latch. (3) SCORESTART ONE-WAY — the guard is `timer !== null`; the single call site is App.tsx beginPlay beside resumeTunes(); game/ngplus.ts never imports or calls it, so NG+ cannot restart or double-start the scheduler — no interval leak path exists. (4) AMBIENTSCORE MERGE — store.ts loads settings as `{ ...G.settings, ...(saved ?? {}) }` over the defaults object that carries `ambientScore: true`, so a pre-Phase-46 save gains the toggle at On. (5) MUSICBUS SYNC — the score tick calls syncMusicBus(bus, BUS_VOL) on every 90 ms tick, so the N mute lands within ~90 ms, the identical treatment Tunes gets. (6) POSTER/LOW SPOT RE-CHECKS — App's poster import has .catch() at the mount effect with the cached promise (no retry loop), the slideshow and caption guard on an empty panel set, and beginPlay never reads poster state; perf.ts' Low ceilings flow through the single-source trafficDensityShare / pedDensityFactor / trafficReserveAlwaysOut functions (the last being Phase 46's own fix, re-verified at its GameSystems call site). PART B — TWO DAY EVENTS (new game/dayevents.ts + components/DayEvents.tsx; economy.ts advanceDay hook; theft.ts fence bonus flag; ModMenu.tsx voucher; Phone.tsx Records row; ngplus.ts stand-down; App.tsx mount). THE EXISTING DISCIPLINE, READ FIRST: the game has two event scopes. Session events (Street Challenge, Spilled Cargo, the Fishing Derby, the B2 set) run on the shared G.eventNow courtesy from Phase17's scheduler — one live at a time, announced by toast/bark, nothing persisted. Day-scope facts (the Phase 41 business events) roll inside advanceDay's serial guard, persist per slot keyed by day serial, and read inert once the serial moves on. The two new events are DAY events — a regatta day and a market day are properties of the day, not of a 3-minute window — so they join the day scope: rollDayEvents(serial) runs inside the guard immediately after rollBizEvents, ~30% regatta / ~30% market / ~40% quiet, ONE slot (the two can never share a day — the one-event-at-a-time rule at day scope), persisted under harborfall-dayevents (own key, per-slot via the Phase 14 prefix layer), announced at the roll by pushNews (Harbour Wire) + pushPhoneMsg (the Harbourmaster for the regatta; the Coral Shores Traders for the market). Day events deliberately do NOT hold G.eventNow all day — that would silence every short event for a whole day; only a live regatta RUN claims the courtesy (kind "regatta"), exactly the derby's arrangement, so a street challenge can still fire on a market day and never mid-run. THE REGATTA: course data lives in dayevents.ts — gate centre (0, 150) with gate buoys at (±7, 150), then six checkpoint buoys (−38, 132), (−100, 140), (−128, 178), (−88, 208), (−30, 210), (18, 188), home through the gate; checkpoint radius 11u (the Bay Circuit's). WHY NOT J.race: the Cup/boat/plane finish block requires a ghost rival (opps[0]) and carries Cup hooks (cupIdx, the win fanfare, the podium reset) — a solo time trial forced through it would need a fake opponent, which the honesty rules forbid. The regatta follows the Slide Series precedent instead: a sibling module owns the run state (REG), the component steps checkpoints against the player's boat, the best lands in jobStats.bestTimes["regatta"] (the Records board's existing pattern — Phone → Stats → Records gains "⛵ Harbour Regatta" beside the Bay Circuit row), racesRun increments like the boat finish, and the component (not the module) calls postRaceResult on settle — leaderboard.ts reads import.meta.env, which the headless audit bundle cannot evaluate, so audit-imported modules stay free of it (the audit caught exactly this in the first draft). Arming: crossing the gate circle in a boat or skiff with no mission/race/slide/status/event live — no countdown, the crossing IS the start (the derby's gate idiom). Abandon: out of a boat for > 5 s, the races' own rule, with a Harbourmaster phone message. Stand-downs: bust/wasted via the status edge, day turn (the course leaves with the day), NG+ via standDownRegatta() beside standDownDerby() — all unsettled; the DAY record itself carries across NG+ because ECO.daySerial is never rewound, the business-events rule. Purse: beating REGATTA_TARGET_S (60 s) pays REGATTA_PRIZE ($250) once per regatta day (DAY.regattaPrizePaid, persisted) plus a Wire headline; later runs that day still record times. HONEST SCOPE, stated in the module header and the Wire copy: a time trial against the clock — there are no AI racers and none are simulated. THE MARKET: three stalls on the Coral Shores promenade at (41.5, 211.7) food, (80, 211.7) parts, (100, 211.7) curios — event fixtures in the Phase 17 idiom (component-owned meshes in the Phase 14 stall language: posts, counter, striped canopy in each stall's colour), NOT static blockers, so the day they leave, nothing about the world's collision changes. The audit's first draft of their sites FAILED, and the failures were real: the food stall's first site stood on the bay bridge's north ramp landing (groundYAt > 0 — the ramp lands across the promenade at x 53–67) and two crowd spots stood inside the z=206 waterfront house row; the stalls were re-sited along the strip and the crowd moved onto open promenade. The road test itself needed care: coarse isOnRoad flags the whole z≈212 band because Coral's vertical grid line x=72 passes through it, but the DRIVEN lanes physically run z 224..304 (world.ts' own laneClearance segments) — the promenade is pedestrian ground the shop row interrupts — so audit §13 tests distance to the lane segments (≥3.95, the world's own scatter rule) plus groundYAt ≤ 0.05 (no deck/ramp) plus raw-box canopy clearance, rather than the grid band. FOUR CROWD figures (the Phase 2 vignette pattern — buildHumanoid rigs, idle animation, two talkers) stand with the stalls; GameSystems' vignette list is fixed world spots, so event crowds are event-owned figures, the Phase 17 challenger pattern. THE THREE OFFERS: FOOD — the Harbour Wraps vendor's exact lines (wrap $10 → +40 health, cooler $5 → stamina 100, eatingUntil + eat sfx), the street-food instance of the existing food effects. PARTS — a VOUCHER, not a shop: the day's deal (one of PARTS_DEALS — engine tune / brakes / drift tyres / respray — rolled with the day) is spent inside Lina's existing ModMenu: marketPrice(cost) = round(cost × 0.75) is the ONE price function, used by the bench's displayed price AND its charge, so the figure shown is the figure paid to the dollar; buy() takes an optional dealKind and consumes the voucher inside the same synchronous click. Purchases verified instant: ModMenu's buy is one synchronous handler (cash check → deduct → apply → persist), so day end cannot strand a transaction mid-flight — and an unspent voucher simply lapses with the day, which the stall's own copy says. The $40 spike-tyre REPLACEMENT is excluded from the deal lines — it is a repair consumable, not an upgrade line. CURIOS — sellStash(where, bonusFrac = 0) in theft.ts: the default path is byte-identical for Coral Pawn and Lina (same payout, same SableBank message); the market passes 0.2, the payout is round(stashTotal × 1.2), and the toast names the bonus. The stall's act closure re-reads dayEventToday() AT THE MOMENT OF SALE — the +20% exists only while the market is live, never from a stale prompt. DESPAWN: the component mounts the stall/buoy/crowd groups only on their day (visibility from dayEventToday each frame), so at day end they are simply gone; nothing is written into the static world. AUDIT SECTION 13 (new): the regatta rig (every buoy + gate point in open water per isWaterAt, ≥19u from Sable Island's centre, clear of the bridge box, inside the server chart clamp, clear of the moored yacht/tug/rental-skiff spawn, and clear of the cargo hull by a box test re-derived from the ship's own placement constants — centre, rotation, half-extents, 3u margin — plus course legs ≥ 20u so no two checkpoints can trigger together) and the market rig (stall centres + counter corners pointTest-clear, canopy corners raw-box-clear and off the ramp, lane-segment distance ≥ 3.95, ≥4u from all four promenade doors, stalls ≥10u apart, crowd spots clear and outside the stall footprints) and the set's shape pins (6 buoys, target 30–120 s, prize $100–$500, 4 deal lines, discount exactly 0.25, bonus exactly 0.2, marketPrice dollar values 400→300 / 300→225 / 600→450). VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR including the new section 13; pytest 48/48 re-run (server untouched); HEADLESS LOGIC CHECK 321/321 (kept in /tmp, the standing precedent): 300 seeded day rolls land 91 regatta / 94 market / 115 quiet with the serial stamped every time; a same-serial re-roll is a no-op even with Math.random forced; yesterday's record reads inert; regatta finishes record the best, pay the purse once, ignore slower runs and no-run finishes; the voucher is live only on a market day and spends exactly once; marketPrice is dollar-exact; sellStash pays face value by default and +20% with the flag. (The harness's first run "failed" 304 checks by destructuring the DAY export — ES live bindings don't survive destructuring; the game reads the binding, the harness was fixed to read the namespace. Recorded because the failure mode is worth recognising.) Perf sanity: initial-load JS ≈633.7 kB gzip (index 258.01 + three 178.23 + react-three 185.77 + CSS 11.01 + HTML 0.66) vs Phase 46's ≈629.9 — +3.8 kB (+0.6%), the day-events module + component; streamed chunks untouched and still lazy (Eastmere 3.20, Westbrook 2.94, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip; posterData 236.14). No C++ touched.*

*Phase 48 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 87/7/0 over 94 topic lines; the §15 Side storylines line grows with the Crown clause and the FINAL TALLY's Branching parenthetical gains the buy-out/exposé pair, no line changed state): THE HARBOUR CROWN — the endgame empire chain. PART A — PHASE 47 REGRESSION AUDIT. ONE defect fixed, ONE gate hardened, the rest verified clean in code. (1) DEFECT — MODMENU VOUCHER STALENESS: ModMenu's buy() computed its charge from the RENDER closure's `deal` (marketDealActive() read at render). The panel re-renders on a 400 ms interval tick, so the closure can be up to 400 ms stale. Inside that window across the day boundary, a click would charge the OLD day's voucher price — and consumeMarketDeal(), which re-checks the day serial, would then either no-op after a discounted charge (yesterday's deal honoured free) or, on back-to-back market days, consume the NEW day's voucher for the old day's click. Phase 47's own defence ("purchases are instant, so a voucher can never strand mid-transaction") covered a transaction spanning midnight, not a CLICK landing across it. FIX: buy() re-reads marketDealActive() at click time and prices from the live value; the display still refreshes on the 400 ms tick, so shown and charged can differ only inside that sub-tick window at the boundary itself, and the charge is always the honest live one. Stated plainly: the click re-validates. (2) HARDENING — REGATTA ARM GATE: DayEvents' arm gate read `!M.active && !J.race && !J.slide && !G.eventNow` under a comment claiming the Phase 17 courtesy list, whose mission/event start checks also stand off for G.dialogueActive and G.incident (both are in the scheduler's own start list). A regatta run could therefore arm in the same moment a dispatch call-out or a dialogue landed. FIX: both tokens added; the gate now matches the list it cites. (3) DAY-ROLL EXACTLY-ONCE — clean: advanceDay's body runs only under `ECO.lastPaidSerial < ECO.daySerial`; rollDayEvents additionally no-ops unless the serial differs from the record's; both entry paths (the 06:00 wrap via Phase7's detector and sleepToMorning) funnel through the one advanceDay, and sleepToMorning's SLEEP_TICK stamp (Phase 31) still prevents the wrap detector firing a second tick for the same morning — the roll cannot fire twice for a day, and a slept-through morning still rolls exactly once. (4) REGATTA SLOT RELEASE — clean, every exit censused: finish → releaseSlot() clears REG.event + G.eventNow; the bust/wasted status edge → the component's status-edge effect (standDown + release); day turn → standDown + release (the course despawns with its day record); abandon → out-of-boat > 5 s (the races' rule) stands the run down and releases; NG+ → standDownRegatta() beside standDownDerby() in the stand-down list, plus ngplus nulls G.eventNow itself. No path leaves the courtesy claimed, so the Phase 17 scheduler cannot deadlock on an abandoned run. (5) SELLSTASH BONUS — clean: DayEvents' curios act closure re-reads dayEventToday() at the moment of sale and passes the bonus only while the market is live; theft.ts's default path is untouched for the pawn shop and Lina. (6) NG+ WORLD STATE — clean: DayEvents renders buoys/stalls/crowd from dayEventToday() each frame; NG+ stands a live run down and the day RECORD (serial-keyed, ECO.daySerial never rewinds) carries across by design, exactly the business-events rule — no buoy or stall state persists in any component or store field. (7) SPOT RE-CHECKS — clean: score.ts' gate reads TUNES_STATE.playing + tunesRoomHold() live each tick; App's poster import carries .catch() with the cached promise (no retry loop) and a plain backdrop fallback. TWO NOTES, not defects: (a) a regatta run still live at a day turn into a SECOND consecutive regatta day survives the stand-down gracefully (buoys still out on the new day; finishRegattaRun reads the new day's record and prize flag) while a DayEvents comment says a run "goes with" the day it started — a comment/code mismatch, behaviour is safe either way; (b) a mission can be started mid-regatta-run (arming was gated, but mission starts never consult G.eventNow) — the pre-existing Phase 17 courtesy pattern shared by every session event, not a Phase 47 regression, left as designed. PART B — THE HARBOUR CROWN (missions.ts defs; missionState.ts state; new game/crown.ts; GameSystems.tsx channel/cap/fallout; App.tsx gate/choice/escrow; economy.ts dividend + series hook; Phone.tsx rows; CrownWorld.tsx set-piece; ngplus.ts reset; the Sovereign kind census across world.ts/store.ts/CarBody.tsx/Vehicle.tsx/drift.ts/depenetrate.ts). THE GATE: the chain is the empire's exam, so the empire is the entry fee — M.storyDone AND ECO.owned ≥ 2 AND RENTALS.owned ≥ 1, checked in App's Ines branch before any mission logic; when short, Ines delivers a two-line dialogue naming the requirement AND the player's current counts ("the Wire counts X businesses and Y rental properties in your name"), and Phone → Story's Crown row shows the same counts. Nothing starts, nothing charges, no flag moves. District gates follow the Ledger/Quiet pattern exactly: crown1 requires BOTH westbrookLoader and eastmereLoader via ensureDistrictLoaded (sequential, each waits out an in-flight attempt and retries once) with Ines postponing in dialogue on failure, naming the district that failed; crown4 gates northreachLoader; crown2/crown3 need no district. startMission warms crown1's two chunks and crown4's one for Missions-app replays. THE NAME: "Meridian" verified in the lore before use — it exists as a RUN/shipment name (the Meridian Pair, the Meridian run in Ch.4, a sunken Meridian ship in quiet2's dialogue) but never as a company; "Meridian Holdings" is therefore a NEW front company deliberately wearing the harbour's own name, which the fiction itself remarks on ("They named themselves after the run that sank"). CROWN1 "Paper Trail" (photo kind, meetSteps 1, photoSteps 2, runTimer 180): the brief asked for the ledger1 tail + ledger2 photo hybrid across two districts. ENGINE TRUTH, stated: a def's kind is one value — the tail kind drives a moving courier van on the road grid and the photo kind arms evidence rings; they cannot combine in one def, and ledger1's van is a Westbrook road-grid vehicle that cannot drive to Eastmere. The landed shape is ledger2's proven hybrid (meet → photograph → timed run) with the MEET moved to Westbrook's Breakwater Point (the WESTBROOK_VIEWPOINT literal, pinned by audit §14) and the photographs at the bagman's two Eastmere handoffs — shadowing by authored stops, the voss2 pattern — rather than a duplicated tail. The runTimer bark/popup destinations were made def-aware in passing (they hardcoded ledger2's Northgate Loft; crown1 runs to the Market Row newsroom), and the meet popup's evidence count now reads photoTotal instead of a hardcoded "Three spots". CROWN2 "Cold Storage" (escape kind): the infiltration is a GameSystems block mirroring heist2's drill — on foot within 7u of the container point (80, 118), an 8 s G.hold channel ("Copying the manifest…") on its own crownT ref; starting the copy trips the seal alarm to 2★ with the alarm sfx (the heist2 precedent, one star lower — a "quiet" alarm). THE WANTED CAP: the brief said "wanted capped by the mission (use existing mission wanted tools — no new police systems)". The cap is a per-frame clamp inside the crown2 mission block (`if (G.wanted > 3) G.wanted = 3`) for the mission's whole life — the same family as forceWanted's mission-set floor, read in reverse; crimes still accrue evidence and stars up to the ceiling, the escape step still demands wanted 0 at the safehouse, and the fiction carries it (Voss leaning on the port authority). The container point was probe-verified before finalising: the first candidate stood on the Bay Bridge deck (groundYAt 5.5); (80, 118) is quay ground (groundY 0, unblocked) south-east of the container stacks — audit §14 re-checks all three properties. CROWN3 "The Auction" (goto kind, choiceKey "crown"): the set-piece is the mansion-row frontage (-96, -236) with CrownWorld's party (auctioneer at the steps, Meridian's bidder, a harbour bidder — named buildHumanoid rigs on the QuietHarbour pattern, visible while M.side.crown === 2, spots mirrored into audit §14). THE CHOICE MACHINERY: crownChoice/crownDone follow the Ledger pair pattern exactly (own keys harborfall-crownchoice/-crowndone; altActive routes choiceKey "crown"; the expose branch is altFlag "crown-expose" with payoutAlt $15,000 through effectivePayout — never paid twice). THE BUY-OUT'S MONEY is the one genuinely new mechanic and it is wired at DECISION time, as the fiction states: resolveChoice deducts the $40,000 and sets M.crownPaid (third key, harborfall-crownpaid) with a SableBank escrow message; a failed attempt re-offers the same branch and crownPaid prevents a second charge; abandoning the mission after deciding keeps the escrow spent — the commitment IS the bid, stated in the choice label, the intro and the bank message. THE OFFER GATE: the buy-out option is only PRESENTED when G.cash ≥ 40,000 at presentation (startMission's choice interruption): with less in hand, Ines first delivers a dialogue naming the shortfall with the player's actual balance, and the panel carries the expose option alone. The check cannot stale between presentation and click — the player is inside dialogue UI and cannot spend. THE DIVIDEND: businessDailyNet applies `net = Math.round(net × 1.1)` LAST (after levels, crew, the club door and the day's event scaling) when crownBuyoutLive() (choice buyout ∧ crownDone ∧ crownPaid — missionState exports it; economy imports missionState, which imports only store + phone, so there is no cycle; crown.ts is imported BY economy for the tick, and crown.ts imports store/missionState/world/phone/feed, never economy). Bank display and morning tick read the one function — shown = paid holds by construction, and the Bank gains a dividend note row while it is live. THE EXPOSÉ: completion fires edition 1 (game/crown.ts EXPOSE_EDITIONS) + armExposeSeries(); editions 2 and 3 print from crownDayTick(serial), called in advanceDay beside rollDayEvents inside the same serial guard, state persisted under harborfall-crownseries with its last-fired serial — exactly one edition per morning, a slept morning still prints one, and NG+ (which never rewinds the serial) simply lets an armed series finish in the new run's first mornings, the papers already being at the printers. Reputation: +25 clamped in all 14 districtAt names (the 11 base districts + Eastmere/Westbrook/Northreach), the Ledger-publish pattern at full-harbour scale. CROWN4 "Harbour Crown" (checkpoints kind, forceWanted 4): marina office pickup (17, 108 — the marina business point), the boat leg, Voss at Patrol HQ (-64, 100 — after1's pickup point). THE LANDING was the design's open question and was settled by probing the real water data: the observatory sits on the Northreach headland (land x −72..32, z −368..−304), so a checkpoint ON the point would be cliff, not water; the landing is the cliff jetty at (44, −340) — open water 12u east of the land edge, below the dome — reached by the east channel (x ≈ 140 runs unbroken from the bay past Eastmere's east shore to the Northreach coast; audit §14 walks ten samples of that line plus the approach, and asserts the landing sits EAST of the district's x1 so it stays water when the chunk loads). The generic checkpoints reach test (7u from the follow position) banks it from the boat; the completion clears the mission's 4★ on the wire3 precedent. THE SOVEREIGN: kind "sovereign" through the full census — world.ts CarSpawn union + CAR_MODELS (Vantara Sovereign, 268/91: top-tier in the existing stat system, honestly between the Tempest's 232/84 and the hyper's 288/92), store.ts CarState union, CarBody (CarKind union, CABIN_POS, SHELL, and a body case on the Tempest's lines with the TWO-TONE livery: the car's paint — deep teal #14524f at delivery — under a fixed GOLD roof panel, waist spear, deck band and exhausts via a new GOLD material; a respray changes the paint, never the gold), Vehicle BODY_DIMS [1.08, 2.2] + the push club (cap 28.5, push 8.0 — above its Tempest sibling's 26.5/7.2, its cap between the wedge's 27.5 and the hyper's 30, its push below the hyper's 8.5), drift DRIFT_FACTOR 1.3 (the Tempest family value), depenetrate HALF_W 1.08. Deliberately ABSENT from: DEALER_CARS (never sold — audit §14 pins this), CAR_SPAWNS (no world spawn), TRAFFIC_KINDS (never traffic). DELIVERY (game/crown.ts deliverSovereign): called once from crown4's completion fallout; existence checks first (storedCars, the garage-saved snapshot, any owned world car of the kind) — if the garage list has room the car joins G.storedCars + persistStoredCars (the dealer-purchase path: take it out at either garage with G, stored-car persistence untouched); if the list is full (6) it is filed as the garage-saved car at the villa zone via persistSavedCar and the Sable Motors message says exactly that. M.crownCarDelivered (harborfall-crowncar) records it either way. SURVIVAL: stored cars and the garage-save are ordinary save data — reload-safe by the existing machinery; NG+ KEEPS cars (ngplus never touches the garage keys) while resetting crownCarDelivered with the arc, and a second NG+ completion finds the first Sovereign through the existence checks and mints no duplicate — the same treatment the Slide/Cup career records get (kept across NG+, re-earnable in name only). PHONE: the Story app gains the Crown row (locked text before storyDone; the empire counts while short; "Call Ines Marlo · n/4" when open; "Done 👑" at 4) + a branch-record row once crownDone (buy-out dividend vs exposé, the Ledger-row pattern); Stats gains the 👑 Harbour Crown title row at side.crown ≥ 4; the NG+ confirm note names the Crown among the reset arcs and states the Sovereign stays. NGPLUS: M.side gains crown: 0; crownChoice/crownDone/crownPaid/crownCarDelivered reset in the reset block; the header's RESETS list names the Crown. AUDIT SECTION 14 (new): the four defs exist with side "crown" and their shape pins (crown1 meet/photo/runTimer, crown2 escape, crown3 choiceKey/altFlag/payouts, crown4 forceWanted 4); crown1's meet pinned to WESTBROOK_VIEWPOINT + inside the Westbrook bounds + clear of WESTBROOK_BLOCKERS at r 1.0; its photo points inside the Eastmere bounds + clear of the full mirrored Eastmere blocker list at r 0.4; the newsroom point point-tested; crown2's container point standable + on land + groundY ≤ 0.05 (not the bridge deck) and the safehouse door point-tested; crown3's frontage + the three CrownWorld figure spots point-tested + clear of the NORTH_MANSIONS boxes; crown4's marina + HQ points point-tested, the landing in open water and east of the Northreach land bounds, and the ten-point sailing line all water; the Sovereign census (CAR_MODELS entry + name + the honest topSpeed band 232 < 268 < 288, DRIFT_FACTOR 1.3, and NOT in DEALER_CARS). VERIFICATION: build PASS; placement audit ALL PLACEMENTS CLEAR including the new section 14; pytest 48/48 re-run (server untouched — the venv had been wiped by a VM restart and was recreated with the standing recipe). Perf: initial-load JS ≈641.1 kB gzip (index 265.43 + three 178.23 + react-three 185.77 + CSS 11.01 + HTML 0.68) vs Phase 47's ≈633.7 — +7.4 kB (+1.2%), essentially all of it the four missions' authored dialogue prose in the index chunk (every side arc ships its defs there; no mission-prose split exists in the codebase's patterns) plus the Crown machinery; streamed chunks untouched and still lazy (Eastmere 3.20, Westbrook 2.94, Northreach 2.94 kB gzip; posterData 236.14). No C++ touched. Playability is, as ever, the user's own smoke test — the morning list covers the gate, both auction branches on separate slots, the dividend's Bank figure against the payout, and the Sovereign after reload and after NG+.*

*Phase 46 note (this round — tally recounted, unchanged at 87/7/0 over 94 topic lines; the §15 Sable Tunes line grows, no line changed state): DYNAMIC MUSIC. PART A — PHASE 45 REGRESSION AUDIT. ONE defect found and fixed; the rest clean. DEFECT — the rush-hour reserve bypassed the single-source density functions. GameSystems' traffic step decides a rushOnly car's off-peak parking with `G.settings.trafficDensity === "high"` read directly — a second consumer of the lever that Phase 45's Low ceiling (in perf.ts trafficDensityShare) never reached. The ceiling's COUNT was never wrong: the density gate runs earlier in the same per-car step and dormants every beyond-share car before the reserve block can touch it (order verified in the loop), so Low never activated more traffic than the preset allows. What leaked was behaviour inside the share: on Low with the lever on High, reserve cars circulated all day instead of parking off-peak, contradicting the preset's documented "ceiling whatever the levers say". Fix: perf.ts gains trafficReserveAlwaysOut() (lever High ∧ quality ≠ Low — Medium/High evaluate exactly as the old expression did) and GameSystems calls it. CLEAN, verified in code: (1) POSTER LAZY-LOAD — App's mount effect attaches .catch() to loadPosterPanels(), so a failed chunk import leaves the backdrop plain and the screen fully usable; the cached promise means the failure is returned, never retried in a loop (the effect runs once); the slideshow effect and the caption both guard on posterPanels.length === 0; startLoad/beginPlay never read poster state, so Play works before, during and after the chunk lands or fails. (2) SHADOWS ON TIER CHANGE — GameSystems rewrites sun.castShadow from settings every frame, so a mid-session tier change converges on the very next frame (the write runs in useFrame, ahead of that frame's render), and the light additionally remounts on its quality-bearing key; there is no stale-write path that could flicker. (The shadowMap.needsUpdate nudge at the same site still fires on Low when the player's toggle is on — with castShadow false no light casts, so the pass renders nothing; harmless, left alone and stated.) (3) WILDLIFE HALF RATE — the porpoise phase is `t × 2π/2.8` off the clock, a pure function of time, so sampling it at 30 Hz cannot double-step it; pod distance, splash lifetimes and the gull state machines all integrate the SUMMED dt the gate hands them; POD_MEMBERS staleness is bounded by one skipped frame, as Phase 45 stated. (4) AUTO-CENTRE EXCLUSIONS — the Phase 45 roadVehicle check (kind ∉ {boat, skiff, plane}) is in place; Tram.tsx only READS G.driving as a boarding gate and BusRide likewise never sets it, so tram/bus rides cannot reach the auto-centre block, whose other gates (aim/FP/photo return earlier; orbit and interior gate it off) are unchanged. PART B — THE AMBIENT SCORE (new game/score.ts, ~200 lines). One lookahead scheduler (90 ms interval, 0.16 s horizon — the theme/Tunes idiom) on an 84 BPM 16th grid, 64-step loop, one chord a bar.
